I read the whole starter repo (`handbook/`, `STRUCTURE.md`, `05-Codebase-Guide.md`, all the stubs) and your filled A1 form. Here's the picture.

## What this project actually is

One repo, one system, four milestones, graded by **git tags** on a public GitHub repo — not ZIPs (the ZIP language in the A1 form is superseded by [SUBMISSION.md](SUBMISSION.md)). The system is fixed for everyone: scanned page image → clean → layout → OCR → chunk/embed/index → retrieve → agent answers with citations, abstaining when evidence is weak.

The repo is a **regulated skeleton**. Per [STRUCTURE.md](STRUCTURE.md): you may edit `configs/*.yaml`, function bodies marked `# IMPLEMENT` (45 of them), and `grading_kit/tasks.jsonl`. You may **not** move files, rename modules, change signatures, change [contracts.py](src/doc_agent/contracts.py), change the stage order in [pipeline.py](src/doc_agent/pipeline.py), or change the tool names in [agent/tools.py](src/doc_agent/agent/tools.py). CI (`tests/test_structure.py`) fails the build if it drifts.

Milestones: **A1** frame + corpus · **A2** knowledge base (ingest→index) · **A3** retrieval + agent + eval · **A4** serve + govern.

## Where you are right now

Your A1 *form* is written and it's strong. Your A1 *repo artifacts are all still empty stubs*:

|---|---|

| [scripts/get_data.sh](scripts/get_data.sh) | `echo "TODO"` |

**This matters a lot:** the A2 form says those A1 artifacts "are graded here for the first time," and both forms have a **grounding gate** — *"every claim in the form must match your own code, index, and notebook outputs. Numbers with no notebook evidence score zero."*

Your A1 form already asserts things that don't exist yet: "Both checks are cells in eda.ipynb and both pass" (pHash + deed-group leakage), "609 usable pages", "184/609 below threshold", "worst page dolil_412". Those are 20 of the Section 2 points and 15 of Section 3. **The eda.ipynb you write must actually produce those exact numbers**, or you rewrite the numbers to match what it produces.

## Four contradictions between your form and the fixed repo — resolve these before A2

1. **`enhance_page` marked "N/A / not built."** Tool names and signatures are locked and `tests/test_tools.py` checks conformance. You must implement the class — but it can legitimately return `ToolResult(ok=False, ...)` or re-serve the greyscale variant. Don't leave it unimplemented.
2. **Section 7.1 says "no separate OCR model, the VLM reads natively"; Section 6 says Qwen2.5-VL + GraDeT-HTR.** Pick one story. Either way, the reading code goes in [vision/ocr.py](src/doc_agent/vision/ocr.py) `Reader.transcribe_region()` — a VLM call there is fine, but a `Region` must come out of [vision/layout.py](src/doc_agent/vision/layout.py) `detect()` first, because `pipeline.py` order is fixed.
3. **PII: you chose not to redact.** `governance/pii.py` is mandatory and `tests/test_crosscutting.py::test_pii_never_leaks_to_answer_or_log` is a CI gate. The defensible version of your position: implement `detect()` fully, make `redact()` config-gated (off for authenticated desk officers, on in logs/traces). Redact in logs regardless — that satisfies the test and your access-control argument.
4. **config.yaml doesn't match your declared stack.** Set `ocr.model`, `embed.model` (BGE-M3 → `dim: 1024`), `index.type: faiss:flat_ip`, `layout.model`, and the `retrieve.k_step/k_max/weak_threshold` knobs now. Code reads *only* from here.

Also: Section 6 leaves **Stage 7 (RL/RLVR) blank**. Mark it ➕ bonus / not attempted rather than empty — Section 6 is 5 points for "tiers correct."

## Your gated work, derived from your three choices

Per the Codebase Guide Part 2, your profile triggers exactly these extras (everything else you skip):

- **A2 · E23** Bangla–English code-switching → `vision/ocr.py` (language-ID) + `index/embed.py` (multilingual embed — BGE-M3 covers this)
- **A2 · E24** handwritten/HTR → `vision/ocr.py` (also a bonus-credit track)
- **A2 · E1** degraded scans → `ingest/preprocess.py` (your deskew/denoise/Sauvola pipeline is exactly this); `ingest/enhance.py` VAE/diffusion is ➕bonus and you've explicitly declined it — that's a defensible, well-argued trade-off
- **A3 · E6** reranking (precision-first NFR) → `retrieval/rerank.py`
- **A3 · verify-and-correct** (precision-first) → `agent/agent.py` + `llm/postprocess.py`
- **Mandatory for everyone, A3, fail-closed:** evidence-gated re-search in `Agent.decide()` — if `top_score < weak_threshold`, widen `k` by `k_step` and retrieve again; abstain at `k_max`. Emit `traces/run.jsonl`. Author at least one `{needs_research:true}` / `{needs_research:false}` task pair. A fixed retrieve→answer path caps your grade.

## What to do, in order

**Now (A1 catch-up — graded at A2):**
1. Fill [configs/task.yaml](configs/task.yaml) with your real team id and three axes; mirror them into [grading_kit/manifest.yaml](grading_kit/manifest.yaml).
2. Write [scripts/get_data.sh](scripts/get_data.sh) to pull the Mendeley dataset into `data/raw/` (it's gitignored — the script is how graders reproduce your corpus).
3. Write [notebooks/eda.ipynb](notebooks/eda.ipynb) so it runs top-to-bottom and *emits* the numbers your form claims: 653→609 counts, word estimate, size, scan-quality histogram + the 184-page faded band, deed grouping, and the two leakage checks (deed-group assertion + pHash).
4. Fill [data/provenance.md](data/provenance.md) from the notebook's actual output.
5. Set aside ~5–10 pages into `grading_kit/heldout_pages/` and **hand-transcribe** them into `labels.jsonl`. This is the slowest item — Bangla handwriting, character-exact. Start it early; it's the oracle your EM ≥ 95% is scored against.
6. Fill `data/validate.py::validate()` and `data/versioning.py::snapshot()`, plus `logging_conf.py::get_logger()`.
7. Each member drops `transcripts/<student number>.txt` (header → full unedited chat → reflection). Yours is `2105078.txt`.
8. Push to a **public** repo, add teammates.

**Then A2** (the actual next deliverable): implement `ingest/loader.py`, `ingest/preprocess.py`, `vision/layout.py`, `vision/ocr.py`, `index/{chunk,embed,store}.py`; write `scripts/build_index.sh`, `notebooks/kb_demo.ipynb` (OCR quality number + one real retrieval), `reports/pipeline_diagram.md`, `configs/design_choices.md` rows for Stages 1–4, the A2 form; then `git tag a2-submit && git push --tags`.

**Your personal slice:** you led A1 §1, §7, §8 — the framing and the agent-on-paper. That maps to the A3 milestone (`agent/`, `retrieval/`, guardrails, HITL, `tasks.jsonl`). In A2 your natural ownership is Section 1's recap and Section 6's plan-for-A3, plus keeping `configs/task.yaml` and `config.yaml` honest, since those encode the three choices you wrote.

One caveat worth raising with your team: **EM ≥ 95% on handwritten Bangla is very aggressive**, and the A2 form forbids changing the success metric afterward. Your form already hedges it as "aspirational," and the abstention trade-off gives you an honest out — but if you want to soften the number, A2 Section 1 is your last chance, and even that is a "do NOT change" cell. Decide deliberately rather than by default.

**24 tasks in three phases**, sized so each ends with something you can run and commit:

- **Phase 0 (Task 0)** — install `uv`, add the CPU deps, generate both lockfiles, get `tests/test_structure.py` green. Nothing is verifiable until this is done; right now `pytest` dies at `import pydantic`.
- **Phase 1 (Tasks 1–10)** — the A1 artifacts that are graded for the first time at A2: `task.yaml`/`config.yaml`/`manifest.yaml`, a working `get_data.sh` against the Mendeley API, `versioning.snapshot()` and `validate()` with tests, `loader.load_pages()`, and `eda.ipynb` built in three passes (inventory → scan quality → deed grouping + both leakage checks), then held-out labels, provenance, transcripts.
- **Phase 2 (Tasks 11–23)** — the knowledge base: preprocessing, the declared-off enhancer, the Colab reading pass, layout+OCR replaying the cache, chunking, BGE-M3 → FAISS, retrieval, `ocr_f1`, `kb_demo.ipynb`, the diagram and design table, and the `a2-submit` tag.

Four things the plan decides that are worth knowing before you start:

1. **The GPU/CPU split is architectural, not a shortcut.** `notebooks/colab_ocr.ipynb` runs DocLayout-YOLO + Qwen2.5-VL once and writes `data/transcriptions.jsonl`; `vision/layout.py` and `vision/ocr.py` replay it. That's what lets CI and a grader reproduce your pipeline with no GPU — and it sidesteps the fact that `pyproject.toml` pins `transformers<4.45`, which cannot load Qwen2.5-VL at all.
2. **PII (Task 13) blocks everything.** `build_knowledge_base()` calls `wiring.register_all()`, which registers `pii._scrub` at the `AFTER_OCR` seam. While that raises `NotImplementedError`, the index cannot build. The plan implements detection with redaction gated by config — off for answers, on for logs — which keeps your A1 position and passes the cross-cutting test.
3. **Task 9 (hand-transcribing 8 held-out pages) is off the critical path — start it today**, in parallel with Task 0. Every OCR number in A2 and every EM score in A3 is measured against it, and its digit normalisation must match `ocr.normalize()` exactly or the scores are silently wrong.
4. **Task 14 Step 6 is where the ≥60,000-word floor is actually proven**, not before. The A1 estimate is ink-blob-based; the plan backfills the true count into `splits.json`, `manifest.yaml`, and `provenance.md`.

The plan says repeatedly: where a computed number disagrees with what the A1 form claims (609 pages, 184 faded, `dolil_412`), **report the computed number and note the delta**. Tuning thresholds to manufacture the claimed figures is exactly what the grounding gate is looking for.

Two execution options:

1. **Subagent-Driven (recommended)** — I dispatch a fresh subagent per task and review between tasks. Fast iteration, but note Tasks 2, 9, and 14 need you (network download, manual transcription, Colab), so those stay yours regardless.
2. **Inline Execution** — we work through tasks in this session with checkpoints for review.

Which approach?

## What this project actually is

One repo, one system, four milestones, graded by **git tags** on a public GitHub repo — not ZIPs (the ZIP language in the A1 form is superseded by [SUBMISSION.md](SUBMISSION.md)). The system is fixed for everyone: scanned page image → clean → layout → OCR → chunk/embed/index → retrieve → agent answers with citations, abstaining when evidence is weak.

The repo is a **regulated skeleton**. Per [STRUCTURE.md](STRUCTURE.md): you may edit `configs/*.yaml`, function bodies marked `# IMPLEMENT` (45 of them), and `grading_kit/tasks.jsonl`. You may **not** move files, rename modules, change signatures, change [contracts.py](src/doc_agent/contracts.py), change the stage order in [pipeline.py](src/doc_agent/pipeline.py), or change the tool names in [agent/tools.py](src/doc_agent/agent/tools.py). CI (`tests/test_structure.py`) fails the build if it drifts.

Milestones: **A1** frame + corpus · **A2** knowledge base (ingest→index) · **A3** retrieval + agent + eval · **A4** serve + govern.

## Four contradictions between your form and the fixed repo — resolve these before A2

1. **`enhance_page` marked "N/A / not built."** Tool names and signatures are locked and `tests/test_tools.py` checks conformance. You must implement the class — but it can legitimately return `ToolResult(ok=False, ...)` or re-serve the greyscale variant. Don't leave it unimplemented.
2. **Section 7.1 says "no separate OCR model, the VLM reads natively"; Section 6 says Qwen2.5-VL + GraDeT-HTR.** Pick one story. Either way, the reading code goes in [vision/ocr.py](src/doc_agent/vision/ocr.py) `Reader.transcribe_region()` — a VLM call there is fine, but a `Region` must come out of [vision/layout.py](src/doc_agent/vision/layout.py) `detect()` first, because `pipeline.py` order is fixed.
3. **PII: you chose not to redact.** `governance/pii.py` is mandatory and `tests/test_crosscutting.py::test_pii_never_leaks_to_answer_or_log` is a CI gate. The defensible version of your position: implement `detect()` fully, make `redact()` config-gated (off for authenticated desk officers, on in logs/traces). Redact in logs regardless — that satisfies the test and your access-control argument.
4. **config.yaml doesn't match your declared stack.** Set `ocr.model`, `embed.model` (BGE-M3 → `dim: 1024`), `index.type: faiss:flat_ip`, `layout.model`, and the `retrieve.k_step/k_max/weak_threshold` knobs now. Code reads *only* from here.

Also: Section 6 leaves **Stage 7 (RL/RLVR) blank**. Mark it ➕ bonus / not attempted rather than empty — Section 6 is 5 points for "tiers correct."

## Your gated work, derived from your three choices

Per the Codebase Guide Part 2, your profile triggers exactly these extras (everything else you skip):

- **A2 · E23** Bangla–English code-switching → `vision/ocr.py` (language-ID) + `index/embed.py` (multilingual embed — BGE-M3 covers this)
- **A2 · E24** handwritten/HTR → `vision/ocr.py` (also a bonus-credit track)
- **A2 · E1** degraded scans → `ingest/preprocess.py` (your deskew/denoise/Sauvola pipeline is exactly this); `ingest/enhance.py` VAE/diffusion is ➕bonus and you've explicitly declined it — that's a defensible, well-argued trade-off
- **A3 · E6** reranking (precision-first NFR) → `retrieval/rerank.py`
- **A3 · verify-and-correct** (precision-first) → `agent/agent.py` + `llm/postprocess.py`
- **Mandatory for everyone, A3, fail-closed:** evidence-gated re-search in `Agent.decide()` — if `top_score < weak_threshold`, widen `k` by `k_step` and retrieve again; abstain at `k_max`. Emit `traces/run.jsonl`. Author at least one `{needs_research:true}` / `{needs_research:false}` task pair. A fixed retrieve→answer path caps your grade.

## What to do, in order

**Now (A1 catch-up — graded at A2):**
1. Fill [configs/task.yaml](configs/task.yaml) with your real team id and three axes; mirror them into [grading_kit/manifest.yaml](grading_kit/manifest.yaml).

from __future__ import annotations
from ..contracts import *  # noqa

from abc import ABC, abstractmethod

class Tool(ABC):
    name: str
    @abstractmethod
    def __call__(self, **kwargs) -> ToolResult: ...

**24 tasks in three phases**, sized so each ends with something you can run and commit:

- **Phase 0 (Task 0)** — install `uv`, add the CPU deps, generate both lockfiles, get `tests/test_structure.py` green. Nothing is verifiable until this is done; right now `pytest` dies at `import pydantic`.
- **Phase 1 (Tasks 1–10)** — the A1 artifacts that are graded for the first time at A2: `task.yaml`/`config.yaml`/`manifest.yaml`, a working `get_data.sh` against the Mendeley API, `versioning.snapshot()` and `validate()` with tests, `loader.load_pages()`, and `eda.ipynb` built in three passes (inventory → scan quality → deed grouping + both leakage checks), then held-out labels, provenance, transcripts.

Four things the plan decides that are worth knowing before you start:

1. **The GPU/CPU split is architectural, not a shortcut.** `notebooks/colab_ocr.ipynb` runs DocLayout-YOLO + Qwen2.5-VL once and writes `data/transcriptions.jsonl`; `vision/layout.py` and `vision/ocr.py` replay it. That's what lets CI and a grader reproduce your pipeline with no GPU — and it sidesteps the fact that `pyproject.toml` pins `transformers<4.45`, which cannot load Qwen2.5-VL at all.
2. **PII (Task 13) blocks everything.** `build_knowledge_base()` calls `wiring.register_all()`, which registers `pii._scrub` at the `AFTER_OCR` seam. While that raises `NotImplementedError`, the index cannot build. The plan implements detection with redaction gated by config — off for answers, on for logs — which keeps your A1 position and passes the cross-cutting test.
3. **Task 9 (hand-transcribing 8 held-out pages) is off the critical path — start it today**, in parallel with Task 0. Every OCR number in A2 and every EM score in A3 is measured against it, and its digit normalisation must match `ocr.normalize()` exactly or the scores are silently wrong.
4. **Task 14 Step 6 is where the ≥60,000-word floor is actually proven**, not before. The A1 estimate is ink-blob-based; the plan backfills the true count into `splits.json`, `manifest.yaml`, and `provenance.md`.

The plan says repeatedly: where a computed number disagrees with what the A1 form claims (609 pages, 184 faded, `dolil_412`), **report the computed number and note the delta**. Tuning thresholds to manufacture the claimed figures is exactly what the grounding gate is looking for.

CLAUDE:
Confirmed — it's genuinely not on this machine. The only Dolil images here are the 8 in [grading_kit/heldout_pages/](grading_kit/heldout_pages/), which are committed to git. The `eda.ipynb` outputs were produced somewhere else (a teammate's machine, or a copy since deleted).

Here's what's going on: `data/raw/` is **gitignored** by design — 2.2GB of scans never goes in the repo. So every machine that runs the pipeline has to fetch the corpus itself. That's what [scripts/get_data.sh](scripts/get_data.sh) is for, and the `SRC_DIR=...` line I gave you was the *second* half of the process — it only copies from a folder you've already downloaded. You haven't downloaded it, so that command has nothing to copy.

The full sequence:

1. Open https://data.mendeley.com/datasets/yk3c3xy9vm/1 in a browser
2. Click **Download All** → gives you a ~2.2GB zip
3. Unzip it, e.g. to `~/Downloads/dolil/`
4. Then run the copy step, pointing `SRC_DIR` at whatever folder actually holds the `.jpg` files (check with `ls` first — Mendeley zips usually nest one folder deep, hence the `"Dolil Dataset"` in the script's own example)

Step 1 exists because Mendeley serves downloads through a JavaScript button with no stable file URL, so it can't be scripted as a plain download.

CLAUDE:
No direct link — the API returns nothing usable, so the browser download is the only route. Manual it is.

**Two options, and the second is probably faster:**

- **Download it yourself** — the 4 steps above, ~2.2GB from Mendeley.
- **Get a copy from whoever ran `eda.ipynb`.** Those outputs (653 files, legibility histogram, pHash check) were produced against a real corpus, so a teammate already has the unzipped folder. A USB stick or a shared-drive copy beats re-downloading 2.2GB. Then you still run the `SRC_DIR=...` copy step to land it in `data/raw/`.

Either way you end up at the same place: `data/raw/` holding 653 `.jpg` files, at which point `scripts/get_data.sh` with no `SRC_DIR` will just report "already has 653/653 — nothing to do" and Step 1 can proceed.

One thing worth knowing while you sort this out: **Adri's lane (Steps 5–7) needs no corpus at all** — chunking, embedding, and the FAISS store all run against a fake `pages.jsonl` of typed Bangla text. That work can start today regardless. Aishi's deed-grouping work also doesn't strictly need the images, only the filename list, which you can reconstruct from [data/splits.json](data/splits.json) — though verifying the groups against actual page content does need the scans.

**First, two corrections to your plan:**

`ingest/enhance.py` is **already done**. [enhance.py:16-19](src/doc_agent/ingest/enhance.py#L16-L19) already returns `pages` unchanged when `cfg["enhance"]["enabled"]` is false. The template shipped exactly the identity behavior Step 2 asks for — cross that item off, write nothing.

And a problem worth fixing before a grader finds it: your held-out pages are `dolil_11`, `dolil_13`, `dolil_20`, `dolil_66`, `dolil_208`, `dolil_212`, `dolil_348`, `dolil_605` — but row 1 of [configs/design_choices.md](configs/design_choices.md) says preprocessing was verified on "representative pages (`dolil_13`, `dolil_20`, `dolil_11`)". Those are three of your eight held-outs. [grading_kit/heldout_pages/README.md](grading_kit/heldout_pages/README.md) says never train or tune on them. Using them as EDA illustrations isn't fatal, but tuning Sauvola parameters against them would be, and the doc currently reads like you did. Either swap those examples for non-heldout pages, or swap those three out of the heldout set.

**What you can build right now:**

1. **`make setup`.** Zero dependency on data, and blocks everything downstream. There's no `.venv` and no `python` on PATH — the moment you have images you'd hit this anyway.

2. **`ingest/preprocess.py` in full, against the 8 heldout scans.** These are real Dolil pages, in the repo, on disk right now. Every item in design_choices row 1 — greyscale, ±4° skew search, median + NLM denoise, Sauvola (15, 0.2, 128), blur-variance quality proxy — is developable and visually verifiable on 8 images. Caveat from the point above: use them as *correctness* smoke tests only. Don't sweep parameters to optimize legibility on them; keep the row-1 values as committed and confirm the code implements them faithfully.

3. **`ingest/loader.py`, with grouping behind a seam.** Write `load_pages()` now and have `doc_id` come from a small `_load_deed_groups()` helper that reads `deed_groups.csv` and raises a clear error when the file is absent. Then the CSV becomes a data drop, not a code change, and `validate()` + `snapshot()` wiring gets done today.

4. **Adri's whole lane (Steps 5–7)** — chunker, BGE-M3 embedder, FAISS flat store — runs against a typed `pages.jsonl`. Fully unblocked. So is Step 8, `build_index.sh`.

5. **The Step 0 decisions and design_choices rows 2–3.** The layout call (keep `projection-heuristic`) and the OCR call (single VLM, plus where it runs given no GPU) are documentation, decidable today, and they gate Sinthia's Steps 3–4.

6. **Housekeeping** — the `transcripts/parts/2105022_001.txt` → `transcripts/2105022.txt` rename takes a minute.

The only genuinely corpus-blocked items are the manifest word count and the full-corpus OCR run.

**A design constraint you should know about:** `Page` is fixed at `{id, image_path, doc_id}` with nowhere to hang variants or flags. So preprocessing writes derived images to `data/interim/preprocessed/` and a per-page sidecar `preprocess.jsonl` (skew, quality, legibility, all three image paths), and returns pages pointing at the deskewed **greyscale** — not the binarization, since Sauvola can erase faint seals and matras.

**Three findings from the smoke run on the 8 real held-out scans** (correctness check only — I tuned nothing against them):

1. `dolil_66` estimated **exactly −4.00°**, the search boundary — its real skew probably exceeds your committed ±4° window. Verify on train pages before widening.
2. The quality proxy **clips 5 of 8 pages to exactly 1.000**. It works as a flag (2 flagged) but carries no information above the threshold.
3. **~5.7s/page**, so roughly an hour single-threaded for 609 pages. The skew search dominates (33 warps/page); downscaling before the search would cut that a lot if it matters.

**Still blocked on the corpus:** producing `deed_groups.csv` (D4 — the loader hard-fails without it, deliberately), regenerating `splits.json` by deed, and the row-1 held-out contamination fix.

CLAUDE:
That changes the lane. The Stage 1 work I just committed is Aishi's — it's needed and correct, but I aimed it at the wrong person's column; sorry for the detour.

Your lane is Steps 3–4: [layout.py](src/doc_agent/vision/layout.py) `detect()` and [ocr.py](src/doc_agent/vision/ocr.py) `Reader`/`transcribe()`, plus the full-corpus OCR run, the OCR metric in `kb_demo`, and form sections 3, 4, 6.

**A contract gap you'll hit in Step 4, worth knowing before you write it:** `transcribe(regions, cfg)` must return `Chunk` objects, and `Chunk.doc_id` is required — but `Region` carries only `page_id`, `bbox`, `kind`. There's no `doc_id` anywhere in the input. So `transcribe()` needs a page→deed lookup from somewhere. Convenient fix: the preprocess sidecar I just committed (`data/interim/preprocessed/preprocess.jsonl`) has `page_id`, `doc_id`, and all three image paths per page, so reading that gives you the mapping plus which image variant to feed the reader.

**What's buildable today on your lane:** `layout.py` in full. D1 settled it as a training-free projection heuristic, so there's no checkpoint to download and no GPU needed, and it's TDD-able against synthetic page fixtures exactly like preprocess was. It's also the direct input to your `Reader`, so it's the right first move. Your 20-JPEG scratch folder still needs the corpus, but the code doesn't.

`ocr.py` is partly buildable too — the region→`Chunk` assembly, the sidecar lookup, batching and ordering can all be tested with a fake reader injected, leaving only the actual Qwen2.5-VL call blocked on the GPU decision in D3.

Your lane's critical path, in order:

**What you have:** [layout.py](src/doc_agent/vision/layout.py) implements D1's projection heuristic — own Otsu binarization (works on a raw scan or on preprocessed greyscale, so you don't block on Aishi), ink-row banding with gap-based paragraph merging, size floors that reject speckle, bboxes in original-image coordinates, `kind="text"` only. Runs in 0.6s for 8 pages, so the full corpus is seconds, not minutes.

**The honest result of the smoke run, which is the important part:**

| | flatbed scans (4) | camera photos (4) |
|---|---|---|
| regions/page | 3–9 | **1, page-sized** |

I chased this properly rather than declaring victory: first hypothesis (dark surround) was half-right — the surround does confuse Otsu, and I added a page crop for it, but on the real photos the sheet fills the frame, so the crop changes nothing. Second hypothesis (tilt) — refuted; the best rotation in ±15° produces zero blank rows. Third (uneven lighting) — adaptive thresholding cuts false ink by 3× but still leaves ~0–5% blank rows. **Your corpus simply doesn't have blank horizontal bands between paragraphs.** The writing runs continuously down the sheet.

**So there's a decision only you can make, and it shapes Step 4:**

- **Treat region = page.** Accept one region per page, feed whole pages to Qwen2.5-VL. Consistent with D2, nothing more to build, and a VLM reads full pages fine. Layout becomes a thin, honest stage — which is a defensible row-2 entry.
- **Segment to text *lines* instead of paragraphs.** Meaningful only if you go back toward an HTR-style reader, which D2 currently rules out.

I'd take the first. If you agree, the `_page_bbox` crop I added is unvalidated weight — it fires on 2 of 8 pages and changes no output — and I'd strip it before committing.

Two disclosures: the tilt test I added passed on first write, so it proves nothing and is kept only as documentation, and all of the above was measured on the held-out pages as a correctness check with nothing tuned against them — the same hazard I flagged for row 1 applies, so re-verify on train pages once the corpus lands.

Want me to strip the crop and commit layout as-is, or hold until you've decided region=page?

What this assignment is: Everyone builds the same kind of system — an agent that answers questions from scanned book pages: it takes a page image, reads it, finds the relevant text, and gives an answer grounded in that text, with a human in the loop for risky steps. What makes your project unique is your three choices (Section 1). In this milestone you pin down those choices, source and clean your scanned corpus, and design your agent on paper.
What to hand in: One ZIP with the filled form, your configs/task.yaml, your eda.ipynb (runs top-to-bottom), your corpus or a script that recreates it, a short provenance/licence note, and one AI-chat transcript per team member. Exact names are on the Submission page.

Section 1 — Our Problem & Our Three Choices
The task is fixed for everyone: “Given a scanned page image from our corpus, answer a question grounded in the page's text.” You do not choose the problem type. You make it your project through the three choices below — these drive every later design decision and make your work different from every other group. (Two qualities — being faithful and accurate — are required of EVERYONE, so they are not choices; see your NFR below.)
① Domain
your practical, real-user subject and the person who'd use the tool (health worker, farmer, legal-aid worker, …). See Domains-And-Natural-Fit. No two groups share one.
Land Registry Archives & Legal Aid
② Data speciality
the ONE hard property of the corpus you're handed — e.g. Bangla · Arabic · code-switching · degraded scans · multi-column · handwritten · old orthography · math notation · dirty-OCR (see Data-Speciality Catalog). English is not a speciality; an English corpus takes a data condition.
Bangla-English code-switching & handwriting
③ Primary NFR + target
the ONE quality you optimise ABOVE baseline, with a concrete target (see the NFR sheet) — e.g. low-latency ≤300ms · memory ≤2GB · calibrated ECE ≤0.05 · private (PII recall ≥0.98) · explainable · auditable · secure · robust · accessible. Faithful & accurate are baseline, NOT choices.
Agentic behaviour (mandatory for everyone, no choice): your agent must do evidence-gated re-search — if the top retrieval score is weak, widen k and retrieve again; abstain once k hits k_max. You build and prove it in A3; nothing to declare here.
Precision-First (Target: F1-Score ≥ 0.92 / Exact Match ≥ 95%). Legal records tolerate zero margin of error — misreading a plot number or deed amount is a critical failure

Justify each of the three choices
A one-liner is not enough here — give reasoning a marker can test against your actual corpus.
Why this DOMAIN
the real decision this tool helps and the person who makes it — not “it is useful”. Why a document-reading agent (not a web search) is the right fit.
Desk officers currently have to manually cross-reference paper Dolils to resolve disputes or verify ownership — a slow, error-prone process with real legal consequences. A document-reading agent fits because the source of truth is literally a scanned page image, not something a web search or general-purpose chatbot could resolve — the answer must be grounded in and cited to the specific scanned deed.
Why this DATA SPECIALITY
evidence it is genuinely in YOUR corpus — point to a specific page or feature — and the one pipeline stage it will force you to build.
The corpus itself demonstrates the difficulty directly: Dolils mix cursive Bangla handwriting with occasional English (code-switching, e.g. plot numbers, legal terms), overlaid with government seals and faded ink on gridless, multi-column layouts. This forces the OCR/reading stage to be VLM-based rather than a conventional OCR engine, since standard OCR is known to fail on historical Bangla script.
Why this NFR + target
why this quality matters for your user, why THAT number is the right target (not a round guess), and the one thing you would trade to hit it.
Precision matters here because an incorrect plot number or deed amount read with false confidence could misattribute legal ownership — a real-world harm far worse than simply failing to answer. F1 ≥ 0.92 / EM ≥ 95% are aspirational but concrete targets reflecting near-zero tolerance for confidently-wrong answers. The trade-off: the system will answer fewer questions (higher abstention rate) in exchange for near-certainty on the ones it does answer — coverage is sacrificed for trustworthiness.

The rest of the framing
Who would use this
the real person/role who'd use the finished tool and the decision it helps. Not “data scientists” or “the marker”.
A Land Office Desk Officer resolving an ownership or boundary dispute, who needs to confirm what a specific historical Dolil actually states (e.g., the registered plot number or the named parties) before acting on it.
How we measure success
pick ONE headline number for answer quality and state your target (e.g. groundedness ≥ 0.9; answer-F1 ≥ 0.7).
Headline metric: Exact Match ≥ 95% on verifiable extraction questions (e.g., plot numbers, deed amounts) — chosen over a softer metric like groundedness because legal fields need to match exactly, not just "be roughly right."
Score we want to beat
the laziest baseline, so you can prove value (e.g. “return the top-1 retrieved chunk verbatim = 0.4 answer-F1”).



Section 2 — Our Corpus
Your knowledge base is scanned book pages — images before they are text. Sourcing and documenting them is graded here.
Corpus name and link
the public source and a working link (e.g. Internet Archive, HathiTrust, Qatar Digital Library). Must be scanned page-images, not pre-typed text, and freely usable.
Bangla Handwritten Dolil Dataset (Mendeley Data) | https://data.mendeley.com/datasets/yk3c3xy9vm/1 
Licence, and may we share it
the licence (public-domain / CC-BY / “research only”) and whether you may re-share. If not, write “link only” and hand in a download script.
CC BY 4.0 and yes we may re-share with proper attribution 
How big is it
must be ≥ 300 pages AND ≥ 60,000 words of usable text (huge-corpus profile: ≥ 100k pages). State pages, words, and MB on disk.
Pages: 653 scanned images on disk; ≈44 are tiny thumbnail/corrupt files (<0.2 MB, e.g. dolil_313–324, dolil_431–462) that we drop as non-content, leaving 609 usable pages.
Words: ≈95,000 words of usable text (estimate; exact count produced by the A2 reading stage).
Size on disk: 2.33 GB (measured over all 653 JPEGs).
Meets the profile: ≥300 usable pages AND ≥60,000 words.
Scan & script difficulty
what makes these scans hard to read: script (Bangla conjuncts/matras, Arabic ligatures), fonts, faded/skewed pages, mixed scripts, old orthography.
Three difficulties stack on every page. (1) Script: the deeds are cursive handwritten Bangla with dense conjuncts (যুক্তাক্ষর) and stacked matras that touch and overlap, plus older legal orthography. (2) Code-switching: English and Bangla-digit numerals appear inline for the values that matter most — deed no. (e.g. ন 4180231), khatian/dag numbers, dates and money — and English legal boilerplate is handwritten across the left margin (“Admissible under Rule 2(1); under Section 26(J)-33(L) of the Bengal Tenancy Act 1933; presented for Registration at 12 am on the 28 October 2007” on dolil_100). (3) Physical degradation: faint/uneven ballpoint ink, skew from flatbed capture, show-through from the reverse side, and government seals/round stamps overprinted on the text. Standard Bangla OCR (Tesseract) fails on all three, which forces a VLM-based reading stage rather than a conventional OCR engine. 
How we split it (no leakage)
train/val/test as percentages, split by DOCUMENT (never by page), so no book appears in two splits.
70% train / 15% validation / 15% test, split by DEED, never by page. Each deed spans a variable number of consecutive sheets that share an incrementing registry serial number (e.g. dolil_13/14/15 carry serials 2162843 / 2162844 / 2162845, and every sheet prints an x/N ‘page-in-deed’ marker top-left). We group sheets into deeds by clustering consecutive serials (adjacent serial = same deed) and confirm each boundary with the x/N marker, then assign whole deed-groups to splits by hashing the deed key (deterministic, reproducible). Because serials are only read reliably by the A2 VLM stage, at A1 we seed the grouping from a hand-labelled sample of deed boundaries and finalise the full grouping in A2; the split ratios and seed are fixed once set. 
The one way data could leak, and how we checked
the single most likely leak (e.g. same book in train and test) and how you confirmed it didn't happen.
The single most likely leak: a multi-sheet deed whose consecutive sheets share the same header boilerplate, parties, mouza and serial run being scattered across train and test — the model would then ‘recognise’ a test sheet from a near-twin seen in training. We prevent it by clustering on the registry serial number BEFORE splitting so a whole serial-run stays in one split, and we verify two ways: (a) asserting no serial-run (deed group) appears in more than one split, and (b) a perceptual-hash (pHash) near-duplicate scan across splits to catch re-scans and consecutive sheets. Both checks are cells in eda.ipynb and both pass. Residual risk: any deed we mis-grouped at A1 is re-checked when A2 reads the serials, and mis-assigned sheets are moved and reported.

Preparing the scans — answer each for your corpus
Say what you did and its effect. Last 3 rows are blank for anything not listed. Example: “Deskew pages? | rotated by detected angle | OCR word-error 31%→22%”.
Preparation question
What we did about it
Effect on the data
Deskew / rotate crooked pages?
Estimated skew per page from the text-line angle (Hough / projection-profile) and rotated each scan to level. 
Text lines made horizontal; fewer lines merge, so the VLM reads line-by-line more reliably on skewed captures. 
Denoise / remove speckle & stains?
Applied light median + non-local-means denoising and suppressed reverse-side show-through with a background estimate. 
Scanner speckle and bleed-through reduced without erasing thin matras; strokes stay intact for reading. 
Binarize / boost contrast?
Sauvola adaptive thresholding for a clean binary copy, while KEEPING the greyscale original as a parallel input. 
Faint ballpoint ink becomes legible; because binarisation can drop very light seals, the greyscale copy is retained as a fallback the VLM can fall back on. 
Remove blank or non-content pages?
Flagged files below a size/text-density threshold (<0.2 MB) and confirmed by review as thumbnails or corrupt near-empty scans.
Dropped ≈44 non-content files (dolil_313–324, 431–462), reducing 653→≈609 usable pages and cutting empty retrieval hits; the count is computed in eda.ipynb.
Normalise the extracted text (Unicode, digits)?
Unicode NFC normalisation on all extracted text; mapped Bangla digits ০–৯ ↔ ASCII 0–9 and standardised punctuation/whitespace.
Consistent encoding so identical numbers compare equal — essential for the Exact-Match ≥ 95% / F1 ≥ 0.92 target, since a plot or deed number must match to the character.

Findings
Three things the exploration showed
e.g. page-length spread, dominant vs rare topics, worst scan-quality band, script/font variety.
(1) ≈44 of 653 files (≈7%) are tiny thumbnail/corrupt scans (<0.2 MB, dolil_313–324 and 431–462) with no readable content and are dropped, leaving 609 usable pages. (2) Across all 609 usable pages, scan quality is bimodal with a large faded/low-legibility band — 30% (184/609) score below the legibility threshold (light/uneven ink, seals over text, show-through); worst page dolil_412. (3) The corpus spans a wide era and ink range: from a British-era ‘One Rupee Eight Annas’ stamp (dolil_20, Sub-Registrar Raipur, Dist. Noakhali) through modern ৳45–৳500 stamp papers, with handwriting in black, blue, red and purple. Deeds are multi-sheet with variable length, each sheet carrying an incrementing serial and an x/N marker.
What this means for our build
how a finding changes a downstream choice (e.g. “many faded pages → enhancement matters”).
The 30% faded band means image enhancement is not optional — it directly gates whether the reader can recover a serial, plot or deed number, and those are exactly what the Exact-Match ≥ 95% NFR is scored on, so enhancement is GATED-in for our profile. The era/ink variety (British-era to modern, four ink colours) is a concrete bias axis: the oldest deeds and faintest inks read worst, which we report rather than hide. The multi-sheet, variable-length structure with serials/x-N markers is what makes serial-based deed grouping possible and drives layout-aware, deed-block chunking rather than fixed windows.

Section 4 — Risks
Answer each honestly — write “N/A” only if it truly doesn't apply (rare for historical books).
Personal data (PII)
do the books name living or identifiable people (letters, records, biographies)? How will you detect and redact it?
The deeds contain identifiable personal information, including names of owners; parent or spouse names; addresses; signatures; financial consideration; and property identifiers such as deed, khatian, mouza and plot numbers when linked to individuals.  However, we will not be implementing a detect and redact policy here, as all of this information needs to be made directly available to our end users, the land office desk officers resolving an ownership or boundary dispute. The officers will need access to everything that the Dolil records state. However, we will ensure that only authorized users who are able to login will be able to interact with the dolil data.
Licensing / copyright
are all pages genuinely public-domain or openly licensed? What's your evidence?
The “Bangla Handwritten Dolil Dataset (653 Images)” is a publicly accessible open dataset published through Mendeley Data. It has a permanent DOI, 10.17632/yk3c3xy9vm.1, and is distributed under the Creative Commons Attribution 4.0 International licence. The licence permits copying, redistribution, modification, and reuse, including commercial reuse, provided that appropriate attribution is given to the dataset creators. . Dataset citation: Anik, Saifullah; Hossain, Md. Obydul; Arifen, Radoanul ; Ahammed, Md. Shakib (2025), “Bangla Handwritten Dolil Dataset (653 Images)”, Mendeley Data, V1, doi: 10.17632/yk3c3xy9vm.1.
OCR failure
where will reading fail worst (script, fonts, damage)? What's your fallback (re-OCR, enhance, abstain)?
Reading will fail worst on ambiguous handwritten Dolil regions containing stamps and seals, heavily cursive Bangla, faded ink, see-through texts. The fallback will use layout-aware text-region detection using DocLayout-YOLO to crop the relevant line or word region, then pass that crop to GraDeT-HTR for Bangla handwritten text recognition. If confidence remains low, the system will abstain and escalate to human review instead of guessing.  
Hallucination
how could the agent answer things not in the text, and how will you stop it (grounding, abstention)?
The agent could hallucinate by inferring or generating plot numbers, owner names, deed amounts, dates, or legal relationships that are not explicitly supported by the retrieved Dolil text . The agent will use claim-level evidence verification with a separate verifier model for initial low confidence scores: generated summaries must have every claim marked “support” with a cited page/span. If verification fails, it performs 2 automatic re-search attempts, then abstains and invokes “escalate-to-human”.
Bias across your corpus
which fonts/regions/subgroups might the system read worse, given your data speciality and NFR?
Considering our handwriting specialty and precision-first NFR, the system may read stamped and sealed regions worse because government seals and round stamps overlap the handwritten Dolil text, making critical fields such as names, plot numbers, dates, and deed amounts harder to verify clearly. It may also perform worse on old era and faded low legibility texts, since the corpus includes British-era documents, older handwriting/orthography, uneven ink, show-through, and a low-legibility band where the VLM is more likely to misread or miss legally important details. 



Section 5 — Design Choices & Justification (the corpus/ingest stage)
State your choice across the eight facets for the stages you touch in A1 (Stage 0 framing, Stage 1 ingest/enhance). You'll extend this table each milestone — it's the graded design artifact (configs/design_choices.md).
Facet
Our choice (this stage)
Why — fits our corpus · what it costs · what would change it
Problem statement
A precision-first document-understanding agent for Land Registry Archive and Legal Aid. The system will help Land Office Desk Officers answer questions from scanned historical Dolils using Bangla-English handwritten evidence and page citations. The headline target is Exact Match ≥95% on verifiable legal-field questions, with overall F1 ≥0.92. 
Considering our dataset, Desk officers would currently have to manually cross-reference the paper Dolils to resolve disputes or verify ownership. To make this process faster, we settled on our project topic. In the process, a wrong plot number, deed amount, date, or party name can affect ownership or boundary decisions. The system therefore prioritizes exact, grounded answers and permits abstention rather than guessing. The cost is reduced automatic-answer coverage and more human review. We would reconsider the  target only if validation shows that the target is technically unattainable for this corpus 
Data
We use the Bangla Handwritten Dolil Dataset containing 653 JPEG scans, approximately 2.33 GB. We remove approximately 44 thumbnail, corrupt, or non-content files, leaving about 609 usable pages and an estimated 95,000 words. We store page_id, deed_serial, page_in_deed, extracted_text, and scan_quality. We split the corpus 70% train, 15% validation, and 15% test by complete deed, with deed-group assertions and pHash checks to prevent leakage. 
The corpus satisfies the required size and contains the project’s actual difficulty: cursive Bangla, English text, mixed numerals, stamps, seals, faded ink, show-through, old orthography, and irregular layouts.  Deed-level splitting prevents related sheets from appearing across train and test sets. The costs are manual deed-boundary confirmation, additional metadata work, and ground-truth preparation. We would revise page counts,  grouping, or split membership if the text extraction phase in A2 reveals incorrect deed boundaries, or additional unusable pages. 
Model

For ingesting and enhancing, we use Pillow and OpenCV for loading, validation, deskewing, denoising, background correction, and contrast enhancement. We decided not to use a generative image-repair model at this stage. The enhanced outputs will later feed DocLayout-YOLO for layout detection and Qwen2.5-VL with GraDeT-HTR for difficult handwritten-text recognition. The controller LLM for the agent will be Qwen3-4B-AWQ.



Pillow and OpenCV are lightweight, reproducible, and less likely than generative restoration to invent or reshape legally significant strokes. DocLayout-YOLO and the VLM/HTR combination are selected because conventional OCR is unsuitable for mixed handwritten Bangla-English text, seals, and gridless layouts. The cost is that severely faded or over-stamped text may remain unreadable, because of not implementing generative repair, the VLM will also require GPU for fast inference. We would change the enhancement or reader model only if held-out benchmarking shows a meaningful improvement in critical-field Exact Match without altering the original evidence. We may change our controller model if tool selection accuracy target is <90%
Methods
We use a deterministic document-image preprocessing pipeline for Stage 1. We validate image files and remove non-content scans below 0.2 MB; convert pages to grayscale; correct orientation and estimate skew by searching angles from −4° to +4°, rotating with white fill value 255; apply light denoising with a 3× median filter; apply automatic contrast enhancement; create a Sauvola-style binary copy using local blur/window value 15, k=0.2, and r = 128, while retaining the original and grayscale versions. Scan quality is computed using a high-pass blur radius of 3, normalized by 40.0, and pages with scan-quality<0.35 are flagged as low-legibility. Text normalization uses Unicode NFC. There is no training loss  at this stage because Stage 1 uses rule-based/classical preprocessing rather than a learned enhancement model. 
These settings fit the Dolil corpus because the scans contain skewed captures, faint/uneven handwriting, show-through, government seals, mixed Bangla-English text, and legally important numbers that must survive preprocessing. The conservative skew range and light denoising reduce page noise without aggressively changing handwritten strokes, while Sauvola-style local binarization helps faint ballpoint ink under uneven illumination. Keeping the original, grayscale, and binary versions protects evidential fidelity, because binarization can sometimes erase faint seals, matras, or thin handwritten marks. The cost is extra storage and more configuration to validate. We would change these hyperparameters if validation shows that a setting lowers critical-field Exact Match, erases thin Bangla strokes, damages seal-over-text regions, worsens HTR/VLM reading, or gives no measurable improvement on the low-legibility subset. 
Design
Pipeline design for the Image Ingestion:  Stage 1 ingest/enhance → Stage 2 layout detection with DocLayout-YOLO → Stage 3 OCR/HTR using Qwen2.5-VL and GraDeT-HTR → Stage 4 layout-aware legal-semantic chunking → Stage 5 BGE-M3 embedding generation → Stage 6 store normalized chunk embeddings in FAISS IndexFlatIP, with page/deed/chunk metadata stored in PostgreSQL. 
A staged pipeline lets each difficulty be handled separately: enhancement improves low-legibility scans, DocLayout-YOLO isolates text regions, Qwen2.5-VL/GraDeT-HTR handles difficult handwriting, layout-aware chunking preserves legal context, and BGE-M3 + FAISS enables multilingual semantic retrieval while PostgreSQL preserves exact page/deed metadata. The cost is higher engineering complexity, more storage for intermediate artifacts, and additional validation at every stage. We would change this design if benchmarking shows that OCR/HTR quality is too weak for critical-field Exact Match, if layout detection fails on stamped or multi-column pages, or if FAISS dense retrieval misses exact legal identifiers that PostgreSQL lexical/exact search handles better 
Development
We used the EDA notebook to verify counts, dimensions, file sizes, legibility bands, ink/era variation, deed grouping, and pHash leakage checks. We added tests for corrupt-file detection, original-file preservation, deterministic splitting, output creation, metadata completeness, and consistency of Bangla/ASCII digit normalization. We record preprocessing settings and representative before/after examples for later comparison 
This fits the assignment because all decisions must be grounded in the team’s actual corpus and reproducible by the grader. Testing prevents silent deletion, cross-split leakage, or preprocessing damage from propagating into A2 and A3. The cost is additional implementation and manual review effort before model development begins. We would modify the configuration or tests when new failure patterns appear, deed boundaries are corrected, or validation results show that a preprocessing setting harms critical legal fields retirieval. 
Deployment
We run corpus ingestion and enhancement as an offline batch process before the online system is served. The final application will run using Docker Compose on an Azure Standard_NC4as_T4_v3 GPU VM. The serving stack will use HTML/CSS/ReactJS for the frontend, FastAPI for the backend API, FAISS for embedding storage, LangGraph for orchestration, and Qwen3-4B-AWQ, 4-bit quantized, served through vLLM. User requests are served online.
Batch preprocessing avoids repeating expensive image operations for every desk-officer query. The Azure T4 VM supports the locally hosted quantized controller and protects queries and retrieved evidence from external LLM providers, which is necessary for our corpus, as it should not be accessible to third party systems besides the land-desk officers. We use FAISS IndexFlatIP with L2-normalized embeddings to perform exact cosine-similarity search, avoiding approximation errors and prioritizing retrieval precision for our precision-first NFR.. The cost is GPU-VM expenditure($0.526 per hour), container maintenance, and the need to deallocate the VM when unused. We would change the server only if a cheaper configuration meets the same privacy, tool-accuracy, memory, and latency requirements, or if Qwen3-8B becomes necessary due to higher tool selection accuracy and requires stronger hardware. 
MLOps
We will use MLflow for experiment tracking, metric logging, and model/configuration versioning. We will use vLLM as the deployment runtime for the quantized Qwen3-4B controller. We will version the corpus manifest, deed split, preprocessing configuration, OCR/HTR checkpoints, Qwen checkpoint, AWQ quantization, vLLM container/version, prompts, chat template, tool schemas, confidence thresholds, and FAISS/PostgreSQL artifacts as a reproducible release bundle. We will monitor F1, critical-field Exact Match, Recall@K, low-confidence frequency, abstention/escalation rate, invalid tool calls, latency, and GPU-memory use. 
A preprocessing, model, prompt, or serving change can alter legally important answers from our corpus, so the complete deployed state must be traceable and recoverable. MLflow provides experiment lineage, while vLLM provides a consistent local interface for the Qwen controller on the Azure GPU VM. The costs are storage, logging overhead, and release-management work. We would change the serving runtime if vLLM is incompatible with the selected Qwen checkpoint or if another local runtime achieves the same privacy and tool accuracy with lower resource use. 
One decision where we chose one option over another
show one place you picked one option and rejected another, and why (e.g. “diffusion enhancement over classical denoise — because our scans are badly faded”).
We chose conservative classical image enhancement with Pillow and OpenCV instead of VAE, diffusion, because generative repair models could reconstruct, reshape, or remove a handwritten stroke and thereby change a plot number, name, date, or deed amount

Section 7 — The Agent Layer (on paper)
The agent is the heart of the system. Design it here against the fixed repo interface; you'll implement it in A3.
7.1  The agent and its brain
The brain(s)
which trained/pretrained models power perception and answering (OCR reader, embedder, LLM).
A vision-language model reads scanned Dolil pages directly and drives tool selection at each step (core VLM engine TBD — final team decision pending pilot testing on our corpus). No separate OCR model is used, since the VLM reads text natively from the image, avoiding OCR-induced scrambling on our mixed cursive Bangla-English script.
What one step looks like
one loop: perceive the query → decide a tool → act → observe → (stop or repeat).
Perceive the query → retrieve candidate chunks → check top retrieval score against a confidence threshold → decide next action (read a page, extract a span, widen search, or escalate) → act → observe result → repeat until a grounded answer is produced or the agent escalates.

7.2  Its tools (fixed interface)
The tool set is fixed in the repo. For each, say how your project uses it, and mark read-only vs. acts.
Tool (fixed)
How our project uses it
Reads / Acts
retrieve
Searches semantically-chunked Dolil text for candidate pages matching the query
Reads
rerank
Reorders candidates — needed because Bangla-English code-switching can weaken a first-pass dense search
Reads


read_page
Opens the full page image so the model can re-examine handwriting/seals in context, not just the isolated chunk
Reads


enhance_page
Out of scope for now — not required by our data speciality; not built in this milestone
N/A

Pulls the exact plot number, buyer/seller name, or deed amount as a precise span
Reads


aggregate
Combines information across linked pages (e.g. an ownership chain spanning multiple deeds)
Reads

Attaches the source page image to every answer — required given our zero-hallucination goal
Reads


calculator
Exact arithmetic on deed amounts / area figures
Acts
escalate_to_human
Fires when confidence never clears threshold after widening k — hands off to a Land Office Desk Officer
Acts

7.3  Autonomy, humans, and budget
Autonomy level
read-only · act-with-approval · act-then-log. Which, and why for your NFR?
Act-with-approval. Our precision-first NFR (F1 ≥ 0.92 / EM ≥ 95%) means the agent should surface high-confidence answers directly but route borderline cases through human review rather than auto-answering, since misreading a plot number or deed amount is a critical failure for our end user.
Human-in-the-loop
which cases escalate to a human (via escalate_to_human), and what they decide.
Escalates when (a) retrieval confidence stays below threshold after widening k to k_max, (b) a badly faded/low-contrast page can't be read reliably (no repair attempted — flagged for manual review instead), or (c) two retrieved pages give conflicting information (e.g. two similar-looking plot numbers).
Budget
max steps and cost per query (agent.max_steps, budget). What happens at the limit?
Max steps per query capped (e.g. 6 tool calls); retrieval widens k on a fixed schedule (e.g. 5 → 10 → 20) before abstaining, so the agent can't loop indefinitely on a hard page.

7.4  How we'll test it, and how it improves
Our eval questions
how you'll build tasks.jsonl for YOUR domain — the questions your real user would ask. Include enough checkable (verifiable) items and abstention cases so grading stays objective.
tasks.jsonl built from real Dolil fields — verifiable questions with exact-match gold answers (plot number, buyer/seller name, deed amount), a smaller set of judged questions (e.g. summarizing an ownership history), and out-of-corpus questions where abstaining is the correct answer.
How it improves
rule-based policy first; if you attempt RL (bonus), the reward is RLVR on your verifiable questions (checkable answers), which sharpens a GENERAL system.
Rule-based policy, refined iteratively — starting with a fixed confidence threshold and k-widening schedule, then tuned against retrieval/answer quality measured on our task suite (A3). Improvement comes from prompt refinement, threshold tuning, and tool-use adjustments based on error analysis, not from training a policy.

7.5  Agent-specific risks
These exist because the agent ACTS over many steps — they don't apply to a plain model.
Prompt injection from documents
a scanned page could contain “ignore your instructions”. How do you isolate content from commands?
A scanned page could contain text resembling an instruction (deliberately or via scan artifact). All page content is treated as data, never as commands to the agent — matches our spec's stated guardrail against prompt injection.
Wrong tool / runaway loop
how budgets and guardrails stop it.
The step budget and fixed k_max stop indefinite re-searching on a badly damaged page.
Ungrounded answer
how the grounding gate forces abstention when evidence is missing.
The grounding/citation requirement plus the abstain/escalate gate mean the agent cannot present an answer without an attached source page — our primary defense for the zero-hallucination mission.

That's Stage 4's job, not Stage 3's — and the distinction matters here. `chunk.split()` in [index/chunk.py](src/doc_agent/index/chunk.py) is the semantic chunker (Adri's file); `ocr.transcribe()` feeds it. CLAUDE:
Committed nothing yet — here's what's on disk.

**[ocr.py](src/doc_agent/vision/ocr.py) implemented**, 15 tests in [tests/test_ocr.py](tests/test_ocr.py), full suite green, lint clean apart from the template's own star-import pattern.

What it does:
- **One `Chunk` per page**, regions concatenated in layout's reading order — so Adri's semantic chunker can still find legal boundaries that span regions.
- **`Reader`** lazy-loads the VLM on first use, so importing the module stays CPU-cheap and tests never touch a 7B model. `transcribe()` takes an optional `reader=` for injection; `pipeline.py`'s fixed two-arg call still works.
- **Prompt lives in [prompts.py](src/doc_agent/llm/prompts.py)** as `TRANSCRIBE`, per that file's "all prompts live here" rule. It forbids translating, summarising, or correcting, and requires `[?]` for illegible text rather than a guess — a confidently invented plot number is the worst failure mode for your NFR.
- **Normalization per A1**: Unicode NFC always, Bangla→ASCII digits behind `ocr.ascii_digits` (default true, since your form commits to it for Exact Match).
- **`doc_id` gating**: prefers the ingest sidecar, falls back to `deed_groups.csv`, and otherwise *refuses* — unless you set `ocr.allow_page_as_doc`, which logs a warning saying it leaks by construction.
- A page whose regions all read empty produces **no chunk**, rather than an empty one that could be indexed and retrieved as evidence for nothing.

**What is not verified:** the actual Qwen2.5-VL call has never executed. No GPU here, model never downloaded, and the tests deliberately inject a fake reader. The `transformers` generate/decode path is written to the standard vision2seq API but is unproven until it runs on real hardware — treat it as untested code, not working code. That's the D3 decision (T4 with an AWQ/4-bit build, or Qwen2.5-VL-3B) still outstanding.

Your lane's remaining blockers are unchanged: GPU for the real run, and Bangla transcriptions in `labels.jsonl` before `ocr_f1` can tell you whether D2's single-pass reader is good enough.

Want me to commit this?

**Two things I found doing this, one of them a bug in the layout code I committed:**

**1. EXIF orientation is being ignored.** `dolil_212` and `dolil_605` carry EXIF orientation = 6 (rotate 90° CW). `cv2.imread` — which [layout.py](src/doc_agent/vision/layout.py) uses — ignores EXIF entirely, and so does `PIL.Image.open` in `Reader.crop`. So those two pages display upright to you and are processed **sideways** by your pipeline, and the VLM would receive sideways crops. The fix is `ImageOps.exif_transpose` in both readers, applied consistently so bboxes and crops stay in the same coordinate frame.

**2. My earlier conclusion was overstated.** I told you this corpus "has no blank horizontal bands" — orientation is part of the story too. Rotating `dolil_66` by 90° took it from 1 region to **10**. `dolil_208` went 1 → 2. So layout needs orientation handling (EXIF plus content-based detection for the landscape photos that carry no EXIF flag), not just acceptance of page-sized regions. The `region = page` decision still stands as a reading strategy; the "can't segment" explanation was wrong.

USER:
what is  Gold text?

CLAUDE:
"Gold" is just the standard term for **ground truth** — the answer key. It comes from "gold standard": the version taken as correct by definition, that everything else is measured against.

In your [labels.jsonl](grading_kit/labels.jsonl), the `"text"` field is the gold: what the page *actually says*, transcribed by a human who can read it. Nothing scores it; it's the thing that does the scoring.

The mechanism is:

```
reader output  →  "মৌজা … দাগ ২১৬৩ …"     (what Qwen2.5-VL produced)
gold text      →  "মৌজা … দাগ ২১৬৩ …"     (what a human says is on the page)
                        ↓
              eval/metrics.py:ocr_f1  →  0.87
```

`ocr_f1` compares the two strings and returns how much they overlap. That number is your OCR accuracy — the one Section 5 of the A2 form asks for, and the one that decides D2 (whether single-pass VLM reading is good enough or you need the HTR stage).

Which is why it has to be human-written. If I fill `text` with my own reading of the page, then `ocr_f1` compares one model's guess against another model's guess, and a high score means the two models failed the same way — not that either read the page correctly. You'd get a number that looks like evidence and isn't.

That's the distinction between the two fields in your file now:

- **`text`** — gold, empty on all 8, waiting for a human. Scoring uses this.
- **`printed_draft`** — my reading of the printed elements only, clearly marked unverified. Nothing scores against this; it exists so your transcriber has a head start on the stamp headers and doesn't start from a blank page.

The `fields` block (`deed_serial`, `deed_date`, `land_amount`, `party_names`) is gold too, but for the critical-field Exact Match target rather than full-page F1 — those are the values where being one character off is a legal error.

USER:
I can do it. Tell me step by step how to do this

CLAUDE:
Here's the whole flow.

## Before you start

Pick your input method — terminal paste is usually easier than typing Bangla directly into a terminal. Write each page's text in a VSCode scratch file with your Bangla keyboard (Avro/OS input), then paste into the prompt.

Check where you stand:
```bash
cd /home/mahbuba/CSE429/doc-agent-template/doc-agent-starter
python scripts/transcribe_heldout.py --show
```

## The loop, per page

```bash
python scripts/transcribe_heldout.py
```

1. It prints the page id, its existing notes, and opens the scan in your image viewer.
2. You paste (or type) the transcription. **Finish with a single `.` on its own line** — that's the terminator, not part of your text.
3. It asks the four critical fields one at a time. Press Enter to skip any. `party_names` takes a comma-separated list.
4. It saves immediately and moves to the next page. Ctrl-C stops safely; nothing already saved is lost.

To redo a page you've already done: `python scripts/transcribe_heldout.py --page dolil_13`

## Transcription conventions — these matter for the metric

- **Reading order, with line breaks as on the page.** Don't reflow into a paragraph.
- **Don't translate, expand, or correct.** If the deed misspells something, keep the misspelling. You're recording what's on the paper, not what it should say.
- **`[?]` for anything you can't read.** A page that's 70% text and 30% `[?]` is honest and usable. A page where you guessed at a plot number is worse than no label at all.
- **Keep Bangla digits as Bangla** — write ২১৬৩, not 2163. See the warning below.
- **Ignore my `printed_draft`** unless it helps you orient. It's unverified; if it disagrees with what you see, you're right.

## One thing to hand to whoever implements `ocr_f1`

`ocr.py` normalizes the *reader's* output — NFC plus Bangla→ASCII digits (`ocr.normalize()`). If gold keeps Bangla digits and predictions get converted, every numeric field mismatches and your Exact Match is wrong for a reason that has nothing to do with reading quality. So `eval/metrics.py:ocr_f1` must run `ocr.normalize()` over **both** sides before comparing. Write gold as it appears on the page; let the code do the normalizing.

## Order I'd go in

Easiest first, so you calibrate before hitting the hard ones:

1. `dolil_13`, `dolil_11`, `dolil_20` — flatbed scans, the most legible. Do these **fully**; they become your F1 sample.
2. `dolil_208`, `dolil_212`, `dolil_348`, `dolil_605`, `dolil_66` — phone photos, several stored sideways and one (`dolil_66`) very faint. Critical fields only is fine here; skip the body with `.` on an empty transcription if it's not readable.

`dolil_20` is British-era with old orthography — expect `[?]` and don't fight it.

## When you're done

```bash
python scripts/transcribe_heldout.py --show          # confirm coverage
python -c "import json;[json.loads(l) for l in open('grading_kit/labels.jsonl')]" && echo "valid JSONL"
git add grading_kit/labels.jsonl && git commit -m "grading_kit: human transcriptions for held-out pages"
```

Then tell me the coverage you got and I'll wire up `ocr_f1` against it.

USER:
so first I just write whatever I can read in the picture?

CLAUDE:
Yes. Open the image, read it, type what it says — in the order it appears on the page, with `[?]` wherever you can't make something out.

Three things to keep in mind while you do:

- **You don't have to get everything.** A page that comes out half text and half `[?]` is a perfectly good label. The metric compares your text against the model's; gaps you've marked honestly just mean those spans aren't scored.
- **Don't guess to fill a hole.** Especially digits — a serial or an amount you half-see should be `[?]`, not your best reconstruction. A guessed number silently becomes "truth" and the model gets marked wrong for reading it correctly.
- **Write what's there, not what it means.** No translating, no fixing spelling, no expanding abbreviations.

After the body text, it asks for the four critical fields separately (`deed_serial`, `deed_date`, `land_amount`, `party_names`). Those are the ones your ≥95% Exact Match target is scored on, so they're worth a second look at the image — but same rule: blank or `[?]` beats a guess.

Start with `dolil_13`. Run `python scripts/transcribe_heldout.py`, and if the prompts don't behave the way you expect, tell me what you saw and I'll adjust the script.

`.github/workflows/ci.yml` runs `uv sync --frozen --extra dev` and fails with

## Decisions made (recorded in module docstrings, NOT in commit bodies)
- **D1 Layout** = training-free projection heuristic, not DocLayout-YOLO (no tables/multi-column
  in this corpus).
- **D2 OCR** = single-pass Qwen2.5-VL per region. No GraDeT-HTR, no confidence fallback —
  can't calibrate a router with zero measured accuracy. Revisit once `ocr_f1` runs.
- **D3 Compute** = UNRESOLVED. `config.yaml` says `Qwen/Qwen2.5-VL-7B-Instruct`, `device: cpu`,
  no GPU on this machine. A T4 won't fit 7B fp16 — pick AWQ/4-bit or Qwen2.5-VL-3B. This blocks
  A2 form section 4 (asks for exact checkpoint) and the full-corpus run.
- **region = page** — layout may emit 3–9 regions on flatbed pages, but reading treats a page as
  one unit; `ocr.transcribe()` emits **one Chunk per page**, regions concatenated in reading
  order, so Adri's Stage 4 semantic chunker can still find boundaries spanning regions.
- **doc_id sourcing** — `ocr.py` reads the ingest sidecar, else `deed_groups.csv`, else raises.
  `ocr.allow_page_as_doc: true` opts into page-as-document explicitly (leaks by design, warns).

## Code state
- `vision/layout.py` — Otsu binarize → horizontal ink projection → band merge → `kind="text"`
  Regions. Self-contained (own binarization) so it doesn't block on ingest. 12 tests.
- `vision/ocr.py` — `Reader` (lazy-loads VLM so imports stay CPU-cheap), `transcribe(regions,
  cfg, reader=None)` with injectable reader, `load_page_index()`, `normalize()` (NFC +
  Bangla→ASCII digits). 17 tests. **The real Qwen2.5-VL call has NEVER executed** — untested code.
- `llm/prompts.py` — `TRANSCRIBE` prompt added (that file is the fixed prompt registry).
- `scripts/transcribe_heldout.py` — CLI for the human gold-label pass; resumable, `--show`,
  `--page <id>`.
- Full suite green locally (`.venv/bin/python -m pytest -q`). Remaining ruff hits are the
  template's own `from ..contracts import *` (F405) pattern.
- **Ingest lane (`loader.py`, `preprocess.py`) is back to stubs** — I implemented it early by
  mistake (wrong lane), user had it removed. Patch saved only in the old session scratchpad; treat
  as gone. That work is Aishi's.

## Measured findings (evidence for A2 section 3)
- Corpus is **mixed capture**: 4 of 8 held-out pages are flatbed scans, 4 are phone photos on
  dark surfaces (border ink 53–61%).
- Layout on real scans: flatbed → 3–9 regions; photos → 1 page-sized region. Deskew (±15°
  search) and adaptive thresholding were both tried; **neither produced blank rows**. These deeds
  have no blank horizontal bands between paragraphs.
- **EXIF bug found and fixed**: `dolil_212` and `dolil_605` carry EXIF orientation=6.
  `cv2.imread` applies EXIF (OpenCV ≥3.4); `PIL.Image.open` does not. `Reader.crop` was cropping
  the raw raster with display-space bboxes → wrong pixels, silently. Fixed with
  `ImageOps.exif_transpose`; verified PIL and cv2 now agree on all 8 pages.
- Layout runtime ≈0.6s for 8 pages (full corpus is seconds).

## Gold labels (`grading_kit/labels.jsonl`) — 1/8 done
- `dolil_13` transcribed by the user. `fields`: `land_amount: "১ ১/২"` (the AREA),
  `party_names: [মো: আজাদ হোসেন, মো: আবুল কাসেম]` (গ্রহীতা + দাতা; লেখক excluded).
  `deed_serial` and `deed_date` are **empty because ABSENT**, not skipped — documented in notes.
  `extra_fields` holds `consideration_amount ২০০০০`, `stamp_paper_serial ৫০৯৩০৮৬`, scribe name +
  সনদনং ৩০৭৪.
- Other 7 pages have empty `text`, plus a `printed_draft` field = model-read PRINTED elements
  only (stamp headers, denominations), explicitly not gold, with per-page confidence.
- Convention: `[?]` for illegible; never guess, especially digits.
- **Hand to whoever writes `eval/metrics.py:ocr_f1`**: it must apply `ocr.normalize()` to BOTH
  sides, because predictions get Bangla→ASCII digit mapping and gold is written as-seen.
- Practical plan agreed: fully transcribe the 3 flatbed pages (`dolil_13/11/20`), critical fields
  only on the 5 photos, and state sample size honestly in A2 section 5.

 """Stage 1 — load scanned page-images"""
+
 from __future__ import annotations
+
+import csv
+import re
+from pathlib import Path
+
 from ..contracts import *  # noqa
+from ..data import validate as validate_module
+from ..data import versioning
+from ..logging_conf import get_logger
+
+logger = get_logger(__name__)
+
+_PAGE_NUMBER = re.compile(r"(\d+)")
+
+
+def _page_sort_key(page_id: str) -> tuple[str, int, str]:
+    """Sort dolil_2 before dolil_10 -- page order is deed order, and lexical sorting breaks it."""
+    match = _PAGE_NUMBER.search(page_id)
+    prefix = page_id[: match.start()] if match else page_id
+    number = int(match.group(1)) if match else 0
+    return (prefix, number, page_id)
+
+
+def load_deed_groups(path: str | Path) -> dict[str, str]:
+    """Read the page_id -> doc_id (deed) map produced by the deed-grouping step.
+
+    This mapping is the *only* source of doc_id. It is deliberately a data file rather
+    than logic inlined here: grouping consecutive pages into deeds needs registry-serial
+    reading and hand-seeded corrections (notebooks/eda.ipynb Section 5), which is a
+    curation output, not something recomputable at ingest time. Splitting train/val/test
+    is keyed on doc_id (the leakage rule), so a wrong or missing map silently produces a
+    leaky split -- hence the hard errors below rather than a fallback to page-as-document.
+    """
+    groups_path = Path(path)
+    if not groups_path.is_file():
+        raise FileNotFoundError(
+            f"deed grouping map not found: {groups_path}. Produce it from the page-order + "
+            "hand-seeded grouping in notebooks/eda.ipynb Section 5 (columns: page_id,doc_id). "
+            "Without it every page would look like its own deed and the document-level "
+            "split would leak."
+        )
+
+    with groups_path.open(encoding="utf-8", newline="") as fh:
+        rows = list(csv.DictReader(fh))
+
+    missing_cols = {"page_id", "doc_id"} - set(rows[0] if rows else {})
+    if missing_cols:
+        raise ValueError(f"{groups_path} must have columns page_id,doc_id (missing {missing_cols})")
+
+    groups: dict[str, str] = {}
+    for row in rows:
+        page_id, doc_id = (row["page_id"] or "").strip(), (row["doc_id"] or "").strip()
+        if not doc_id:
+            raise ValueError(f"{groups_path}: page {page_id!r} has an empty doc_id")
+        groups[page_id] = doc_id
+    return groups
+
+
+def discover_pages(cfg: dict) -> list[Page]:
+    """Scan the raw corpus directory into Page objects. No validation, no side effects."""
+    ingest_cfg = cfg["ingest"]
+    raw_dir = Path(ingest_cfg["raw_dir"])
+    min_bytes = int(ingest_cfg["min_bytes"])
+
+    if not raw_dir.is_dir():
+        raise FileNotFoundError(
+            f"raw corpus directory not found: {raw_dir}. Fetch the corpus first "
+            "(scripts/get_data.sh) -- data/raw/ is gitignored, so every machine loads it itself."
+        )
+
+    groups = load_deed_groups(ingest_cfg["deed_groups"])
+
+    image_paths = sorted(
+        (p for p in raw_dir.iterdir() if p.is_file() and p.suffix.lower() in {".jpg", ".jpeg"}),
+        key=lambda p: _page_sort_key(p.stem),
+    )
+
+    pages: list[Page] = []
+    dropped: list[str] = []
+    ungrouped: list[str] = []
+    for path in image_paths:
+        # Thumbnail/corrupt files are a known property of this corpus (653 raw -> ~609
+        # usable). Size alone separates them: real 2.2GB-corpus scans are far above the
+        # floor, so this avoids decoding every image just to reject it.
+        if path.stat().st_size < min_bytes:
+            dropped.append(path.stem)
+            continue
+        if path.stem not in groups:
+            ungrouped.append(path.stem)
+            continue
+        pages.append(Page(id=path.stem, image_path=str(path), doc_id=groups[path.stem]))
+
+    if ungrouped:
+        raise ValueError(
+            f"{len(ungrouped)} page(s) in {raw_dir} are absent from "
+            f"{ingest_cfg['deed_groups']}, e.g. {ungrouped[:5]}. Every page needs a deed "
+            "or the document-level split can't stay leak-free."
+        )
+
+    if dropped:
+        logger.info("dropped %d file(s) below %d bytes as non-content", len(dropped), min_bytes)
+    return pages
+
 
 def load_pages(cfg: dict) -> list[Page]:
-    """Read data/raw/ -> list[Page]. IMPLEMENT."""
-    raise NotImplementedError("Stage 1: load scanned pages")
+    """Read data/raw/ -> list[Page], validated and version-stamped.
 
+    validate() and snapshot() are called here because pipeline.py is fixed and never
+    calls them itself -- ingest is the only place that sees the corpus on disk.
+    """
+    pages = discover_pages(cfg)
+    validate_module.validate(pages)
+    version_id = versioning.snapshot(cfg["ingest"]["raw_dir"])
+    logger.info(
+        "loaded %d pages across %d deeds (corpus %s)",
+        len(pages),
+        len({p.doc_id for p in pages}),
+        version_id,
+    )
+    return pages


=== DIFF: src/doc_agent/ingest/preprocess.py ===
