-"""Stage 1 — deskew / denoise / binarize / augment"""
+"""Stage 1 — deskew / denoise / binarize / augment
+
+Classical and deterministic, per configs/design_choices.md row 1. No generative repair:
+a VAE/diffusion model could reshape a handwritten stroke and change a legally significant
+plot number, name, date, or amount, so Stage 1 only ever removes noise, never invents ink.
+
+The `Page` contract is fixed at {id, image_path, doc_id} with nowhere to hang variants or
+flags, so the derived artefacts live on disk instead: each page's deskewed greyscale
+becomes its new image_path (the primary input for layout/OCR), the Sauvola binarization is
+written beside it as a parallel copy, the raw scan in data/raw/ is never modified, and the
+per-page skew/quality/legibility record goes to a sidecar JSONL for downstream stages and
+for the A2 scan-quality report.
+"""
+
 from __future__ import annotations
+
+import json
+from pathlib import Path
+
+import cv2
+import numpy as np
+from skimage.filters import threshold_sauvola
+
 from ..contracts import *  # noqa
+from ..logging_conf import get_logger
+
+logger = get_logger(__name__)
+
+SIDECAR_NAME = "preprocess.jsonl"
+
+
+def _read_grey(image_path: str) -> np.ndarray:
+    image = cv2.imread(image_path, cv2.IMREAD_GRAYSCALE)
+    if image is None:
+        raise ValueError(f"unreadable scan: {image_path}")
+    return image
+
+
+def _row_profile_score(image: np.ndarray) -> float:
+    """Variance of the horizontal ink profile -- maximal when text lines are level."""
+    ink = 255.0 - image.astype(np.float64)
+    return float(np.var(ink.sum(axis=1)))
+
+
+def _rotate(image: np.ndarray, angle_deg: float) -> np.ndarray:
+    h, w = image.shape[:2]
+    matrix = cv2.getRotationMatrix2D((w / 2, h / 2), angle_deg, 1.0)
+    return cv2.warpAffine(
+        image, matrix, (w, h), flags=cv2.INTER_LINEAR,
+        borderMode=cv2.BORDER_CONSTANT, borderValue=255,  # fill=255: page ground, not black
+    )
+
+
+def estimate_skew(image: np.ndarray, cfg: dict) -> float:
+    """Search [-range, +range] for the rotation that best aligns text rows.
+
+    Projection-profile search rather than Hough: the corpus is dense handwritten Bangla
+    with few straight rulings for Hough to lock onto, while the row-ink profile peaks
+    sharply once cursive lines are level. Returns the angle the page is skewed BY, so
+    deskew() rotates by its negation.
+    """
+    limit = float(cfg["skew_range_deg"])
+    step = float(cfg["skew_step_deg"])
+    angles = np.arange(-limit, limit + step / 2, step)
+    scores = [_row_profile_score(_rotate(image, -angle)) for angle in angles]
+    return float(angles[int(np.argmax(scores))])
+
+
+def deskew(image: np.ndarray, cfg: dict) -> np.ndarray:
+    angle = estimate_skew(image, cfg)
+    return _rotate(image, -angle) if angle else image
+
+
+def denoise(image: np.ndarray, cfg: dict) -> np.ndarray:
+    """Light median (salt-and-pepper scan speckle) then non-local means (show-through)."""
+    smoothed = cv2.medianBlur(image, int(cfg["median_ksize"]))
+    return cv2.fastNlMeansDenoising(smoothed, None, h=float(cfg["nlm_h"]))
+
+
+def binarize(image: np.ndarray, cfg: dict) -> np.ndarray:
+    """Sauvola adaptive threshold -- local, so it survives uneven ageing across a scan."""
+    threshold = threshold_sauvola(
+        image,
+        window_size=int(cfg["sauvola_window"]),
+        k=float(cfg["sauvola_k"]),
+        r=float(cfg["sauvola_r"]),
+    )
+    return np.where(image > threshold, 255, 0).astype(np.uint8)
+
+
+def quality_score(image: np.ndarray, cfg: dict) -> float:
+    """Scan-legibility proxy in [0,1]: normalized variance of the high-pass response.
+
+    Sharp strokes leave high-frequency energy; a blurred, faded, or out-of-focus scan
+    leaves little. Used only to FLAG pages for review -- nothing is dropped on this score,
+    since an illegible page is still evidence a desk officer may need cited.
+    """
+    radius = int(cfg["quality_radius"])
+    ksize = radius * 2 + 1
+    low_pass = cv2.GaussianBlur(image.astype(np.float32), (ksize, ksize), 0)
+    high_pass = image.astype(np.float32) - low_pass
+    return float(min(1.0, np.var(high_pass) / float(cfg["quality_scale"])))
+
 
 def run(pages: list[Page], cfg: dict) -> list[Page]:
-    """Classical preprocessing. IMPLEMENT."""
-    raise NotImplementedError("Stage 1: preprocess")
+    """Greyscale -> deskew -> denoise -> (greyscale primary + Sauvola parallel copy)."""
+    params = cfg["preprocess"]
+    out_dir = Path(cfg["ingest"]["interim_dir"])
+    out_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
+
+    processed: list[Page] = []
+    records: list[dict] = []
+    for page in pages:
+        grey = _read_grey(page.image_path)
+        skew = estimate_skew(grey, params)
+        cleaned = denoise(_rotate(grey, -skew) if skew else grey, params)
+        quality = quality_score(cleaned, params)
+        binary = binarize(cleaned, params)
+
+        grey_path = out_dir / f"{page.id}.png"
+        binary_path = out_dir / f"{page.id}_bin.png"
+        cv2.imwrite(str(grey_path), cleaned)
+        cv2.imwrite(str(binary_path), binary)
+
+        records.append(
+            {
+                "page_id": page.id,
+                "doc_id": page.doc_id,
+                "original_path": page.image_path,
+                "greyscale_path": str(grey_path),
+                "binary_path": str(binary_path),
+                "skew_deg": round(skew, 3),
+                "quality": round(quality, 4),
+                "low_legibility": quality < float(params["low_legibility_thr"]),
+            }
+        )
+        # Greyscale, not the binarization, is what moves downstream: Sauvola can erase
+        # faint seals and matras, so the binary copy stays available but optional.
+        processed.append(Page(id=page.id, image_path=str(grey_path), doc_id=page.doc_id))
+
+    sidecar = out_dir / SIDECAR_NAME
+    with sidecar.open("w", encoding="utf-8") as fh:
+        for record in records:
+            fh.write(json.dumps(record) + "\n")
 
+    flagged = sum(1 for r in records if r["low_legibility"])
+    logger.info(
+        "preprocessed %d pages (%d flagged low-legibility) -> %s", len(records), flagged, out_dir
+    )
+    return processed

+def _write_jpeg(path, size=(64, 64), seed=0):
+    """A JPEG with enough noise that it doesn't compress down to a thumbnail-sized file."""
+    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
+    arr = rng.integers(0, 255, size=(size[1], size[0], 3), dtype=np.uint8)
+    Image.fromarray(arr).save(path, format="JPEG", quality=95)
+    return path

=== DIFF: tests/test_preprocess.py ===
@@ -0,0 +1,159 @@
+"""Stage 1 preprocess — deskew / denoise / binarize / quality. CI runs these."""
+
+from __future__ import annotations
+
+import json
+from pathlib import Path
+
+import cv2
+import numpy as np
+import pytest
+from PIL import Image
+
+from doc_agent.contracts import Page
+from doc_agent.ingest import preprocess
+
+CFG_PREPROCESS = {
+    "skew_range_deg": 4.0,
+    "skew_step_deg": 0.25,
+    "median_ksize": 3,
+    "nlm_h": 7,
+    "sauvola_window": 15,
+    "sauvola_k": 0.2,
+    "sauvola_r": 128,
+    "quality_radius": 3,
+    "quality_scale": 40.0,
+    "low_legibility_thr": 0.35,
+}
+
+
+def _page_array(rotate_deg: float = 0.0, blur: int = 0) -> np.ndarray:
+    """A page-like image: white ground, dark 'text' lines. Rotated/blurred on demand."""
+    # Scan-like, not synthetic-crisp: faded grey ink on aged ground, softened edges.
+    # A hard black-on-white fixture saturates the quality score's [0,1] clip and would
+    # hide the blur ordering the score exists to detect.
+    img = np.full((300, 400), 210, dtype=np.uint8)
+    for y in range(40, 260, 24):
+        img[y : y + 5, 40:360] = 120
+    img = cv2.GaussianBlur(img, (3, 3), 0)
+    if rotate_deg:
+        centre = (img.shape[1] / 2, img.shape[0] / 2)
+        matrix = cv2.getRotationMatrix2D(centre, rotate_deg, 1.0)
+        img = cv2.warpAffine(
+            img,
+            matrix,
+            (img.shape[1], img.shape[0]),
+            borderMode=cv2.BORDER_CONSTANT,
+            borderValue=255,
+        )
+    if blur:
+        img = cv2.GaussianBlur(img, (blur, blur), 0)
+    return img
+
+
+def _write_page(path, **kwargs):
+    Image.fromarray(_page_array(**kwargs)).save(path, format="JPEG", quality=95)
+    return path
+
+
+def _cfg(tmp_path):
+    return {
+        "ingest": {"interim_dir": str(tmp_path / "interim")},
+        "preprocess": dict(CFG_PREPROCESS),
+    }
+
+
+@pytest.fixture()
+def pages(tmp_path):
+    raw = tmp_path / "raw"
+    raw.mkdir()
+    made = []
+    for n, kwargs in enumerate([{}, {"rotate_deg": 3.0}], start=1):
+        path = _write_page(raw / f"dolil_{n}.jpg", **kwargs)
+        made.append(Page(id=f"dolil_{n}", image_path=str(path), doc_id="deed_001"))
+    return made
+
+
+def test_skew_of_a_straight_page_is_near_zero():
+    assert abs(preprocess.estimate_skew(_page_array(), CFG_PREPROCESS)) < 0.5
+
+
+def test_skew_of_a_rotated_page_is_measured():
+    estimated = preprocess.estimate_skew(_page_array(rotate_deg=3.0), CFG_PREPROCESS)
+
+    assert abs(abs(estimated) - 3.0) < 0.6
+
+
+def test_deskew_straightens_a_rotated_page():
+    corrected = preprocess.deskew(_page_array(rotate_deg=3.0), CFG_PREPROCESS)
+
+    assert abs(preprocess.estimate_skew(corrected, CFG_PREPROCESS)) < 0.6
+
+
+def test_blurred_page_scores_lower_quality_than_a_sharp_one():
+    sharp = preprocess.quality_score(_page_array(), CFG_PREPROCESS)
+    blurred = preprocess.quality_score(_page_array(blur=9), CFG_PREPROCESS)
+
+    assert blurred < sharp

+def test_binarize_returns_a_two_valued_image():

+    assert set(np.unique(binary)).issubset({0, 255})
+
+
+def test_run_preserves_page_identity(pages, tmp_path):
+    out = preprocess.run(pages, _cfg(tmp_path))

+    assert [(p.id, p.doc_id) for p in out] == [(p.id, p.doc_id) for p in pages]
+

+def test_run_points_pages_at_the_processed_image_not_the_raw_scan(pages, tmp_path):
+    out = preprocess.run(pages, _cfg(tmp_path))
+
+    originals = {p.id: p.image_path for p in pages}

+        assert page.image_path != originals[page.id]
+        assert Path(page.image_path).parent == tmp_path / "interim"
+        assert Image.open(page.image_path).size

+def test_run_leaves_the_original_scan_untouched(pages, tmp_path):
+    before = {p.id: open(p.image_path, "rb").read() for p in pages}

+    preprocess.run(pages, _cfg(tmp_path))
+
+    assert {p.id: open(p.image_path, "rb").read() for p in pages} == before

+def test_run_writes_a_binarized_copy_alongside(pages, tmp_path):
+    preprocess.run(pages, _cfg(tmp_path))
+
+    records = _sidecar(tmp_path)
+    assert all(Image.open(r["binary_path"]).size for r in records)
+

+def test_run_records_quality_and_legibility_per_page(pages, tmp_path):
+    records = _sidecar(tmp_path, run_first=(pages, _cfg(tmp_path)))
+
+    assert {r["page_id"] for r in records} == {"dolil_1", "dolil_2"}
+    assert all("quality" in r and "low_legibility" in r for r in records)
+
+
+def test_low_legibility_flag_is_set_for_an_illegible_page(tmp_path):
+    raw = tmp_path / "raw"
+    raw.mkdir()

+    page = Page(id="dolil_9", image_path=str(raw / "dolil_9.jpg"), doc_id="deed_009")
+
+    preprocess.run([page], _cfg(tmp_path))
+
+    assert _sidecar(tmp_path)[0]["low_legibility"] is True
+
+
+def _sidecar(tmp_path, run_first=None):
+    if run_first:
+        preprocess.run(*run_first)
+    path = tmp_path / "interim" / "preprocess.jsonl"
+    return [json.loads(line) for line in path.read_text(encoding="utf-8").splitlines() if line]

**Sinks touched by the diff**:
- Filesystem writes: `cv2.imwrite(out_dir / f"{page.id}.png")` and `cv2.imwrite(out_dir / f"{page.id}_bin.png")` in preprocess.py

- Log messages (`logger.info(...)` — only counts, dirs, version IDs, no per-file content)

**Data-flow trace for the one non-trivial sink** — filename-derived output paths:
- `page.id = path.stem` where `path` comes from `raw_dir.iterdir()`. `iterdir()` yields direct children only; POSIX filename components cannot contain `/`, so `path.stem` cannot introduce a separator. `out_dir / f"{page.id}.png"` therefore cannot escape `out_dir` via a traversal. @@ -1,8 +1,117 @@
-"""Stage 2 — layout detection / segmentation"""
+"""Stage 2 — layout detection / segmentation
+
+Training-free projection heuristic (Step 0 decision D1). The corpus is dense handwritten
+deed body text with no tables and no multi-column pages, so a detector's general region
+typing would buy classes this corpus does not contain, at the cost of a checkpoint, a GPU
+pass per page, and another pinned dependency. Ink projected onto the vertical axis is
+enough: handwritten lines produce clear ink bands, and the blank runs between paragraphs
+are wider than the leading within one.
+
+Every region is emitted as kind="text". Heading/table/figure are left unused rather than
+guessed at -- an unconsumed class nobody evaluates is untested surface, and the reader
+handles structure inside a region anyway.
+
+Self-contained by design: this does its own Otsu binarization from whatever image the Page
+points at, so it works on a raw scan or on the ingest stage's preprocessed greyscale.
+
+KNOWN LIMITATION, measured on the 8 held-out scans (correctness check, nothing tuned):
+this corpus largely does not contain the blank horizontal bands the heuristic looks for.
+The 4 flatbed-style pages split into 3-9 regions; the 4 camera-photo pages yield a single
+page-sized region, and no amount of deskewing or adaptive thresholding produced blank rows
+on them (both were tried and measured -- the writing genuinely runs continuously down the
+sheet).
+
+DECIDED: region = page. A page-sized region is the expected output here, not a failure to
+segment -- Qwen2.5-VL (Step 0 decision D2) reads a whole page in one call, so finer
+segmentation would buy nothing and would risk splitting a plot number or deed amount
+across two crops, which the precision-first NFR cannot afford. The banding below is kept
+because it still yields tighter crops on flatbed-style pages, but nothing downstream may
+assume more than one region per page.
+"""
+
 from __future__ import annotations
+
+import cv2
+import numpy as np
+
 from ..contracts import *  # noqa
+from ..logging_conf import get_logger
+
+logger = get_logger(__name__)
+
+
+def _read_grey(image_path: str) -> np.ndarray:
+    grey = cv2.imread(image_path, cv2.IMREAD_GRAYSCALE)
+    if grey is None:
+        raise ValueError(f"unreadable page image: {image_path}")
+    return grey
+
+
+def _ink_mask(grey: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:
+    """Binary ink mask (True = ink), via Otsu so an aged grey ground still separates."""
+    # Otsu picks the threshold per page: scan ground ranges from near-white to heavily
+    # aged grey across this corpus, and a fixed cutoff would erase the darker scans.
+    _, binary = cv2.threshold(grey, 0, 255, cv2.THRESH_BINARY_INV + cv2.THRESH_OTSU)
+    return binary > 0
+
+
+def _bands(ink_rows: np.ndarray, gap: int) -> list[tuple[int, int]]:
+    """Row indices where ink is present -> [start, end) bands, merging gaps below `gap`."""
+    if not ink_rows.any():
+        return []
+
+    rows = np.flatnonzero(ink_rows)
+    bands: list[list[int]] = [[int(rows[0]), int(rows[0]) + 1]]
+    for row in rows[1:]:
+        row = int(row)
+        # A blank run shorter than line_gap_px is the leading between lines of one
+        # paragraph; anything wider is a real block boundary.
+        if row - bands[-1][1] <= gap:
+            bands[-1][1] = row + 1
+        else:
+            bands.append([row, row + 1])
+    return [(start, end) for start, end in bands]
+
 
 def detect(pages: list[Page], cfg: dict) -> list[Region]:
-    """Detect text/table/figure/heading regions. IMPLEMENT."""
-    raise NotImplementedError("Stage 2: layout detection")
+    """Detect text regions on each page. Returns them in page order, top to bottom."""
+    params = cfg["layout"]
+    min_ink_frac = float(params["min_ink_frac"])
+    gap = int(params["line_gap_px"])
+    min_height = int(params["min_height_px"])
+    min_width = int(params["min_width_px"])
+    pad = int(params["pad_px"])
+
+    regions: list[Region] = []
+    for page in pages:
+        mask = _ink_mask(_read_grey(page.image_path))
+        height, width = mask.shape
+
+        # A row counts as text only if enough of it is ink -- this is what keeps dust
+        # specks and scan-edge artefacts from opening a band.
+        row_ink = mask.sum(axis=1) >= max(1, int(min_ink_frac * width))
+
+        for top, bottom in _bands(row_ink, gap):
+            columns = np.flatnonzero(mask[top:bottom].any(axis=0))
+            if columns.size == 0:
+                continue
+            left, right = int(columns[0]), int(columns[-1]) + 1
+
+            if (bottom - top) < min_height or (right - left) < min_width:
+                continue
+
+            regions.append(
+                Region(
+                    page_id=page.id,
+                    bbox=(
+                        max(0, left - pad),
+                        max(0, top - pad),
+                        min(width, right + pad),
+                        min(height, bottom + pad),
+                    ),
+                    kind="text",
+                )
+            )
 
+    logger.info("layout: %d region(s) across %d page(s)", len(regions), len(pages))
+    return regions

+"""Stage 2 layout — projection-heuristic region detection. CI runs these."""

+from __future__ import annotations
+
+import cv2
+import numpy as np
+import pytest
+from PIL import Image
+
+from doc_agent.contracts import Page
+from doc_agent.vision import layout
+
+CFG_LAYOUT = {
+    "model": "projection-heuristic",
+    "score_thr": 0.5,
+    "min_ink_frac": 0.01,
+    "line_gap_px": 18,
+    "min_height_px": 10,
+    "min_width_px": 30,
+    "pad_px": 4,
+}
+
+
+def _blank(height=600, width=400):
+    return np.full((height, width), 235, dtype=np.uint8)
+
+
+def _add_block(img, top, n_lines=3, left=40, right=360, line_h=8, leading=14):
+    """Draw a paragraph: n_lines dark bars separated by `leading` blank rows."""
+    y = top
+    for _ in range(n_lines):
+        img[y : y + line_h, left:right] = 60
+        y += line_h + leading
+    return img
+
+
+def _page(tmp_path, img, page_id="dolil_1"):
+    path = tmp_path / f"{page_id}.jpg"
+    Image.fromarray(img).save(path, format="JPEG", quality=95)
+    return Page(id=page_id, image_path=str(path), doc_id="deed_001")
+

+    img = _blank()
+    for top in (40, 240, 440):
+        _add_block(img, top)
+    return _page(tmp_path, img)
+
+
+def _cfg():
+    return {"layout": dict(CFG_LAYOUT)}
+
+
+def test_paragraphs_separated_by_wide_gaps_become_separate_regions(three_block_page):
+    regions = layout.detect([three_block_page], _cfg())
+
+    assert len(regions) == 3
+
+
+def test_lines_within_a_paragraph_stay_in_one_region(tmp_path):

+
+
+def test_regions_are_ordered_top_to_bottom(three_block_page):
+    tops = [r.bbox[1] for r in layout.detect([three_block_page], _cfg())]
+
+    assert tops == sorted(tops)
+
+
+def test_region_bbox_covers_the_ink_and_excludes_the_margins(tmp_path):

+    img = np.clip(img.astype(np.int16) - gradient[None, :], 0, 255).astype(np.uint8)
+    _add_block(img, 100, n_lines=3)
+    img = cv2.GaussianBlur(img, (3, 3), 0)
+
+    assert len(layout.detect([_page(tmp_path, img)], _cfg())) == 1

CLAUDE:
Based on my analysis of the changed files:

**Data flow trace:**
- `layout.detect()` receives `pages: list[Page]` from `pipeline.py:build_knowledge_base` → the pages come from `loader.load_pages(cfg)`, i.e., local trusted files pointed at by config.
- `page.image_path` flows into `cv2.imread()` (file-read sink) and into a `ValueError` message.
- Config values `min_ink_frac`, `line_gap_px`, `min_height_px`, `min_width_px`, `pad_px` are coerced via `float()`/`int()` — no eval or injection risk. All bbox math is clamped with `max(0, …)` / `min(width, …)`.

**Considered and dismissed:**
- **Path traversal / arbitrary file read via `page.image_path`**: `cv2.imread` only decodes image formats; more importantly, `image_path` originates from the local loader/validator (`validate.py` checks `Path(p.image_path).is_file()`), not from any HTTP/RPC/CLI entry point in this diff. No new attacker-controlled source is introduced here.
- **Decompression-bomb DoS via `cv2.imread`**: possible in principle, but this is a batch pipeline processing local trusted corpus files (handwritten deeds); no attacker-controlled image ingestion is introduced by this diff.
- **Path leaked in `ValueError(f"unreadable page image: {image_path}")`**: goes to an internal exception, not surfaced to an external principal. Local filesystem paths for the operator's own corpus are not sensitive here.
- **Logging** (`logger.info("layout: %d region(s) across %d page(s)", …)`): only aggregate counts, no PII/content.
- **Config parameter tampering**: config is authored by the operator; no external write path is added in this diff.
- **Sibling-path gate parity, allowlist changes, IaC omissions, CI/CD trust, over-broad grants, stale identity mappings, fail-open state drift, security-registry fanout, gate/action mismatches, parser differentials**: none applicable — the diff is purely a numeric image-projection routine plus tests.
- **Resource-bound placement**: `_bands` iteration is bounded by image height; no attacker-controlled loop bound was introduced.
- **Tests file**: pure fixtures/assertions; no security surface.

No concrete source→sink→missing-mitigation path was found that is introduced by this change.

=== DIFF: scripts/transcribe_heldout.py ===
@@ -0,0 +1,162 @@
+#!/usr/bin/env python3
+"""Fill grading_kit/labels.jsonl by hand, one held-out page at a time.
+
+These are GOLD labels: eval/metrics.py:ocr_f1 scores the Stage 3 reader against them, so
+they have to be written by someone who reads Bangla. A model-written transcription would
+make the metric measure agreement-with-a-model instead of correctness, and would do it
+invisibly -- if the reader and the labeller share a failure mode, the score comes out high.
+
+Usage:
+    python scripts/transcribe_heldout.py            # work through unfilled pages
+    python scripts/transcribe_heldout.py --page dolil_20   # just one page
+    python scripts/transcribe_heldout.py --show     # print progress and exit
+
+Resumable: pages that already have text are skipped unless you name them with --page.
+Each page is saved as soon as you finish it, so stopping half way loses nothing.
+"""
+
+from __future__ import annotations
+
+import argparse
+import json
+import subprocess
+import sys
+from pathlib import Path
+
+LABELS = Path("grading_kit/labels.jsonl")
+PAGES_DIR = Path("grading_kit/heldout_pages")
+
+FIELD_PROMPTS = {
+    "deed_serial": "Registry serial (as printed, or blank if illegible)",
+    "deed_date": "Deed date as written",
+    "land_amount": "Land amount / consideration",
+    "party_names": "Party names, comma-separated",
+}
+
+
+def load_records() -> list[dict]:
+    return [
+        json.loads(line)
+        for line in LABELS.read_text(encoding="utf-8").splitlines()
+        if line.strip()
+    ]
+
+
+def save_records(records: list[dict]) -> None:
+    LABELS.write_text(
+        "\n".join(json.dumps(r, ensure_ascii=False) for r in records) + "\n",
+        encoding="utf-8",
+    )
+
+
+def open_image(page_id: str) -> None:
+    """Best-effort: open the scan in the desktop viewer, else just print the path."""
+    path = PAGES_DIR / f"{page_id}.jpg"
+    print(f"\n  image: {path.resolve()}")
+    for opener in ("xdg-open", "open"):
+        try:
+            subprocess.Popen(
+                [opener, str(path)], stdout=subprocess.DEVNULL, stderr=subprocess.DEVNULL
+            )
+            return
+        except FileNotFoundError:
+            continue
+    print("  (no image viewer found -- open the path above manually)")
+
+
+def read_multiline(prompt: str) -> str:
+    """Read until a line containing only '.' -- Bangla text is pasted, not typed blind."""
+    print(f"\n{prompt}")
+    print("  Paste or type the transcription. End with a single '.' on its own line.")
+    print("  Use [?] for anything illegible. Leave empty and enter '.' to skip this page.")
+    lines: list[str] = []
+    while True:
+        try:
+            line = input()
+        except EOFError:
+            break
+        if line.strip() == ".":
+            break
+        lines.append(line)
+    return "\n".join(lines).strip()
+
+
+def transcribe(record: dict) -> bool:
+    """Fill one record in place. Returns True if anything was entered."""
+    page_id = record["page_id"]
+    print("\n" + "=" * 72)
+    print(f"PAGE {page_id}")
+    if record.get("notes"):
+        print(f"\n  existing notes: {record['notes'][:300]}...")
+    open_image(page_id)
+
+    text = read_multiline("Full page transcription (reading order, line breaks preserved):")
+    if not text:
+        print("  skipped.")
+        return False
+    record["text"] = text
+
+    print("\nNow the critical fields (Enter to leave blank):")
+    for key, prompt in FIELD_PROMPTS.items():
+        value = input(f"  {prompt}: ").strip()
+        if not value:
+            continue
+        record.setdefault("fields", {})[key] = (
+            [v.strip() for v in value.split(",") if v.strip()]
+            if key == "party_names"
+            else value
+        )
+
+    record["notes"] = (record.get("notes", "") + " | HUMAN-TRANSCRIBED").lstrip(" |")
+    return True
+
+
+def main() -> int:
+    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=__doc__)
+    parser.add_argument("--page", help="transcribe only this page id, even if already filled")
+    parser.add_argument("--show", action="store_true", help="print progress and exit")
+    args = parser.parse_args()
+
+    if not LABELS.is_file():
+        print(f"{LABELS} not found -- run from the repo root.", file=sys.stderr)
+        return 1
+
+    records = load_records()
+
+    if args.show:
+        done = sum(1 for r in records if r.get("text"))
+        print(f"{done}/{len(records)} pages transcribed")
+        for r in records:
+            mark = "x" if r.get("text") else " "
+            filled = sum(1 for v in r.get("fields", {}).values() if v)
+            print(f"  [{mark}] {r['page_id']:10} fields {filled}/{len(FIELD_PROMPTS)}")
+        return 0
+
+    if args.page:
+        targets = [r for r in records if r["page_id"] == args.page]
+        if not targets:
+            print(f"no held-out page called {args.page!r}", file=sys.stderr)
+            return 1
+    else:
+        targets = [r for r in records if not r.get("text")]
+    if not targets:
+        print("Nothing to do -- every page already has a transcription.")
+        return 0
+
+    print(f"{len(targets)} page(s) to transcribe. Ctrl-C to stop; progress is saved as you go.")
+    for record in targets:
+        try:
+            if transcribe(record):
+                save_records(records)
+                print(f"  saved {record['page_id']}")
+        except KeyboardInterrupt:
+            print("\nStopped. Progress saved.")
+            break
+
+    done = sum(1 for r in records if r.get("text"))
+    print(f"\n{done}/{len(records)} pages now transcribed.")
+    return 0
+
+
+if __name__ == "__main__":
+    raise SystemExit(main())

+    "- Preserve numerals exactly as written, including Bangla digits.\n"
+    "- Keep the reading order and line breaks of the page.\n"

=== DIFF: src/doc_agent/vision/ocr.py ===
@@ -1,15 +1,216 @@
-"""Stage 3 — OCR/HTR (BASELINE = pretrained foundation, fine-tuned)"""
+"""Stage 3 — OCR/HTR (BASELINE = pretrained foundation, fine-tuned)
+
+Single-pass VLM reading (Step 0 decision D2): one Qwen2.5-VL call per region, no
+GraDeT-HTR stage and no confidence-routed fallback. A two-stage reader needs a calibrated
+confidence signal to route on, and there is no OCR accuracy measurement yet -- the held-out
+labels are unfilled -- so the fallback branch would be tuned against nothing. Revisit once
+eval/metrics.py:ocr_f1 scores the held-out pages.
+
+One Chunk per PAGE, not per region: Stage 4's legal-semantic chunker cuts on deed and
+paragraph boundaries, and it cannot find a boundary that spans two regions if this stage
+has already cut the text up. Regions are concatenated in the reading order layout emitted.
+
+A Region carries only page_id and bbox, but a Chunk needs doc_id and the reader needs the
+image. Both come from the page index below, which prefers the ingest sidecar and otherwise
+reads the deed grouping directly. It refuses to invent a doc_id unless explicitly allowed:
+doc_id is what keeps the train/val/test split document-level, so a silent page-as-document
+fallback would manufacture exactly the leak the corpus policy exists to prevent.
+"""
+
 from __future__ import annotations
+
+import csv
+import json
+import unicodedata
+from pathlib import Path
+
+from PIL import Image, ImageOps
+
 from ..contracts import *  # noqa
+from ..llm.prompts import TRANSCRIBE
+from ..logging_conf import get_logger
+
+logger = get_logger(__name__)
+
+# A1 commits to mapping Bangla numerals to ASCII so that "২১৬৩" and "2163" compare equal
+# under Exact Match -- a plot or deed number must match to the character.
+_BANGLA_DIGITS = str.maketrans("০১২৩৪৫৬৭৮৯", "0123456789")
+
+_IMAGE_SUFFIXES = (".png", ".jpg", ".jpeg")
+
+
+def normalize(text: str, ascii_digits: bool = True) -> str:
+    """Unicode NFC + optional Bangla->ASCII digits, per the A1 normalization policy."""
+    out = unicodedata.normalize("NFC", text).strip()
+    return out.translate(_BANGLA_DIGITS) if ascii_digits else out
+
+
+def load_page_index(cfg: dict) -> dict[str, dict[str, str]]:
+    """page_id -> {image_path, doc_id}, from the ingest sidecar or the raw dir + grouping."""
+    params = cfg["ocr"]
+    sidecar = Path(params["sidecar"])
+
+    if sidecar.is_file():
+        index: dict[str, dict[str, str]] = {}
+        for line in sidecar.read_text(encoding="utf-8").splitlines():
+            if not line.strip():
+                continue
+            record = json.loads(line)
+            # Prefer the deskewed greyscale: it is what Stage 1 hands downstream, and the
+            # binarization can drop faint seals and matras the reader still needs.
+            image = record.get("greyscale_path") or record["original_path"]
+            index[record["page_id"]] = {"image_path": image, "doc_id": record["doc_id"]}
+        return index
+
+    pages_dir = Path(params["pages_dir"])
+    if not pages_dir.is_dir():
+        raise FileNotFoundError(
+            f"no ingest sidecar at {sidecar} and no page directory at {pages_dir}"
+        )
+
+    groups = _load_deed_groups(params)
+    index = {}
+    for path in sorted(pages_dir.iterdir()):
+        if not path.is_file() or path.suffix.lower() not in _IMAGE_SUFFIXES:
+            continue
+        page_id = path.stem
+        doc_id = groups[page_id] if groups is not None else page_id
+        index[page_id] = {"image_path": str(path), "doc_id": doc_id}
+    return index
+
+
+def _load_deed_groups(params: dict) -> dict[str, str] | None:
+    """The page->deed map, or None when running standalone with page-as-document allowed."""
+    groups_path = Path(params["deed_groups"])
+    if not groups_path.is_file():
+        if params.get("allow_page_as_doc"):
+            logger.warning(
+                "no deed grouping at %s -- treating every page as its own document. This "
+                "makes the split leak by construction; standalone development only.",
+                groups_path,
+            )
+            return None
+        raise FileNotFoundError(
+            f"no ingest sidecar and no deed_groups file at {groups_path}. Stage 3 cannot "
+            "invent doc_id: it is what keeps the train/val/test split document-level. Run "
+            "ingest first, or set ocr.allow_page_as_doc for standalone development."
+        )
+
+    with groups_path.open(encoding="utf-8", newline="") as fh:
+        return {row["page_id"]: row["doc_id"] for row in csv.DictReader(fh)}
+
 
 class Reader:
-    """Model set by cfg['ocr']. Baseline: pretrained TrOCR/Donut/Tesseract."""
+    """Model set by cfg['ocr']. Baseline: pretrained Qwen2.5-VL, one call per region."""
+
     def __init__(self, cfg: dict) -> None:
         self.cfg = cfg["ocr"]
+        self.pages = load_page_index(cfg)
+        self._model = None
+        self._processor = None
+
+    def crop(self, region: Region) -> Image.Image:
+        """The region's pixels, cut from its page image, in display orientation.
+
+        exif_transpose is required, not cosmetic: some of this corpus is phone photos
+        carrying an EXIF orientation tag. cv2.imread (used by Stage 2 layout) applies that
+        tag, so region bboxes are in display coordinates, while PIL.Image.open does not.
+        Without this the reader would silently be handed the wrong part of a rotated page.
+        """
+        page = self.pages.get(region.page_id)
+        if page is None:
+            raise ValueError(f"no image known for page {region.page_id!r}")
+        with Image.open(page["image_path"]) as image:
+            upright = ImageOps.exif_transpose(image)
+            return upright.convert("RGB").crop(region.bbox)
+
+    def _load(self) -> None:
+        """Load the VLM on first use, so importing this module stays cheap and CPU-safe."""
+        if self._model is not None:
+            return
+        from transformers import AutoModelForVision2Seq, AutoProcessor  # heavy, load late
+
+        name = self.cfg["model"]
+        logger.info("loading reader %s", name)
+        self._processor = AutoProcessor.from_pretrained(name)
+        self._model = AutoModelForVision2Seq.from_pretrained(name, device_map="auto")
+
     def transcribe_region(self, region: Region) -> str:
-        raise NotImplementedError("Stage 3: transcribe a region")
+        """Read one region with a single VLM call. Transcription only -- see prompts.TRANSCRIBE."""
+        self._load()
+        image = self.crop(region)
+        messages = [
+            {
+                "role": "user",
+                "content": [{"type": "image"}, {"type": "text", "text": TRANSCRIBE}],
+            }
+        ]
+        prompt = self._processor.apply_chat_template(
+            messages, tokenize=False, add_generation_prompt=True
+        )
+        inputs = self._processor(text=[prompt], images=[image], return_tensors="pt").to(
+            self._model.device
+        )
+        generated = self._model.generate(
+            **inputs, max_new_tokens=int(self.cfg["max_new_tokens"]), do_sample=False
+        )
+        trimmed = generated[0][inputs["input_ids"].shape[1] :]
+        return self._processor.decode(trimmed, skip_special_tokens=True)
+
+
+def transcribe(regions: list[Region], cfg: dict, reader: object | None = None) -> list[Chunk]:
+    """Regions -> one Chunk per page, in the order layout emitted them.
+
+    `reader` is injectable so the assembly around the model can be tested without loading
+    a 7B VLM; production callers (pipeline.py) leave it None.
+    """
+    if not regions:
+        return []
+
+    params = cfg["ocr"]
+    ascii_digits = bool(params["ascii_digits"])
+    if reader is None:
+        reader = Reader(cfg)
+        pages = reader.pages  # already built; don't read the sidecar twice
+    else:
+        pages = load_page_index(cfg)
+
+    ordered_pages: list[str] = []
+    by_page: dict[str, list[Region]] = {}
+    for region in regions:
+        if region.page_id not in by_page:
+            by_page[region.page_id] = []
+            ordered_pages.append(region.page_id)
+        by_page[region.page_id].append(region)
+
+    unknown = [page_id for page_id in ordered_pages if page_id not in pages]
+    if unknown:
+        raise ValueError(
+            f"{len(unknown)} region page(s) missing from the page index, e.g. {unknown[:5]}"
+        )
+
+    chunks: list[Chunk] = []
+    for page_id in ordered_pages:
+        pieces = []
+        for region in by_page[page_id]:
+            text = normalize(reader.transcribe_region(region), ascii_digits)
+            if text:
+                pieces.append(text)
+
+        if not pieces:
+            # An entirely unreadable page yields no chunk rather than an empty one: an
+            # empty chunk would be indexed and could be retrieved as evidence for nothing.
+            logger.warning("page %s produced no readable text", page_id)
+            continue

-    raise NotImplementedError("Stage 3: OCR to chunks")
+        chunks.append(
+            Chunk(
+                id=f"{page_id}#p",
+                doc_id=pages[page_id]["doc_id"],
+                text="\n".join(pieces),
+                page_ids=[page_id],
+            )
+        )
 
+    logger.info("read %d page(s) into %d chunk(s)", len(ordered_pages), len(chunks))
+    return chunks

     img = cv2.GaussianBlur(img, (3, 3), 0)
 
     assert len(layout.detect([_page(tmp_path, img)], _cfg())) == 1

+def _exif_rotated_page(tmp_path, page_id="dolil_212"):
+    """A page stored sideways with EXIF orientation=6, i.e. 'rotate 90 CW to display'.
+
+    Two of the eight held-out scans are stored exactly like this. Viewers honour the tag,
+    so the page looks upright to a human; cv2.imread ignores it, so the pipeline would see
+    vertical text lines and find no blank rows at all.
+    """
+    upright = _blank(height=600, width=400)
+    for top in (40, 240, 440):
+        _add_block(upright, top)
+    sideways = np.rot90(upright)  # what is actually stored in the file
+    image = Image.fromarray(sideways)
+    exif = image.getexif()
+    exif[274] = 6
+    path = tmp_path / f"{page_id}.jpg"
+    image.save(path, format="JPEG", quality=95, exif=exif)
+    return Page(id=page_id, image_path=str(path), doc_id="deed_001")
+
+
+def test_exif_orientation_is_honoured_when_reading_a_page(tmp_path):
+    page = _exif_rotated_page(tmp_path)
+
+    assert len(layout.detect([page], _cfg())) == 3
+
+
+def test_exif_rotated_bboxes_are_in_display_coordinates(tmp_path):
+    page = _exif_rotated_page(tmp_path)
+
+    for region in layout.detect([page], _cfg()):
+        assert region.bbox[2] <= 400  # page is 400 wide once displayed upright
+        assert region.bbox[3] <= 600

+def _exif_rotated_page(tmp_path, page_id="dolil_212"):
+    """Stored sideways with EXIF orientation=6, as two of the held-out scans are.
+
+    cv2.imread (layout) honours the tag, so region bboxes are in DISPLAY coordinates.
+    PIL.Image.open does not, so a naive crop reads the raw raster and returns the wrong
+    pixels -- the reader would be handed the wrong part of the page, silently.
+    """
+    display = np.full((600, 400), 240, dtype=np.uint8)
+    display[0:120, 0:100] = 20  # a dark marker at the display-space top-left corner
+    stored = np.rot90(display)
+    image = Image.fromarray(stored)
+    exif = image.getexif()
+    exif[274] = 6
+    raw = tmp_path / "raw"
+    raw.mkdir(exist_ok=True)
+    path = raw / f"{page_id}.jpg"
+    image.save(path, format="JPEG", quality=95, exif=exif)
+    (tmp_path / "preprocess.jsonl").write_text(
+        json.dumps({"page_id": page_id, "doc_id": "deed_009", "greyscale_path": str(path)}) + "\n",
+        encoding="utf-8",
+    )
+    return path
+

**Trust boundary**: This is a local batch OCR/HTR pipeline (Bangla land deed reader). There are no HTTP handlers, RPC endpoints, message consumers, or webhook receivers. Inputs are:

- `configs/*.yaml`, `grading_kit/manifest.yaml`: Data-only edits (setting team id, adding OCR config keys). No code paths.

- `tests/*`: Test-only code.

=== DIFF: scripts/transcribe_heldout.py ===
@@ -0,0 +1,162 @@
+#!/usr/bin/env python3
+"""Fill grading_kit/labels.jsonl by hand, one held-out page at a time.
+
+These are GOLD labels: eval/metrics.py:ocr_f1 scores the Stage 3 reader against them, so
+they have to be written by someone who reads Bangla. A model-written transcription would
+make the metric measure agreement-with-a-model instead of correctness, and would do it
+invisibly -- if the reader and the labeller share a failure mode, the score comes out high.
+
+Usage:
+    python scripts/transcribe_heldout.py            # work through unfilled pages
+    python scripts/transcribe_heldout.py --page dolil_20   # just one page
+    python scripts/transcribe_heldout.py --show     # print progress and exit
+
+Resumable: pages that already have text are skipped unless you name them with --page.
+Each page is saved as soon as you finish it, so stopping half way loses nothing.
+"""
+
+from __future__ import annotations
+
+import argparse
+import json
+import subprocess
+import sys
+from pathlib import Path
+
+LABELS = Path("grading_kit/labels.jsonl")
+PAGES_DIR = Path("grading_kit/heldout_pages")
+
+FIELD_PROMPTS = {
+    "deed_serial": "Registry serial (as printed, or blank if illegible)",
+    "deed_date": "Deed date as written",
+    "land_amount": "Land amount / consideration",
+    "party_names": "Party names, comma-separated",
+}
+
+
+def load_records() -> list[dict]:
+    return [
+        json.loads(line)
+        for line in LABELS.read_text(encoding="utf-8").splitlines()
+        if line.strip()
+    ]
+
+
+def save_records(records: list[dict]) -> None:
+    LABELS.write_text(
+        "\n".join(json.dumps(r, ensure_ascii=False) for r in records) + "\n",
+        encoding="utf-8",
+    )
+
+
+def open_image(page_id: str) -> None:
+    """Best-effort: open the scan in the desktop viewer, else just print the path."""
+    path = PAGES_DIR / f"{page_id}.jpg"
+    print(f"\n  image: {path.resolve()}")
+    for opener in ("xdg-open", "open"):
+        try:
+            subprocess.Popen(
+                [opener, str(path)], stdout=subprocess.DEVNULL, stderr=subprocess.DEVNULL
+            )
+            return
+        except FileNotFoundError:
+            continue
+    print("  (no image viewer found -- open the path above manually)")
+
+
+def read_multiline(prompt: str) -> str:
+    """Read until a line containing only '.' -- Bangla text is pasted, not typed blind."""
+    print(f"\n{prompt}")
+    print("  Paste or type the transcription. End with a single '.' on its own line.")
+    print("  Use [?] for anything illegible. Leave empty and enter '.' to skip this page.")
+    lines: list[str] = []
+    while True:
+        try:
+            line = input()
+        except EOFError:
+            break
+        if line.strip() == ".":
+            break
+        lines.append(line)
+    return "\n".join(lines).strip()
+
+
+def transcribe(record: dict) -> bool:
+    """Fill one record in place. Returns True if anything was entered."""
+    page_id = record["page_id"]
+    print("\n" + "=" * 72)
+    print(f"PAGE {page_id}")
+    if record.get("notes"):
+        print(f"\n  existing notes: {record['notes'][:300]}...")
+    open_image(page_id)
+
+    text = read_multiline("Full page transcription (reading order, line breaks preserved):")
+    if not text:
+        print("  skipped.")
+        return False
+    record["text"] = text
+
+    print("\nNow the critical fields (Enter to leave blank):")
+    for key, prompt in FIELD_PROMPTS.items():
+        value = input(f"  {prompt}: ").strip()
+        if not value:
+            continue
+        record.setdefault("fields", {})[key] = (
+            [v.strip() for v in value.split(",") if v.strip()]
+            if key == "party_names"
+            else value
+        )
+
+    record["notes"] = (record.get("notes", "") + " | HUMAN-TRANSCRIBED").lstrip(" |")
+    return True
+
+
+def main() -> int:
+    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=__doc__)
+    parser.add_argument("--page", help="transcribe only this page id, even if already filled")
+    parser.add_argument("--show", action="store_true", help="print progress and exit")
+    args = parser.parse_args()
+
+    if not LABELS.is_file():
+        print(f"{LABELS} not found -- run from the repo root.", file=sys.stderr)
+        return 1
+
+    records = load_records()
+
+    if args.show:
+        done = sum(1 for r in records if r.get("text"))
+        print(f"{done}/{len(records)} pages transcribed")
+        for r in records:
+            mark = "x" if r.get("text") else " "
+            filled = sum(1 for v in r.get("fields", {}).values() if v)
+            print(f"  [{mark}] {r['page_id']:10} fields {filled}/{len(FIELD_PROMPTS)}")
+        return 0
+
+    if args.page:
+        targets = [r for r in records if r["page_id"] == args.page]
+        if not targets:
+            print(f"no held-out page called {args.page!r}", file=sys.stderr)
+            return 1
+    else:
+        targets = [r for r in records if not r.get("text")]
+    if not targets:
+        print("Nothing to do -- every page already has a transcription.")
+        return 0
+
+    print(f"{len(targets)} page(s) to transcribe. Ctrl-C to stop; progress is saved as you go.")
+    for record in targets:
+        try:
+            if transcribe(record):
+                save_records(records)
+                print(f"  saved {record['page_id']}")
+        except KeyboardInterrupt:
+            print("\nStopped. Progress saved.")
+            break
+
+    done = sum(1 for r in records if r.get("text"))
+    print(f"\n{done}/{len(records)} pages now transcribed.")
+    return 0
+
+
+if __name__ == "__main__":
+    raise SystemExit(main())

+# Stage 3 reading prompt. Transcription only -- the reader must never summarise, translate,
+# or "correct" a legal field, and must mark what it cannot read rather than guessing at it,
+# because a confidently invented plot number is the precision-first NFR's worst failure.
+TRANSCRIBE = (
+    "Transcribe every character of handwritten and printed text in this image of a Bangla "
+    "land deed (dolil), exactly as written.\n"
+    "Rules:\n"
+    "- Preserve the original script: Bangla stays Bangla, English stays English.\n"
+    "- Preserve numerals exactly as written, including Bangla digits.\n"
+    "- Keep the reading order and line breaks of the page.\n"
+    "- Do NOT translate, summarise, correct spelling, or expand abbreviations.\n"
+    "- If a word or digit is illegible, write [?] in its place. Never guess.\n"
+    "Output only the transcription."

-"""Stage 3 — OCR/HTR (BASELINE = pretrained foundation, fine-tuned)"""
+"""Stage 3 — OCR/HTR (BASELINE = pretrained foundation, fine-tuned)
+
+Single-pass VLM reading (Step 0 decision D2): one Qwen2.5-VL call per region, no
+GraDeT-HTR stage and no confidence-routed fallback. A two-stage reader needs a calibrated
+confidence signal to route on, and there is no OCR accuracy measurement yet -- the held-out
+labels are unfilled -- so the fallback branch would be tuned against nothing. Revisit once
+eval/metrics.py:ocr_f1 scores the held-out pages.
+
+One Chunk per PAGE, not per region: Stage 4's legal-semantic chunker cuts on deed and
+paragraph boundaries, and it cannot find a boundary that spans two regions if this stage
+has already cut the text up. Regions are concatenated in the reading order layout emitted.
+
+A Region carries only page_id and bbox, but a Chunk needs doc_id and the reader needs the
+image. Both come from the page index below, which prefers the ingest sidecar and otherwise
+reads the deed grouping directly. It refuses to invent a doc_id unless explicitly allowed:
+doc_id is what keeps the train/val/test split document-level, so a silent page-as-document
+fallback would manufacture exactly the leak the corpus policy exists to prevent.
+"""
+
 from __future__ import annotations
+
+import csv
+import json
+import unicodedata
+from pathlib import Path
+
+from PIL import Image, ImageOps
+
 from ..contracts import *  # noqa
+from ..llm.prompts import TRANSCRIBE
+from ..logging_conf import get_logger
+
+logger = get_logger(__name__)
+
+# A1 commits to mapping Bangla numerals to ASCII so that "২১৬৩" and "2163" compare equal
+# under Exact Match -- a plot or deed number must match to the character.
+_BANGLA_DIGITS = str.maketrans("০১২৩৪৫৬৭৮৯", "0123456789")
+
+_IMAGE_SUFFIXES = (".png", ".jpg", ".jpeg")
+
+
+def normalize(text: str, ascii_digits: bool = True) -> str:
+    """Unicode NFC + optional Bangla->ASCII digits, per the A1 normalization policy."""
+    out = unicodedata.normalize("NFC", text).strip()
+    return out.translate(_BANGLA_DIGITS) if ascii_digits else out
+
+
+def load_page_index(cfg: dict) -> dict[str, dict[str, str]]:
+    """page_id -> {image_path, doc_id}, from the ingest sidecar or the raw dir + grouping."""
+    params = cfg["ocr"]
+    sidecar = Path(params["sidecar"])
+
+    if sidecar.is_file():
+        index: dict[str, dict[str, str]] = {}
+        for line in sidecar.read_text(encoding="utf-8").splitlines():
+            if not line.strip():
+                continue
+            record = json.loads(line)
+            # Prefer the deskewed greyscale: it is what Stage 1 hands downstream, and the
+            # binarization can drop faint seals and matras the reader still needs.
+            image = record.get("greyscale_path") or record["original_path"]
+            index[record["page_id"]] = {"image_path": image, "doc_id": record["doc_id"]}
+        return index
+
+    pages_dir = Path(params["pages_dir"])
+    if not pages_dir.is_dir():
+        raise FileNotFoundError(
+            f"no ingest sidecar at {sidecar} and no page directory at {pages_dir}"
+        )
+
+    groups = _load_deed_groups(params)
+    index = {}
+    for path in sorted(pages_dir.iterdir()):
+        if not path.is_file() or path.suffix.lower() not in _IMAGE_SUFFIXES:
+            continue
+        page_id = path.stem
+        doc_id = groups[page_id] if groups is not None else page_id
+        index[page_id] = {"image_path": str(path), "doc_id": doc_id}
+    return index
+
+
+def _load_deed_groups(params: dict) -> dict[str, str] | None:
+    """The page->deed map, or None when running standalone with page-as-document allowed."""
+    groups_path = Path(params["deed_groups"])
+    if not groups_path.is_file():
+        if params.get("allow_page_as_doc"):
+            logger.warning(
+                "no deed grouping at %s -- treating every page as its own document. This "
+                "makes the split leak by construction; standalone development only.",
+                groups_path,
+            )
+            return None
+        raise FileNotFoundError(
+            f"no ingest sidecar and no deed_groups file at {groups_path}. Stage 3 cannot "
+            "invent doc_id: it is what keeps the train/val/test split document-level. Run "
+            "ingest first, or set ocr.allow_page_as_doc for standalone development."
+        )
+
+    with groups_path.open(encoding="utf-8", newline="") as fh:
+        return {row["page_id"]: row["doc_id"] for row in csv.DictReader(fh)}
+

+    """Model set by cfg['ocr']. Baseline: pretrained Qwen2.5-VL, one call per region."""

     def __init__(self, cfg: dict) -> None:
         self.cfg = cfg["ocr"]
+        self.pages = load_page_index(cfg)
+        self._model = None
+        self._processor = None
+
+    def crop(self, region: Region) -> Image.Image:
+        """The region's pixels, cut from its page image, in display orientation.
+
+        exif_transpose is required, not cosmetic: some of this corpus is phone photos
+        carrying an EXIF orientation tag. cv2.imread (used by Stage 2 layout) applies that
+        tag, so region bboxes are in display coordinates, while PIL.Image.open does not.
+        Without this the reader would silently be handed the wrong part of a rotated page.
+        """
+        page = self.pages.get(region.page_id)
+        if page is None:
+            raise ValueError(f"no image known for page {region.page_id!r}")
+        with Image.open(page["image_path"]) as image:
+            upright = ImageOps.exif_transpose(image)
+            return upright.convert("RGB").crop(region.bbox)
+
+    def _load(self) -> None:
+        """Load the VLM on first use, so importing this module stays cheap and CPU-safe."""
+        if self._model is not None:
+            return
+        from transformers import AutoModelForVision2Seq, AutoProcessor  # heavy, load late
+
+        name = self.cfg["model"]
+        logger.info("loading reader %s", name)
+        self._processor = AutoProcessor.from_pretrained(name)
+        self._model = AutoModelForVision2Seq.from_pretrained(name, device_map="auto")
+
     def transcribe_region(self, region: Region) -> str:
-        raise NotImplementedError("Stage 3: transcribe a region")
+        """Read one region with a single VLM call. Transcription only -- see prompts.TRANSCRIBE."""
+        self._load()
+        image = self.crop(region)
+        messages = [
+            {
+                "role": "user",
+                "content": [{"type": "image"}, {"type": "text", "text": TRANSCRIBE}],
+            }
+        ]
+        prompt = self._processor.apply_chat_template(
+            messages, tokenize=False, add_generation_prompt=True
+        )
+        inputs = self._processor(text=[prompt], images=[image], return_tensors="pt").to(
+            self._model.device
+        )
+        generated = self._model.generate(
+            **inputs, max_new_tokens=int(self.cfg["max_new_tokens"]), do_sample=False
+        )
+        trimmed = generated[0][inputs["input_ids"].shape[1] :]
+        return self._processor.decode(trimmed, skip_special_tokens=True)
+

+def transcribe(regions: list[Region], cfg: dict, reader: object | None = None) -> list[Chunk]:
+    """Regions -> one Chunk per page, in the order layout emitted them.
+
+    `reader` is injectable so the assembly around the model can be tested without loading
+    a 7B VLM; production callers (pipeline.py) leave it None.
+    """
+    if not regions:
+        return []
+
+    params = cfg["ocr"]
+    ascii_digits = bool(params["ascii_digits"])
+    if reader is None:
+        reader = Reader(cfg)
+        pages = reader.pages  # already built; don't read the sidecar twice
+    else:
+        pages = load_page_index(cfg)
+
+    ordered_pages: list[str] = []
+    by_page: dict[str, list[Region]] = {}
+    for region in regions:
+        if region.page_id not in by_page:
+            by_page[region.page_id] = []
+            ordered_pages.append(region.page_id)
+        by_page[region.page_id].append(region)
+
+    unknown = [page_id for page_id in ordered_pages if page_id not in pages]
+    if unknown:
+        raise ValueError(
+            f"{len(unknown)} region page(s) missing from the page index, e.g. {unknown[:5]}"
+        )
+
+    chunks: list[Chunk] = []
+    for page_id in ordered_pages:
+        pieces = []
+        for region in by_page[page_id]:
+            text = normalize(reader.transcribe_region(region), ascii_digits)
+            if text:
+                pieces.append(text)
+
+        if not pieces:
+            # An entirely unreadable page yields no chunk rather than an empty one: an
+            # empty chunk would be indexed and could be retrieved as evidence for nothing.
+            logger.warning("page %s produced no readable text", page_id)
+            continue

+            Chunk(
+                id=f"{page_id}#p",
+                doc_id=pages[page_id]["doc_id"],
+                text="\n".join(pieces),
+                page_ids=[page_id],
+            )
+        )
 
+    logger.info("read %d page(s) into %d chunk(s)", len(ordered_pages), len(chunks))
+    return chunks

+def _exif_rotated_page(tmp_path, page_id="dolil_212"):
+    """A page stored sideways with EXIF orientation=6, i.e. 'rotate 90 CW to display'.
+
+    Two of the eight held-out scans are stored exactly like this. Viewers honour the tag,
+    so the page looks upright to a human; cv2.imread ignores it, so the pipeline would see
+    vertical text lines and find no blank rows at all.
+    """
+    upright = _blank(height=600, width=400)
+    for top in (40, 240, 440):
+        _add_block(upright, top)
+    sideways = np.rot90(upright)  # what is actually stored in the file
+    image = Image.fromarray(sideways)
+    exif = image.getexif()
+    exif[274] = 6
+    path = tmp_path / f"{page_id}.jpg"
+    image.save(path, format="JPEG", quality=95, exif=exif)
+    return Page(id=page_id, image_path=str(path), doc_id="deed_001")
+
+
+def test_exif_orientation_is_honoured_when_reading_a_page(tmp_path):
+    page = _exif_rotated_page(tmp_path)
+
+    assert len(layout.detect([page], _cfg())) == 3
+
+
+def test_exif_rotated_bboxes_are_in_display_coordinates(tmp_path):
+    page = _exif_rotated_page(tmp_path)
+
+    for region in layout.detect([page], _cfg()):
+        assert region.bbox[2] <= 400  # page is 400 wide once displayed upright
+        assert region.bbox[3] <= 600

+def _write_page(path, size=(200, 300)):
+    rng = np.random.default_rng(0)
+    arr = rng.integers(200, 255, size=(size[1], size[0]), dtype=np.uint8)
+    Image.fromarray(arr).save(path, format="JPEG", quality=95)
+    return path

+def _cfg(tmp_path, **overrides):

+def _exif_rotated_page(tmp_path, page_id="dolil_212"):
+    """Stored sideways with EXIF orientation=6, as two of the held-out scans are.
+
+    cv2.imread (layout) honours the tag, so region bboxes are in DISPLAY coordinates.
+    PIL.Image.open does not, so a naive crop reads the raw raster and returns the wrong
+    pixels -- the reader would be handed the wrong part of the page, silently.
+    """

=== DIFF: src/doc_agent/vision/layout.py ===
@@ -1,8 +1,117 @@
-"""Stage 2 — layout detection / segmentation"""
+"""Stage 2 — layout detection / segmentation
+
+Training-free projection heuristic (Step 0 decision D1). The corpus is dense handwritten
+deed body text with no tables and no multi-column pages, so a detector's general region
+typing would buy classes this corpus does not contain, at the cost of a checkpoint, a GPU
+pass per page, and another pinned dependency. Ink projected onto the vertical axis is
+enough: handwritten lines produce clear ink bands, and the blank runs between paragraphs
+are wider than the leading within one.
+
+Every region is emitted as kind="text". Heading/table/figure are left unused rather than
+guessed at -- an unconsumed class nobody evaluates is untested surface, and the reader
+handles structure inside a region anyway.
+
+Self-contained by design: this does its own Otsu binarization from whatever image the Page
+points at, so it works on a raw scan or on the ingest stage's preprocessed greyscale.
+
+KNOWN LIMITATION, measured on the 8 held-out scans (correctness check, nothing tuned):
+this corpus largely does not contain the blank horizontal bands the heuristic looks for.
+The 4 flatbed-style pages split into 3-9 regions; the 4 camera-photo pages yield a single
+page-sized region, and no amount of deskewing or adaptive thresholding produced blank rows
+on them (both were tried and measured -- the writing genuinely runs continuously down the
+sheet).
+
+DECIDED: region = page. A page-sized region is the expected output here, not a failure to
+segment -- Qwen2.5-VL (Step 0 decision D2) reads a whole page in one call, so finer
+segmentation would buy nothing and would risk splitting a plot number or deed amount
+across two crops, which the precision-first NFR cannot afford. The banding below is kept
+because it still yields tighter crops on flatbed-style pages, but nothing downstream may
+assume more than one region per page.
+"""
+
 from __future__ import annotations
+
+import cv2
+import numpy as np
+
 from ..contracts import *  # noqa
+from ..logging_conf import get_logger
+
+logger = get_logger(__name__)
+
+
+def _read_grey(image_path: str) -> np.ndarray:
+    grey = cv2.imread(image_path, cv2.IMREAD_GRAYSCALE)
+    if grey is None:
+        raise ValueError(f"unreadable page image: {image_path}")
+    return grey
+
+
+def _ink_mask(grey: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:
+    """Binary ink mask (True = ink), via Otsu so an aged grey ground still separates."""
+    # Otsu picks the threshold per page: scan ground ranges from near-white to heavily
+    # aged grey across this corpus, and a fixed cutoff would erase the darker scans.
+    _, binary = cv2.threshold(grey, 0, 255, cv2.THRESH_BINARY_INV + cv2.THRESH_OTSU)
+    return binary > 0
+
+
+def _bands(ink_rows: np.ndarray, gap: int) -> list[tuple[int, int]]:
+    """Row indices where ink is present -> [start, end) bands, merging gaps below `gap`."""
+    if not ink_rows.any():
+        return []
+
+    rows = np.flatnonzero(ink_rows)
+    bands: list[list[int]] = [[int(rows[0]), int(rows[0]) + 1]]
+    for row in rows[1:]:
+        row = int(row)
+        # A blank run shorter than line_gap_px is the leading between lines of one
+        # paragraph; anything wider is a real block boundary.
+        if row - bands[-1][1] <= gap:
+            bands[-1][1] = row + 1
+        else:
+            bands.append([row, row + 1])
+    return [(start, end) for start, end in bands]
+
 
 def detect(pages: list[Page], cfg: dict) -> list[Region]:
-    """Detect text/table/figure/heading regions. IMPLEMENT."""
-    raise NotImplementedError("Stage 2: layout detection")
+    """Detect text regions on each page. Returns them in page order, top to bottom."""
+    params = cfg["layout"]
+    min_ink_frac = float(params["min_ink_frac"])
+    gap = int(params["line_gap_px"])
+    min_height = int(params["min_height_px"])
+    min_width = int(params["min_width_px"])
+    pad = int(params["pad_px"])
+
+    regions: list[Region] = []
+    for page in pages:
+        mask = _ink_mask(_read_grey(page.image_path))
+        height, width = mask.shape
+
+        # A row counts as text only if enough of it is ink -- this is what keeps dust
+        # specks and scan-edge artefacts from opening a band.
+        row_ink = mask.sum(axis=1) >= max(1, int(min_ink_frac * width))
+
+        for top, bottom in _bands(row_ink, gap):
+            columns = np.flatnonzero(mask[top:bottom].any(axis=0))
+            if columns.size == 0:
+                continue
+            left, right = int(columns[0]), int(columns[-1]) + 1
+
+            if (bottom - top) < min_height or (right - left) < min_width:
+                continue
+
+            regions.append(
+                Region(
+                    page_id=page.id,
+                    bbox=(
+                        max(0, left - pad),
+                        max(0, top - pad),
+                        min(width, right + pad),
+                        min(height, bottom + pad),
+                    ),
+                    kind="text",
+                )
+            )
 
+    logger.info("layout: %d region(s) across %d page(s)", len(regions), len(pages))
+    return regions

- `numpy>=1.26,<2.0` (pinned `<2` — torch 2.3 requires it)

I did **not** add `scikit-image`. It's installed in `.venv` but nothing in `src/`, `tests/`, or `scripts/` imports it — I grepped. Declaring it would just be dead weight in the lock.

`uv lock --check` passes, so `uv sync --frozen` will now resolve. That step goes green.

**`dcdeeb2`** — cleaned my own lane's lint: one `I001` in `prompts.py`, black on three files. Full suite: **37 passed, 11 skipped**.

## What's still broken (pre-existing, not ours)

| gate | at `2776008` (baseline) | now |
|---|---|---|

**CI has never been green.** The lockfile was only the first of four failing steps. `uv run ruff check .`, `black --check .`, and `mypy src` all fail on the template's own scaffold code.

My remaining 11 ruff errors are all `F405` from `from ..contracts import *` — that's the template's mandated pattern and `tests/test_structure.py` locks it, so I can't fix them without changing the import style the structure test enforces. My mypy delta (+7) is real though: 5 in `ocr.py` from the lazily-initialized `self._model = None` (the VLM loads on first use), plus 2 more. Those are genuinely mine and fixable with proper `Optional` typing and an assert-or-raise in the load path.

## What I did not do

Turning CI actually green needs `black .` across ~70 files — the whole template, including Aishi's ingest stubs and Adri's index/governance files. That's a repo-wide diff touching both their lanes right before A2, and it'd conflict with anything they have in flight. **That's a team call, not mine to make unilaterally.** Same reasoning as the eda.ipynb items.

Two options when you want it:
1. **Someone runs `black .` + `ruff check --fix .` in one dedicated commit**, ideally whoever pushes last before the tag, so nobody rebases onto a reformat.
2. **Relax the CI gates** — drop `black --check` and `mypy` from `ci.yml`, keep ruff and the pytest steps. Defensible for a course project, and it means CI reports something real instead of being permanently red.

I'd lean toward (2) plus fixing my own mypy errors, since (1) makes a 70-file diff that reviewers have to scroll past on a graded repo.

CLAUDE:
Yes — write them too. Everything visible on the page goes in `text`.

The reason is what `text` feeds. `ocr_f1` compares the model's transcription against gold token-by-token over the **whole page**. The VLM doesn't know what's "important" — it transcribes whatever is in the crop, boilerplate included. Every line you leave out of gold that the model correctly reads becomes a **false positive** and pushes precision down. You'd be measuring your own omissions, not the model's errors. dolil_13 already set this precedent: its gold `text` includes the fee breakdown (`[?] - ২৫০`, `ই - ১০`, `এন - ১২`, `কর - ১০০`), the stamp serial `৫০৯৩০৮৬`, and the whole scribe block — none of which is "important info" either.

Selectivity belongs one level down, in `fields`. That's what critical-field EM scores, and that's where judgment about what matters is *supposed* to live. So: `text` = exhaustive, `fields` = curated. The boring lines cost you nothing there.

Two things about what you've sent:

**The English printed text is fine to include** — and it resolves something. dolil_20 currently carries a `printed_draft` (model-read, unverified) holding exactly those lines: `ONE RUPEE EIGHT ANNAS`, `I R. 8 As.`, the `Sub-Registrar Raipur, Dist. Noakhali` block, and a partly-legible 1922 registration stamp. Once your human transcription covers that same material, two competing versions of it exist on one page. When I record this I'd fold the printed lines into `text` and mark `printed_draft` superseded rather than delete it, so the provenance stays visible. Note the model read a registration rubber stamp with `Act. 1922 / No. [?]` — if you can make out that number, it's a candidate `deed_serial`, and this page is the one A1 cited.

**You have both parties already**: গ্রহিতা = জাহাবকক পাটারি (পিতা রহিম বকক পাটারি), দাত্রী = শ্রীমতী আতরজান. `৯০ টাকা` reads as consideration → `extra_fields.consideration_amount`, same slot dolil_13 used for its ২০০০০. I don't see a land area yet, so `land_amount` may be in the untranscribed body — worth a look before you close the page out, since it's a critical field.

Keep using `[?]` for the illegible spots exactly as you have (`হাওলা দাবি ? ?`, `পতি ? মিত্র`) — I'd normalize those bare `?` to `[?]` for consistency with dolil_13.

Want me to write this into `labels.jsonl` now as a partial (status stays `TODO`, notes recording that the body text is still outstanding), or hold until you've finished the page?

CLAUDE:
Written, uncommitted. **3/8 pages** now have gold. Both dolil_20 and dolil_66 are sitting in the working tree.

I used two distinct markers, because they mean different things to the metric: `[illegible]` = a human looked at the whole line and recovered nothing; `[?]` = an unreadable span inside a readable line. Eleven of eighteen lines are `[illegible]`.

## The problem this page creates

**61% of dolil_66 has no ground truth.** I set `ocr_f1_eligible: false` on it.

If you score this page, the model will emit *something* for those eleven lines, and every token becomes a false positive — not because the model got it wrong, but because we have nothing to compare against. That measures how hard the page was to photograph, not how well the OCR reads. It would drag the corpus F1 down with a number that means nothing.

This is already what you planned — full text on the flatbeds, critical fields only on the photos. dolil_66 is a photo. So it feeds **critical-field EM only**, and the flag makes that machine-readable instead of buried in prose.

I added two new keys, `ocr_f1_eligible` and `illegible_line_ratio`, which is a schema change. `eval/metrics.py` doesn't exist yet so nothing breaks, but whoever writes `ocr_f1` has to honor the flag or the number is wrong. That's a contract to hand over, not just a note in my lane.

## A second EM problem

Both party names contain `[?]`: `মো: [?] রব` and `[?] [?] উদ্দিন সরকার`. **A gold value with an unreadable span can never satisfy exact match** — EM on this field is guaranteed to fail no matter how good the model is. They need excluding from the EM denominator, not scoring as misses. I flagged it in the notes. Worth deciding as a team how partially-legible criticals are handled, since it directly sets whether the ≥95% EM target is even reachable.

## One thing I left blank — need your answer

**`deed_serial` is empty, pending you.** The page opens with a bare `23` and I don't know what it is — registry serial, volume, page number, something else. It's in `text`, just withheld from `fields`.

I didn't guess because this is the field that matters most right now. Both previous pages yielded no registry serial, which undercuts the serial-run assumption behind the deed-grouping method. If `23` *is* a registry serial, that's the first real evidence in the other direction. If it's a page number, the pattern holds at three for three. Either way it shouldn't rest on my inference.

What is `23` on that page?

That's a strong signal, actually — more than "no idea" suggests.

Red ink on a page whose body is blue is almost certainly a **different hand at a different time**. Registry offices annotate deeds in red after the fact; the deed writer uses blue. CLAUDE:
I merged a wrong string into gold and have removed it. Details below, along with what `23` actually is.

## `23` is a registration endorsement number

It's in the **red rubber-stamped English endorsement** at the top of dolil_66 — the blue blurred handwriting in front of it is the deed body, which is why it looked orphaned. The stamp reads roughly:

```
…the … Bengal
Amendment Act, 19_8.
S…dule 1 …      No. 2_      ← underlined in red
Per …
```

So the red ink is the registry office writing on the deed after the fact, exactly as the colour split suggested. `23` sits in the `No.` slot of that endorsement. It is **not** something the deed writer wrote, which is why it doesn't belong to any body line.

## The bigger find: dolil_20 has one too

While checking the comparable stamp on dolil_20, I found a handwritten number at the top-left beside the printed `I R. 8 As.` — reads as **`27_`**. Neither you nor I transcribed it; it's not in gold.

That matters. If these were a *statutory* article number (the "Schedule 1" phrasing made me suspect that), every deed would carry the same value and it'd be useless as a grouping key. But dolil_66 shows `2_` and dolil_20 shows `27_` — **different numbers on different deeds**. So they're per-document office annotations, and they *are* plausible registry serials. That's the first evidence supporting the deed-grouping premise rather than undercutting it.

One catch: the final glyph is the **same shape in both pages** — a loop with a descending tail. If it's `3` in dolil_66, it's `3` in dolil_20 (`273`). You read dolil_66 as `23`, so that's the consistent reading, but `9` is genuinely possible for both. I'm not calling it — that's handwriting, and it's your call.

## The correction

dolil_20's gold contained `এক রুপয়া আট আনা`. **The page is Devanagari — `एक रुपया आठ आना` — not Bangla.** That string came from the model-written `printed_draft`; when you confirmed the printed elements were there, you were right that a denomination line exists, but the draft had the script wrong and I merged it verbatim.

I've **deleted** the bad line rather than replacing it, because gold has to be typed by a human and I'm not going to author it. Please add it by hand. Gold text is now 27 lines. `I R. 8 As.`, `INDIA`, and the `Act. 1922 / No. [?]` stamp all check out and stayed.

That `No. [?]` on dolil_20 is the same field as dolil_66's — and now we know `27_` is written elsewhere on the page, so the two may or may not be the same number.

Still uncommitted: dolil_20 (merge + correction), dolil_66 (new). Three things I'd want from you: the second digit on each page, the Devanagari line typed by hand, and whether `27_` goes into dolil_20's `deed_serial`.

## This page breaks `land_amount`

I left it empty, deliberately. The deed conveys a **share of multiple plots**: দাগ ১৩৯১ / ১৩৯২ / ১৩৯৪ / ১৪০৫ (with সাবেক ৫২৫–৫৩৪ as the former survey numbers), areas ৩৪ ডিং, ১২ ডিং, ৬ ডিং, ৯ ১/২ ডি, against a ১/৬ অংশ share — and `৮ ১/২` appears twice as the portion actually moving (`মো:৩৬ ডিং এর ৮ ১/২`).

**A single scalar can't hold that.** I could have written `৮ ১/২` and it would look fine, but it would be one reading of a compound structure, and critical-field EM would then score the model against my interpretation rather than the document. The per-plot areas are all preserved in `extra_fields.area_by_dag`, so nothing is lost while you decide.

This is a schema question for the team, not a transcription one: does `land_amount` mean *total area referenced*, *area conveyed*, or *per-plot list*? dolil_13's `১ ১/২` and dolil_66's `১০ ১/২` are both single-plot deeds, so the ambiguity never surfaced until now. Whatever you pick has to be applied back to those two.

## Also left blank

**`deed_serial`** — four candidates on this page (`ণ/২ - ১৫০৮৫৪২`, `১৬৪৯`, `৪৬৮৪ নং`, `(৬)৫৮ নং`) and no way to tell which is the deed's. I filed `১৫০৮৫৪২` as `stamp_paper_serial` since it's 7 digits in the same slot as dolil_13's `৫০৯৩০৮৬`. Note this page has **no red endorsement stamp** like dolil_66's, so the `No.` annotation series doesn't appear here.

**Roles for `party_names`** — seven legible names, but this page has no `দাতা`/`গ্রহীতা` labels in the readable text, unlike 13 and 20. Line 21 (`ওয়ারিশ সূত্রে খরিদ সূত্রে মালিক`) suggests co-heirs on one side, and there's a `৩য় পক্ষ` referenced. I recorded the names without assigning sides. An eighth name (`[?] বেগম`) is dropped rather than guessed.

## One finding worth flagging to the team

This is a **modern Bangladesh deed** — `গণপ্রজাতন্ত্রী বাংলাদেশ সরকার`, ৳২. dolil_20 is British-era, dated 15.1.19, `ONE RUPEE EIGHT ANNAS`. The corpus spans **two states and ~70 years**, with different stamp formats, endorsement languages, and survey vocabulary (`হালে`/`সাবেক` current-vs-former দাগ numbering appears here and nowhere else so far).

That's a heterogeneity claim for §3, and it also bears on Aishi's deed-grouping: a serial scheme that exists on 1919 deeds needn't exist on 1990s ones, and vice versa.

Four pages left: dolil_11 (the last flatbed), 208, 212, 605.

## First real `deed_serial` — and I did assign it

`৯০০৩`. It's the one field I've filled on inference, so here's the reasoning to check:

You read the digits, twice — lines 4 and 11. What I inferred is only the *label*: that this repeated number is the deed serial. It recurs inside the registrar's endorsement, directly beside `মো: শাহজাহান কবির` / `S. R. Joydebpur`, which is where a registration number belongs. That's different from the guesses I refused on the other pages, where I'd have been inventing the reading itself.

Blank the field if you disagree — it's one word, and the note says so.

## The stamp-serial pattern is now clear

`ট ১১৮২২১৩` (here) · `ণ/২ - ১৫০৮৫৪২` (dolil_348) · `৫০৯৩০৮৬` (dolil_13). **All 7 digits, some with a Bangla letter prefix.** These are stamp *paper* serials — they identify the blank sheet the deed was written on, not the deed. Anything clustering on 7-digit numbers to build `doc_id` would be grouping by stationery. Worth passing to Aishi alongside the other grouping findings.

## Two things I deliberately kept out

**`মো: শাহজাহান কবির` is not a party** — he's the Sub-Registrar, name sitting right above his office. He's excluded from `party_names`. This page is a good adversarial case: any extractor keying on "names near the top" will pull the registrar in as a party.

**`land_amount` empty again.** `জমি ৪ ত্র` / `শং মাত্র` breaks across a line — the `৪` is legible, the unit isn't. Probably শতাংশ, but I'm not completing a broken word in gold.

Also flagged: the last four lines repeat and the final one ends mid-word (`যেহেতু নি`), so **the image itself looks cropped short of the full page**. If so, recall scored against this page is measuring a partial document. Worth eyeballing before it goes in the eval.

## The important thing: this isn't a deed body

It's a **শপথনামা** — the sworn declaration the transferor files alongside the deed. Two consequences that hit the project's design decisions directly.

**It's a continuation page.** The numbered clauses start at `৫।` and run ৫, ৬, ৭. Clauses ১–৪ are on a page we don't have. The last line also breaks mid-phrase (`সনাক্তকারী মো: জাফর আলী পিতা হাজী`). So this is hard evidence that **a page is not a document** — which is exactly the assumption `allow_page_as_doc: true` opts into, and the assumption `data/splits.json` currently encodes with its contiguous page ranges. It's the same problem Aishi's `doc_id` work is circling.

**It has no grantee and no land description at all.** Only the declarant, `মো: আ: হামিদ দেওয়ান`. So per-page critical-field EM will score `land_amount` and the grantee side as misses here **no matter how good the extractor is**, because the information isn't on the page. Combined with dolil_20 (area deferred to a তফসিল on another page), that's two of six pages where a critical field is structurally unreachable. The ≥95% EM target can't be evaluated per-page without accounting for it.

## Second witness-vs-party trap

`মো: জাফর আলী` is the **সনাক্তকারী** (identifying witness), excluded from `party_names`. That's the same failure mode as the Sub-Registrar on dolil_605 — two pages in a row where a prominent name is not a party. Good adversarial material for §6.

Stamp serial `থ ৯২৮৬১৯৮` continues the pattern exactly: 7 digits, Bangla letter prefix, four for four.

## Two typing queries — I did not change either

I left both exactly as you typed them, because gold has to record what was read:

- **line 9: `৯৮nong`** — Latin letters mid-Bangla. The page almost certainly reads `৯৮ নং`. This looks like a keyboard slip rather than something on the paper. It matters beyond tidiness: Latin characters in gold will score as mismatches against any correct Bangla prediction and depress `ocr_f1`.
- **line 8: `বাংলাদেহ`** where `বাংলাদেশ` is expected — you spelled it correctly on line 6.

Confirm and I'll fix both; if they're genuinely what's on the page, they stay.
