## A possible cross-page link — worth checking, not concluding

The দাতা here is **আব্দুল হালিম দেওয়ান ওরফে হামিদ দেওয়ান** of village **সুরাবাড়ি**. dolil_208's declarant is **মো: আ: হামিদ দেওয়ান**. dolil_605 concerns **মৌজা সুরবাড়ি**, Joydebpur, Gazipur.

Shared surname দেওয়ান, shared alias হামিদ, shared village — three pages that may belong to one family or one chain of deeds.

**Against it:** dolil_208 is dated ৯/১২/৯৭, this page ৩/৯/০৮ with the grantor's age given as ২৭ — which would make him about 16 in 1997. And `আ: হামিদ` (আব্দুল হামিদ) may just be a different man from আব্দুল হালিম. I've written it into the notes as a lead requiring verification, not a finding.

**If it holds, it's a problem for deed grouping:** dolil_208 and dolil_212 are **four pages apart, not adjacent**. A method that clusters consecutive page or serial numbers into a `doc_id` would never put them together. That's now converging with the missing-serial results on 13/20/66/348 — four pages with no deed serial at all.

## Stamp serials: five for five

`থ ১৭০৪৬৭৬` — 7 digits, Bangla prefix, and it shares the prefix `থ` with dolil_208's `থ ৯২৮৬১৯৮`. The pattern holds across every page that has one. These identify the blank sheet, not the deed.

## Structural findings hardening

**Third continuation page.** Clauses run ৪, ৫, ৬ and it opens mid-address inside clause ৩; the last line breaks at `যেন উহা দাতার`. dolil_208 was ৫,৬,৭. Neither is a whole document.

**Third page with a structurally absent critical field** — no land description, no grantee side; it's the grantor's particulars section. After dolil_20 (area on a তফসিল elsewhere) and dolil_208 (no grantee), that's 3 of 7.

**Third registrar/witness trap.** `হারুনার রশীদ` is the Sadar Sub-Registrar, excluded from `party_names`. After dolil_605's registrar and dolil_208's সনাক্তকারী, this is now a consistent pattern rather than an anomaly — worth its own line in §6.

## Two things I flagged rather than resolved

- **`২৮৯৫৪`** alone at the foot of the page. A `deed_serial` candidate, but it appears once with no label or registrar context — unlike dolil_605's `৯০০৩`, which repeated inside the registrar's block. Left in `extra_fields`, not promoted.
- **`মো: মনতাজ উদ্দিন`** appears only inside a photo caption in the truncated clause ৩. Probably a গ্রহীতা, but that's inferred from position. Recorded with the caution stated.
- **Line 31, `ঠিকানা হবিবর ন:আদালত,`** — `হবিবর ন:` interrupts printed form boilerplate. Could be a name handwritten into a blank, or a misread. Worth a second look when convenient.

CLAUDE:
~48 text lines by ink-row projection — about 30% longer than dolil_212, which is the longest you've done at 37. So the concern is real, not imagined.

## On swapping

**Don't swap it for a shorter page.** That's the one move that damages the numbers: if pages enter the eval set based on how easy they are to transcribe, and transcription difficulty correlates with OCR difficulty (it does — dolil_66 is both), then measured OCR quality gets inflated by construction. It's precisely the kind of thing A2 §5 asks you to disclose, and it would be hard to defend.

**Better: give dolil_11 the dolil_66 treatment.** Critical fields only — `deed_serial`, `deed_date`, `land_amount`, `party_names` — with `ocr_f1_eligible: false` and the reason stated. That's a few minutes instead of 48 lines. You already have this precedent, the page stays in the set, and nothing is quietly dropped.

I'd be straight in §5 that **two of eight pages are excluded from `ocr_f1`** (dolil_66 for illegibility, dolil_11 for transcription cost), that both exclusions plausibly correlate with difficulty, and that `ocr_f1` therefore rests on **6 pages / 886 tokens** and is likely a mild overestimate. Stating the bias is worth more than hiding it behind a swap.

The alternative that's statistically clean — drawing a *random* replacement blind to content — is now possible, but it could easily hand you a 60-line page. Not obviously a win.

Your call: full transcription, critical-fields-only, or leave it TODO at n=7.

## Two things I found while checking — both Aishi's lane

**The corpus is here.** `data/raw/` has **653 pages, 2.2 GB**. It was empty at handoff. That unblocks the full OCR run and the OCR half of `kb_demo` — the only thing still blocking those is D3 (the model/quantization decision).

**`splits.json` doesn't cover the corpus, and the held-out set sits inside `train`:**

```
dolil_11, 13, 20, 66, 208, 212, 348  -> train
dolil_605                            -> test
```

`manifest.yaml:22` says held-out pages are "never OCR-trained on", but 7 of 8 are in the train split. With a zero-shot VLM nothing is actually trained, so there's no practical leak today — but the declared contract and the file contradict each other, and A1 claims the leak checks pass.

Also: **653 pages on disk, 609 in splits — 44 unaccounted for.** That 609 is the same denominator as both legibility figures in the handoff (184/609 and 119/609), so whatever dropped those 44 predates this.

I haven't touched any of it. Adding to the handoff list for Aishi rather than fixing unilaterally.

CLAUDE:
Your teammate is right and I was wrong. I've verified it against the corpus.

## D1's stated rationale is false

`vision/layout.py` records the decision as "projection heuristic, not DocLayout-YOLO (no tables/multi-column in this corpus)." **There are tables.** `dolil_74` is a তফসিল laid out as a four-column table — দাগ numbers, descriptions, a marker column, and areas — and the projection heuristic will merge those columns into single horizontal bands and scramble the reading order.

My first measurement of this was also broken, and I want to flag that since I nearly reported it: Otsu over a whole phone photo separates *paper from the dark surface it was shot on*, not ink from paper, so the "gutters" it found were the sheets themselves. After cropping to the paper first, the honest numbers are:

```
widest interior ink-free gutter / inked span   (650 pages)
  p50 0.000   p90 0.026   p95 0.048   p99 0.109
  > 0.06 of span:  23 pages (3.5%)
  > 0.15 of span:   4 pages (0.6%)
```

**Treat 3.5% as a lower bound, not the rate.** A wide-gutter test finds two-column splits; it does *not* find dense tables like dolil_74, whose inter-column gaps are narrow. So the real tabular fraction is higher than this and I can't put a number on it without a proper detector.

Two other things fell out that I didn't go looking for: **some pages are rotated 90° in-content** (`dolil_429`), which EXIF correction won't fix and which defeats horizontal projection entirely; and that page carries a **Pakistan-era FOUR ANNAS stamp**, so the corpus spans three states — British India (dolil_20, 1919), Pakistan, and Bangladesh — not two as I said earlier.

## On the model

Their local-first reasoning is the stronger argument and it converges with what I recommended for unrelated reasons, so: **`Qwen/Qwen2.5-VL-3B-Instruct`, local.** Fits a T4 in fp16, no quantization toolchain, and D2's single-pass-first stance matches their "single VLM, no 2-step."

Two caveats to carry into A2 §4 rather than lose:

- **The API remark cuts against their own rationale.** If the design reason for local inference is that land deeds are sensitive and API providers retain data, then reaching for an API when the local model underperforms discards the reason. Pick one and defend it. I'd stay local.
- **State the privacy argument precisely.** This corpus is a public CC BY 4.0 Mendeley dataset, so nothing here is actually sensitive. The local-first constraint is about the *deployment scenario* a real land-deed system implies. Said plainly that's a strong answer; said loosely it reads as a factual error about your own data.

Their GraDeT-HTR + DocLayout-YOLO framing as *fallback only* is compatible with D2 as written — D2 deferred it for lack of measured accuracy, not on principle. I'd amend the docstring to say deferred pending `ocr_f1`, not rejected.

## Your next step

Unchanged, and now better specified: **pin the model and fix D1's rationale, then run.**

1. `configs/config.yaml:14` → `Qwen/Qwen2.5-VL-3B-Instruct`; line 3 `device` stays `cpu` here but needs to be `cuda` wherever you actually run.
2. Correct the D1 comment in `layout.py` and `config.yaml:6` — the "no tables/multi-column" claim is disproven and it's currently load-bearing for a decision in the A2 form.
3. Full-corpus OCR run, then `ocr_f1`.

Worth knowing before step 3: since `transcribe()` emits one Chunk per page and the VLM sees the page image, **the table problem may not bite much in practice** — Qwen2.5-VL can read a table off a full page. Layout mainly governs cropping. So I'd run first and measure, rather than build column-splitting on spec.

Want me to make the two config/docstring edits?

## Decisions
- **D1 — layout = training-free projection heuristic. RATIONALE CORRECTED THIS SESSION.**
  The old docstring claimed "no tables and no multi-column pages". **That is FALSE.**
  `dolil_74` is a তফসিল laid out as a 4-column table. Measured over all 653 pages
  (crop to paper FIRST, then threshold ink): widest interior ink-free gutter / inked span
  p50 0.000, p90 0.026, p95 0.048, p99 0.109; >0.06 = 23 pages (3.5%); >0.15 = 4 (0.6%).
  **3.5% is a LOWER BOUND** — a wide-gutter test misses dense tables with narrow gutters.
  Decision still stands on a narrower argument: D2 reads whole pages in one VLM call, so
  layout only governs cropping. Overturn condition: ocr_f1 materially worse on tabular pages.
  *Methodological trap that produced a wrong first answer: Otsu over a whole phone photo
  separates paper from the dark surface it was shot on, NOT ink from paper.*
- **D2 — single-pass VLM, no confidence router.** GraDeT-HTR + DocLayout-YOLO is
  **DEFERRED, NOT REJECTED** — it was always the Bangla-specific fallback (DocLayout-YOLO only
  because GraDeT-HTR does no layout). Revisit once ocr_f1 exists.
- **D3 — RESOLVED. `Qwen/Qwen2.5-VL-3B-Instruct`, run LOCALLY.** Pinned in `config.yaml`.
  Local-first is a deployment constraint (land deeds are sensitive; hosted APIs may retain
  data) — state precisely that THIS corpus is public CC BY 4.0 and carries no such risk, the
  constraint is about the deployment being modelled. 3B over 7B: 7B fp16 doesn't fit a T4, and
  3B avoids a quantization toolchain. Escalate to 7B-AWQ only if ocr_f1 says so.
  ⚠️ `config.yaml:3` still `device: cpu` — must be `cuda` wherever you actually run.
- **region = page**; `ocr.transcribe()` emits one Chunk per page, regions concatenated.
- **doc_id sourcing** — ingest sidecar → deed_groups.csv → raise. `allow_page_as_doc` leaks by design.

  otherwise in A2 §3; n=7 and the variables are confounded.
- Conventions: `[?]` = unreadable span inside a readable line; `[illegible]` = whole line

### Two open schema questions (need the team, block nothing else)
1. **`land_amount` cannot represent dolil_348** — multi-plot deed, 4 দাগ with separate areas
   (৩৪/১২/৬ ডিং, ৯ ১/২ ডি) against a ১/৬ share; `৮ ১/২` appears twice as the portion moving.
   Whatever definition is chosen must be applied back to dolil_13 and dolil_66.
2. **Partially-legible criticals can never satisfy EM** — dolil_66's party names both contain
   `[?]`. Must be excluded from the EM denominator. Likewise the 3 pages where a critical field
   is structurally ABSENT (dolil_20 area on a তফসিল elsewhere; dolil_208 no grantee;
   dolil_212 no land). **≥95% EM is unreachable per-page without accounting for this.**

## Findings for A2 §3 / §6
- **Corpus spans THREE states**: British India (dolil_20, 1919, ONE RUPEE EIGHT ANNAS),
  Pakistan (dolil_429, FOUR ANNAS crescent stamp), Bangladesh (348/605/208/212).
- **Pages are not documents.** dolil_208 (clauses ৫,৬,৭) and dolil_212 (clauses ৪,৫,৬) are
  continuation pages opening mid-list; both break mid-sentence at the bottom.
- **Registrar/witness trap, 3 pages running** — names that must NOT go in `party_names`:
  `মো: শাহজাহান কবির` (Sub-Registrar, dolil_605), `মো: জাফর আলী` (সনাক্তকারী, dolil_208),
  `হারুনার রশীদ` (Sadar Sub-Registrar, dolil_212). Good adversarial cases.
- **Stamp-paper serials are 7 digits with a Bangla letter prefix, 5 for 5**: ৫০৯৩০৮৬ (13),
  ণ/২-১৫০৮৫৪২ (348), ট-১১৮২২১৩ (605), থ-৯২৮৬১৯৮ (208), থ-১৭০৪৬৭৬ (212).
  **These identify the blank SHEET, not the deed.** Grouping on them = grouping by stationery.
- **Only ONE deed serial found: `৯০০৩` on dolil_605** (repeated twice inside the registrar's
  block). dolil_13/20/66/348 yield none. dolil_66's `23` is in a red rubber-stamped English
  registration endorsement (`…Bengal Amendment Act, 19_8 … No. 2_`); dolil_20 has a handwritten
  `27_` top-left. Final glyph is the SAME shape on both pages — `3` vs `9` unresolved.
- **Some pages are rotated 90° in-content** (dolil_429). EXIF does not fix it; defeats
  horizontal projection outright. **Prevalence not yet measured.**
- EXIF bug (fixed earlier): dolil_212/605 carry orientation=6; `cv2.imread` applies EXIF,
  `PIL.Image.open` does not. Fixed with `ImageOps.exif_transpose`.

CLAUDE:
No — I haven't created any tag. `git tag -l` is empty locally, and the remote has none either.

You're right that it goes last. A tag is just a named pointer at a commit, so it should point at the state you're actually submitting — after the OCR run, `ocr_f1`, and the A2 form sections are in. Tagging now would pin a snapshot with §3/§4/§6 unwritten and no accuracy number.

Nothing in this session touched tags; the five commits are ordinary commits on `main`.

When you're actually ready, the sequence is:

```bash
git push origin main          # commits first
git tag -a a2-submit -m "A2 submission"
git push origin a2-submit     # tags need their own push
```

Two things worth knowing now rather than at the deadline:

**Tags don't ride along with `git push`.** `git push origin main` pushes commits only. Forgetting `git push origin a2-submit` is the classic way to have a tag that exists only on your laptop while the grader sees an untagged repo.

**Confirm the repo is public before the deadline, not on it.** A2 is graded off the tag on a public repo rather than a ZIP. My `git ls-remote` came back empty with no auth error, which is consistent with a public repo that has no tags — but that's weak evidence, so check it in the GitHub UI.

And the push is still outstanding — `main` is `[ahead 5]` and I couldn't push it for lack of credentials.

  [result] 1	#!/usr/bin/env python3
2	"""Fill grading_kit/labels.jsonl by hand, one held-out page at a time.
3	
4	These are GOLD labels: eval/metrics.py:ocr_f1 scores the Stage 3 reader against them, so
5	they have to be written by someone who reads Bangla. A model-written transcription would
6	make the metric measure agreement-with-a-model instead of correctness, and would do it
7	invisibly -- if the reader and the labeller share a failure mode, the score comes out high.

+it. Measured over all 653 raw pages (crop to the paper first, then threshold ink -- an
+Otsu pass over a whole phone photo separates paper from the surface it was shot on, not
+ink from paper, and gives nonsense):
+
+    widest interior ink-free gutter / inked span
+    p50 0.000   p90 0.026   p95 0.048   p99 0.109
+    > 0.06 of span:  23 pages (3.5%)     > 0.15 of span: 4 pages (0.6%)
+
+and dolil_74 is a তফসিল (schedule) laid out as a FOUR-COLUMN TABLE. Treat 3.5% as a LOWER
+BOUND on tabular pages, not a rate: a wide-gutter test finds two-column splits but misses
+dense tables whose inter-column gaps are narrow. Horizontal projection will merge such
+columns into single bands and scramble reading order on those pages.
+
+The decision still stands, on a different and narrower argument: reading is a single VLM
+call over the whole page (D2), so the VLM sees the table itself and layout governs only
+cropping. Revisit if ocr_f1 turns out to be materially worse on tabular pages -- that is
+the measurement that should decide it, not this docstring.
+
+ALSO UNHANDLED: some pages are rotated 90 degrees in-content (e.g. dolil_429), which EXIF
+correction does not fix and which defeats horizontal projection outright. Prevalence not
+yet measured.

  [result] # Cross-cutting features — audit & touchpoint map

Not every feature lives in one file. **Vertical** features are a pipeline stage and own one module.
**Horizontal** features are a property of the whole system: they have ONE owner (the logic) but run at
MANY touchpoints. The clean tree holds because horizontals attach at fixed **hook seams** (`hooks.py`),
wired in one manifest (`wiring.py`) - so their touchpoints are explicit, not scattered.

## Vertical features (own one module - the clean tree is enough)
ingest/loader - preprocess - enhance - vision/layout - vision/ocr - index/chunk - embed

## Where your lane stands

Both your modules are code-complete and green — `pytest tests/test_ocr.py tests/test_layout.py` → 29 passed.

- [layout.py](src/doc_agent/vision/layout.py) — projection heuristic, D1 rationale corrected (the "no tables" claim retracted, `dolil_74` is a 4-column তফসিল), decision landed on region = page.
- [ocr.py](src/doc_agent/vision/ocr.py) — `Reader` + `transcribe`, page index with the doc_id-refusal guard, EXIF fix on crops, Qwen2.5-VL-3B pinned in [config.yaml:14](configs/config.yaml#L14).

What has **not** happened: the reader has never been scored. Every deferred decision in your own docstrings is explicitly waiting on one number — "revisit once `eval/metrics.py:ocr_f1` scores the held-out pages" appears in both files. And [metrics.py:5](src/doc_agent/eval/metrics.py#L5) is still `raise NotImplementedError`.

Meanwhile Aishi's pilot (977803f) already found two concrete reader defects and wrote them into [data/provenance.md](data/provenance.md): **token truncation** and **repetition looping on dense pages**, with the pilot's median ~60 words/page undercounting hand-counted pages that ran 259–447 words. Your `max_new_tokens: 768` with plain greedy decoding (`do_sample=False`, no repetition penalty) is the direct cause of both.

## Next step: baseline `ocr_f1` on the 7 gold pages

Measure before you tune — otherwise you're fixing truncation with no way to tell if it helped.

1. Implement `ocr_f1(pred, gold)` in [metrics.py:5](src/doc_agent/eval/metrics.py#L5) — token-level F1 over `normalize()`d text, so it shares the reader's NFC + Bangla→ASCII digit policy rather than a second one.
2. Run the reader over `grading_kit/heldout_pages/` and score against the 7 `status: done` rows in `labels.jsonl` (`dolil_11` is still TODO — human-typed only, don't fill it).
3. Report per-page: F1, output token count, and whether generation hit the 768 cap. That separates "the model can't read it" from "we cut it off."
4. Then tune with evidence — raise `max_new_tokens`, add `repetition_penalty` / `no_repeat_ngram_size`, re-score. Deltas against the baseline, not vibes.

That one baseline unblocks four decisions your lane parked: 3B vs escalating to 7B-AWQ, single-pass vs the deferred GraDeT-HTR fallback, whether D1's page-level layout hurts on tabular pages, and the real corpus word count.

Two coordination notes: you don't need Aishi's sidecar — `data/interim/` doesn't exist yet, but `load_page_index` falls back to `data/raw` + `deed_groups.csv` (653 rows, present), so you're not blocked. And `ocr_f1` lives in the eval module, which the lane table doesn't assign to anyone; you own "OCR metric in kb_demo," so claim it explicitly with Adri before writing into `eval/`.

Want me to start with the `ocr_f1` implementation and the scoring harness?

## What's in place

**`ocr_f1`** — [metrics.py:34](src/doc_agent/eval/metrics.py#L34). Token-level F1 that imports `normalize` from the reader rather than reimplementing it, so "২১৬৩" and "2163" match exactly the way the pipeline emits them. Key semantics, each pinned by a test in [tests/test_eval.py](tests/test_eval.py) (10 tests):

- **Multiset, not set** — a repetition loop costs precision instead of scoring 1.0. Set-based F1 would have hidden the exact defect the pilot found.
- **Truncation loses recall, keeps precision** — so the score's shape tells you which failure you're looking at.
- **`[?]` and `[illegible]` dropped from both sides** — otherwise a reader inflates F1 by agreeing about what it couldn't read. Your gold labels use `[?]` heavily (`dolil_13` has 8), so this mattered.

**Truncation diagnostic** — `Reader.generation_stats` in [ocr.py:125](src/doc_agent/vision/ocr.py#L125), one record per region with `generated_tokens` and `truncated` (hit the cap under greedy decoding). 4 tests, faking the VLM at the processor/model seam.

**`scripts/score_heldout.py`** — runs the real path (`layout.detect` → `ocr.transcribe`), so the number describes what the pipeline actually produces, not a direct model call. Per-page F1 + region count + word counts + tokens + cut flag, written to `reports/ocr_baseline.json`. 5 tests.

## Verification

- Full suite: **56 passed, 10 skipped**.
- `black --check` and `ruff` clean on my five files (the 9 remaining `F405` are the template's mandated `from ..contracts import *`).
- `mypy src`: **49 errors before, 49 after** — I added none. The repo baseline is already red from unimplemented stubs.
- Dry run on the 7 real held-out scans: gold-in → **1.000 on every page**; a silent reader → **0.000 with all 7 still listed** (a missed page can't vanish and quietly lift the mean).

That dry run also surfaced something for your run: layout emits **9 regions on `dolil_605`, 5 on `dolil_13`, 4 on `dolil_20`, 1 each on the four camera-photo pages** — matching your layout docstring. The multi-region pages get 9 separate VLM calls concatenated, so if truncation shows up there it's per-region, not per-page.

CLAUDE:
Verified the blocker before writing the recipe: **your pinned `transformers` cannot load this checkpoint.** [pyproject.toml:7](pyproject.toml#L7) pins `>=4.40,<4.45` (4.44.2 installed here), and `qwen2_5_vl` first appears in the auto-mapping in **4.49.0** — I checked the `modeling_auto.py` source at v4.49.0 and v4.53.0. Under 4.44 `from_pretrained` fails with "does not recognize this architecture." CI still passes because the tests fake the model, so nothing caught it.

Second issue: `_load()` uses defaults — fp32 (3B ≈ 12.4 GB on a 15 GB T4) and Qwen2.5-VL's default `max_pixels` (~12.8M px ≈ 16k visual tokens per crop). On full-page deed scans that OOMs or crawls.

Both are fixable in the notebook without touching the repo, because `score_pages` takes an injected reader.

## Prerequisites

1. **Push first** — the clone needs `scripts/score_heldout.py`, which is uncommitted. Your remote is `github.com/Mahbuba51/doc-agent-12`. (Say the word and I'll commit + push.)
2. Kaggle notebook → **Settings**: Accelerator **GPU T4 x2**, Internet **On**. Both required — internet for the HF download.
3. `data/raw/` is gitignored, but `grading_kit/heldout_pages/` is tracked (all 8 JPEGs), so the clone has everything this run needs.

## Cell 1 — setup

```python
!pip install -q -U "transformers>=4.49,<5" accelerate
!git clone -q https://github.com/Mahbuba51/doc-agent-12.git /kaggle/working/doc-agent-12
```

Pinned `<5` deliberately: transformers 5.x renamed `torch_dtype`→`dtype` and reshuffled the auto classes, so `AutoModelForVision2Seq` in your `_load()` may not survive it.

## Cell 2 — run (fresh kernel session after the install)

```python
import importlib.util, torch
from pathlib import Path

ROOT = Path("/kaggle/working/doc-agent-12")
spec = importlib.util.spec_from_file_location("sh", ROOT / "scripts/score_heldout.py")
sh = importlib.util.module_from_spec(spec); spec.loader.exec_module(sh)  # also puts src/ on sys.path

from doc_agent import config
from doc_agent.vision import ocr
from transformers import AutoModelForVision2Seq, AutoProcessor

cfg = config.load(ROOT / "configs/config.yaml")
cfg["ocr"] = {**cfg["ocr"], "sidecar": "", "pages_dir": str(sh.HELDOUT_PAGES),
              "deed_groups": str(ROOT / cfg["ocr"]["deed_groups"])}

name = cfg["ocr"]["model"]
reader = ocr.Reader(cfg)
# Injected at the processor/model seam, so _load() stays a no-op. fp16 halves the weights to
# ~6GB; max_pixels caps a full-page scan at ~1280 visual tokens instead of ~16k.
reader._processor = AutoProcessor.from_pretrained(
    name, min_pixels=256 * 28 * 28, max_pixels=1280 * 28 * 28
)
reader._model = AutoModelForVision2Seq.from_pretrained(
    name, torch_dtype=torch.float16, device_map="auto"
)

gold = sh.load_gold(ROOT / "grading_kit/labels.jsonl")
rows = sh.score_pages(gold, cfg, reader=reader)
sh._print_table(rows, sh.summarise(rows))
```

## Cell 3 — save the baseline

```python
import json
summary = sh.summarise(rows)
out = Path("/kaggle/working/ocr_baseline.json")
out.write_text(json.dumps(
    {"model": name, "config": cfg["ocr"], "dtype": "float16",
     "max_pixels": 1280 * 28 * 28, "summary": summary, "pages": rows},
    ensure_ascii=False, indent=2) + "\n", encoding="utf-8")
print(summary)
```

Download it from the notebook's Output pane and commit it to `reports/ocr_baseline.json` — that file is the artifact the four parked decisions get argued from.

## What to expect

7 pages, 22 regions (9 on `dolil_605`, 5 on `dolil_13`, 4 on `dolil_20`, 1 each on the four camera photos). Roughly 30–90s per region on a T4 → **15–35 minutes**, well inside Kaggle's 9h GPU budget. First run also downloads ~7GB of weights.

Watch the `cut` column. All-YES means `max_pixels` or the 768-token cap is what you measured, not the model — raise the cap and re-run before drawing any conclusion about 3B.

## Two things to fix in the repo afterward

- **The `transformers` pin is wrong for your own checkpoint.** Bumping it means editing `pyproject.toml` and regenerating `uv.lock` + `requirements.lock`, which is Aishi's CI lane (f09b4c5) — coordinate rather than pushing it yourself. Worth doing: A2 grades reproducibility, and right now `uv sync --frozen` installs a stack that provably cannot run your Stage 3.
- **`dtype` and `max_pixels` are notebook-only right now.** They belong in `configs/config.yaml` under `ocr:` and read in `_load()`, or your baseline isn't reproducible from config the way the rest of the pipeline is.

Want me to do either — or commit and push so you can clone?

the second cell is running, but the output is stuck here for a while. is it okay?
INFO:doc_agent.vision.layout:layout: 22 region(s) across 7 page(s)
The following generation flags are not valid and may be ignored: ['temperature']. Set `TRANSFORMERS_VERBOSITY=info` for more details.

CLAUDE:
That's expected — both lines are normal, and the silence after them is by design.

**The `temperature` warning is benign.** Qwen ships a `generation_config` with `temperature` set, but `transcribe_region` passes `do_sample=False`, so sampling flags get ignored. It actually confirms greedy decoding is active, which is what the `truncated` flag depends on being meaningful.

**`22 region(s) across 7 page(s)` is exactly right** — it matches the dry run I did here, so layout ran correctly on the real scans.

You're now inside the first `generate()` call, and there is no per-region logging — `transcribe` only logs once, at the very end (`read N page(s) into M chunk(s)`). So from here you get **zero output until the whole run finishes** and the table prints.

Rough timing: 22 regions, up to 768 new tokens each, greedy, fp16 3B on a T4 ≈ 20–40s per region if generation stops early, up to ~60s if it runs to the cap. Call it **20–35 minutes** of total silence. Kaggle runs cells sequentially in one kernel, so you can't slip an `nvidia-smi` in alongside it — just let it sit.

Start suspecting a problem past **~60 minutes**. The realistic failure is `device_map="auto"` having spilled layers to CPU, which makes it crawl rather than error. You can confirm after it finishes (or after an interrupt) with:

```python
print(reader._model.hf_device_map)
```

Every entry should be `0` or `1`, never `'cpu'` or `'disk'`.

If it does finish, `reader.generation_stats` will have one row per region even for the pages that scored badly — that's the truncation evidence, and it survives independently of the table.

Worth adding before the next run: a per-region INFO line in `transcribe` so long runs show progress instead of looking hung. Small change, and it'd have saved you this question. Want me to?

cell 3 output
{'pages': 7, 'mean_f1': 0.022014637199672588, 'median_f1': 0.0, 'min_f1': 0.0, 'max_f1': 0.07608695652173912, 'truncated_pages': 7}

CLAUDE:
That number is not a verdict on Qwen — it's a diagnosis of a broken generation loop. **22 of 22 regions hit the 768 cap.** A healthy transcription emits EOS and stops; a 100% cap-hit rate is pathological.

The decisive tell is `dolil_208`: **768 tokens generated → 1 word.** That's 768 tokens containing essentially no whitespace — a single token repeated to exhaustion. `dolil_66` is the same story from the other side: 43 gold words, but it burned 768 tokens producing 111 words with **zero** overlap. The pilot's repetition loop (977803f), now measured instead of eyeballed.

The metric itself is working — `dolil_20` at 0.076 and `dolil_212` at 0.062 prove it matches Bangla tokens when they're there. Near-zero is a real reading failure, not a scoring artifact.

**One confound is mine.** I set `max_pixels=1280*28*28` (~1M pixels) in the notebook for OOM safety. Your scans are ~3.5MB each, so that's a heavy downscale of dense handwritten Bangla — quite possibly below the resolution where the strokes survive at all. A VLM given an illegible image is exactly what degenerates into repetition. So resolution and repetition control are tangled together right now, and neither is settled.

## Before anything else: read what it actually wrote

You have the text already — `rows[i]["pred"]`. This is the cheapest and most informative next step, no GPU needed:

```python
for r in sorted(rows, key=lambda r: -r["pred_words"])[:3]:
    print("=" * 60, r["page_id"], f"f1={r['f1']:.3f} words={r['pred_words']}")
    print(repr(r["pred"][:600]))
```

`repr` on purpose — you need to see the whitespace. What you find decides the fix:

- **One token repeated** → pure decode loop; fix with `repetition_penalty`.
- **Fluent English describing the deed** → the model ignored `TRANSCRIBE` and captioned instead; that's a prompt problem.
- **Chinese, or Bangla-shaped nonsense** → it can't resolve the script at 1M pixels; raise `max_pixels`.

These call for completely different fixes, and the F1 column can't tell them apart.

## Then one page, not seven

`dolil_66` — 1 region, 43 gold words, ~1 minute per attempt. Vary one thing at a time from the current baseline:

1. `max_pixels=4096*28*28` (~3.2M px), everything else unchanged.
2. Baseline resolution + `repetition_penalty=1.1` in `generate`.
3. Both.

If (1) alone rescues it, the model was fine and I starved it of pixels. Watch VRAM — 3.2M px is ~3× the visual tokens, so drop to one page at a time if it OOMs.

## What to record now

`reports/ocr_baseline.json` is worth committing as-is, labelled for what it is: the reader's score **at 1M pixels with no repetition control**, not Qwen2.5-VL-3B's score on Bangla deeds. Keep `dtype` and `max_pixels` in that file — they're already there, which is what makes it a reproducible data point rather than an anecdote.

Do **not** let 0.022 propagate into `provenance.md` or the D2 decision yet. It doesn't currently support "escalate to 7B" — a 7B model fed 1M pixels and left to loop would score just as badly.

Want me to add `dtype`, `max_pixels`, and `repetition_penalty` as real `ocr:` config knobs read by `_load`/`transcribe_region`, plus the per-region progress log? That turns each of these experiments into a config edit and a re-run, and makes the winning settings reproducible from `config.yaml` instead of living in a notebook cell.

CLAUDE:
Careful — the baseline is *measured*, not *good*. What's done is the instrument, not the reader. And the reader is still the blocker for everything else in your lane.

Here's the part that makes it urgent beyond accuracy. Your held-out sample ran **3.1 regions per page**, every region burning the full 768 tokens. Extrapolate to the corpus: ~652 pages × ~2–3 regions ≈ 1,300–2,000 regions × ~30–60s ≈ **11–33 hours of GPU**. Kaggle caps sessions at 9h and gives ~30h/week. At the current loop rate, **the full-corpus run is not feasible.** A reader that stops at EOS instead of grinding to the cap is a 3–5× compute saving — that's what decides whether the corpus run fits in your quota at all, quite apart from F1.

If your session restarted and `rows` is gone, reload instead of re-running the model:

```python
import json

```

`repr` matters — it shows you whether those 768 tokens were one token repeated with no spaces.

## Cell B — the trial harness

```python
import time, torch
from transformers import AutoProcessor
from doc_agent.contracts import Page
from doc_agent.eval.metrics import ocr_f1
from doc_agent.llm.prompts import TRANSCRIBE
from doc_agent.vision import layout, ocr as ocrmod

PAGE = "dolil_66"                      # 1 region, 43 gold words — the cheapest page
idx = ocrmod.load_page_index(cfg)
page = Page(id=PAGE, image_path=idx[PAGE]["image_path"], doc_id=idx[PAGE]["doc_id"])
regions = layout.detect([page], cfg)
gold_text = gold[PAGE]

_procs = {}
def processor_for(max_pixels):
    if max_pixels not in _procs:      # cached: rebuilding per trial re-reads the config
        _procs[max_pixels] = AutoProcessor.from_pretrained(
            name, min_pixels=256 * 28 * 28, max_pixels=max_pixels)
    return _procs[max_pixels]

def trial(max_pixels, repetition_penalty=1.0, max_new_tokens=768):
    """One read of PAGE. Same crop and prompt as production — only the knobs change."""
    proc, model = processor_for(max_pixels), reader._model
    pieces, tokens, cut, t0 = [], 0, False, time.time()
    for region in regions:
        image = reader.crop(region)
        messages = [{"role": "user",
                     "content": [{"type": "image"}, {"type": "text", "text": TRANSCRIBE}]}]
        prompt = proc.apply_chat_template(messages, tokenize=False, add_generation_prompt=True)
        inputs = proc(text=[prompt], images=[image], return_tensors="pt").to(model.device)
        with torch.inference_mode():
            out = model.generate(**inputs, max_new_tokens=max_new_tokens, do_sample=False,
                                 repetition_penalty=repetition_penalty)
        trimmed = out[0][inputs["input_ids"].shape[1]:]
        n = int(trimmed.shape[0]); tokens += n; cut |= n >= max_new_tokens
        pieces.append(ocrmod.normalize(proc.decode(trimmed, skip_special_tokens=True)))
        del inputs, out
        torch.cuda.empty_cache()
    text = "\n".join(p for p in pieces if p)
    return {"f1": ocr_f1(text, gold_text), "words": len(text.split()), "tokens": tokens,
            "cut": cut, "secs": round(time.time() - t0), "text": text}
```

## Cell C — one variable at a time

```python
GRID = [
    (1280 * 28 * 28, 1.0),   # control: reproduces the 0.000 baseline
    (4096 * 28 * 28, 1.0),   # resolution only
    (1280 * 28 * 28, 1.1),   # repetition penalty only
    (4096 * 28 * 28, 1.1),   # both
]
for mp, rp in GRID:
    r = trial(mp, rp)
    print(f"px={mp // (28 * 28):>5}  rep={rp}  f1={r['f1']:.3f}  words={r['words']:>4}  "
          f"tokens={r['tokens']:>4}  cut={str(r['cut']):>5}  {r['secs']}s")
    print("   ", repr(r["text"][:200]), "\n")
```

The control row is there on purpose — if it doesn't come back ~0.000, something differs from the baseline run and the other three rows can't be trusted.

**Reading it:** `cut=False` anywhere is the first real progress — it means the model chose to stop, which is what makes the corpus run affordable. Then compare F1 across rows. If resolution alone fixes it, my 1M-pixel cap was starving it. If the penalty alone fixes it, it's a decode pathology and resolution was fine.

**If row 2 or 4 OOMs**, 3.2M pixels is too much for the T4 at fp16 — drop to `2048 * 28 * 28` and re-run just that row. `torch.cuda.empty_cache()` between regions helps but won't save you from a single oversized forward pass.

Budget ~1–4 minutes per row (higher resolution is slower), so the whole grid is well under 15 minutes. Paste the output back here and I'll tell you which knob to promote into `config.yaml`.

CLAUDE:
That output settles it, and not in the direction the F1 column suggested.

**The model isn't misreading the handwriting — it isn't reading it at all.** Look at what it produced:

- `dolil_66`: "established on 20 May 1657… one of the oldest national memorials of an artist… a book about the artist's life"
- `dolil_605`: "this deed was established by the founder of the university on 10 May 1972"
- `dolil_13`: "This Bangla deed is not like my own. It is a recognition and founding deed, granted by the Government of Bangladesh."

That's fluent, confident, entirely invented Bangla prose about a document-shaped image. It's confabulation from a language prior, not transcription. Then it degenerates into the repetition loop — the loop is the *symptom*, the hallucination is the disease.

**Ignore the 0.108.** That row produced 14 words, mostly digits, and a run of 90 sevens. A handful of numeric tokens happened to collide with gold. It's small-sample noise, and it is precisely why I pushed to read the text before tuning on the number — the "best" row in your grid is the one reading least.

The grid is unambiguous once you read it that way: 4× the pixels changed nothing, the repetition penalty changed nothing. Every cell still hit the cap. There's no knob here.

## Your pre-registered revisit trigger just fired

From [ocr.py:6](src/doc_agent/vision/ocr.py#L6): *"Revisit once `eval/metrics.py:ocr_f1` scores the held-out pages."* It has. **Qwen2.5-VL-3B has no Bangla handwriting capability**, and this is a categorical gap, not a margin — you're at 0.022 against a headline target of F1 ≥ 0.92. Escalating to 7B-AWQ does not close a gap of that shape; a bigger model with the same missing capability hallucinates more fluently, not more accurately. I'd spend that GPU time elsewhere.

The deferred path was written for exactly this: *"a Bangla-handwriting-specific fallback for when the single VLM underperforms."* You now have the evidence to promote it.

**But it cascades into your layout decision.** D1 currently survives on the narrow argument that "reading is a single VLM call over the whole page, so layout only governs cropping." A line-level HTR model (GraDeT-HTR is TrOCR-style, so it reads **line crops**, not pages) makes layout load-bearing again — and your own docstring records that the 4 camera-photo pages yield a single page-sized region with no blank rows to split on. Line segmentation on those pages becomes a real piece of work, not a formality.

## What I'd do next, in order

1. **Probe the capability boundary — one cell, ~10 minutes.** Does it read *printed* Bangla? `dolil_20` has printed gold (d425f22). Crop a printed region and read it. If printed works and handwriting doesn't, that's a sharp, citable finding for the report and it tells you whether any VLM survives in the pipeline at all.
2. **Verify GraDeT-HTR is actually obtainable** — weights on HF, license, input format — before committing the lane to it. If it's paper-only or unreleased, you need to know today, not after rebuilding layout.
3. **Then decide**, with Aishi and Adri in the room, because it moves your lane's scope: line-level segmentation in `layout.py` + an HTR reader in `ocr.py`.

One constraint worth re-opening explicitly: your local-inference rule rules out cloud OCR (Google Vision reads Bangla handwriting far better than any local option). You justified it as modeling a production land-registry deployment, while noting this corpus is public CC BY. That's your team's call to make deliberately — but make it, rather than inheriting it.

## For the report, this is a good result

A pre-registered measurement that overturned a design decision is stronger evidence of engineering judgment than a pipeline that quietly scored 0.02 and got written up as working. Commit `ocr_baseline.json` and the grid output — with the predicted text, not just the F1s. The hallucinated "university founded in 1657" line is the single most convincing artifact you have, and it belongs in `design_choices.md` row 3 and the A2 form.

Want me to write up the finding as a decision record in `ocr.py`'s docstring plus the design table row, while you run the printed-text probe?

## Before you push — this needs your call

```
## main...origin/main [ahead 13, behind 12]
```

**You'd already pushed** — those four commits are live on GitHub, which is why this reads as diverged. Publishing the rewrite means a force-push:

```
git push --force-with-lease origin main
```

Two consequences, both real:

1. **Your two collaborators.** Anyone who has pulled `main` has the old 12 commits. After a force-push their next `git pull` merges the old and new history into a duplicated mess. They each need to re-clone, or `git fetch && git reset --hard origin/main` after saving any local work. Tell them *before* you push, not after.
2. **Submission.** No tags exist yet, so nothing is at risk there — but if you were about to tag `a2-submit`, do the force-push first so the tag lands on the final history.

I stopped at the commit, since you didn't ask me to push and this one shouldn't be a surprise to your teammates.

Once you've pushed and everyone has resynced, clean up the backups:

```
git branch -D backup-before-msg-rewrite
git update-ref -d refs/original/refs/heads/main
```

Those are the only remaining copies of the old messages — delete them and the trailer is gone locally for good.

CLAUDE:
Adri is asking about the Stage 3 → Stage 4 handoff: *"So the output text — for embedding there's just one text per page? Instead of a region-based division? And that's what I'll embed?"*

He's right on the first half, wrong on the second.

## Yes — Stage 3 emits one text per page, not per region

[ocr.py:236](src/doc_agent/vision/ocr.py#L236) builds exactly one `Chunk` per page, with the page's regions concatenated in layout's reading order, joined by `\n`:

```python
Chunk(id=f"{page_id}#p", doc_id=..., text="\n".join(pieces), page_ids=[page_id])
```

One caveat for him: a page whose regions all come back empty produces **no chunk at all**, so the output list can be shorter than the page list. He shouldn't index by position.

## No — that's not what he embeds

The pipeline has a step between them ([pipeline.py:20-23](src/doc_agent/pipeline.py#L20-L23)):

```python
text   = ocr.transcribe(regions, cfg)   # Stage 3 → one Chunk per PAGE
chunks = chunk.split(text, cfg)         # Stage 4 → HIS re-chunking, 256 tokens / 32 overlap
vectors = embed.encode(chunks, cfg)     # embeds what split() returned
```

The page-level chunk is the **input to his chunker**, not the embedding unit. He embeds whatever `split()` emits, sized by `index.chunk_tokens: 256, overlap: 32` in [config.yaml:22](configs/config.yaml#L22).

## Why it's page-level — this is the answer to his "instead of region based division?"

It's deliberate, and it exists *for his stage*. From the Stage 3 docstring: his legal-semantic chunker cuts on deed and paragraph boundaries, and **it cannot find a boundary that spans two regions if Stage 3 already cut the text apart**. Region-level chunks would destroy exactly the boundaries he needs. So Stage 3 hands him the page whole and lets him decide where to cut.

## Two things that are actually his decisions

- **Cross-page chunks.** A deed (`doc_id`) spans several pages. If a chunk merges text from two pages, `page_ids` must list both — that field is what the agent's citations resolve against. Getting it wrong breaks citation accuracy, not retrieval, so it fails silently.
- **What `\n` means.** Don't treat a single `\n` as a paragraph boundary — it's just a region join. Cut on blank lines, danda (`।`), or deed markers instead.

## And the part he's probably really worried about

The reader failing at 0.022 does **not** change his interface. If we pivot to line-level HTR, `transcribe()` still returns one `Chunk` per page — line crops just become more regions that get joined the same way. His `split(list[Chunk], cfg)` signature and input shape are unchanged, and his fake `pages.jsonl` stays a valid stand-in.

The one thing that shifts is granularity: with line-level reading, the joined text will be one line per `\n` rather than a few blocks. Which is the second bullet above — so if he writes the chunker to not lean on `\n`, the pivot costs him nothing.

## Two things this changes

**1. Line segmentation isn't unsolved work anymore.** Their pipeline is BN-DRISHTI (YOLOv5 line/word detection) → GraDeT-HTR (recognition per line). That answers the question the email can't: GraDeT-HTR was trained on BN-HTRd, which is modern everyday Bangla handwriting, while your corpus is **historical land deeds** with legal formulae, seals, and stamps. The domain gap is real, and you'd rather discover it in a browser today than three weeks after the weights land.

- Recognition weights: **academic/research only** — you cannot redistribute them in a public Space.

- YOLOv5 (ultralytics) is **AGPL-3.0**.

None of that blocks a course project, but "we deploy this publicly" is no longer straightforwardly true, and it belongs in the design table's Deployment cell rather than being discovered at A4.

Two corrections before the code, both checked against Hugging Face:

**There is no Qwen VL at 9B.** The sizes are 2B/3B/4B/7B/8B/32B/72B. Your teammate almost certainly means **`Qwen3-VL-8B-Instruct`** — newest generation, 4.9M downloads. I've used that. If they specifically wanted the same-family step up from your 3B baseline, swap `NAME` to `Qwen/Qwen2.5-VL-7B-Instruct`; everything else is identical.

## Cell 2 — locate the data, reconcile the labels (no GPU)

```python
import glob, json, importlib.util
from pathlib import Path

ROOT = Path("/kaggle/working/doc-agent-12")
spec = importlib.util.spec_from_file_location("sh", ROOT / "scripts/score_heldout.py")
sh = importlib.util.module_from_spec(spec); spec.loader.exec_module(sh)

DS = Path(glob.glob("/kaggle/input/**/dolil_13.jpg", recursive=True)[0]).parent
print("images:", DS, sorted(p.name for p in DS.glob("*.jpg")))

repo_gold = sh.load_gold(ROOT / "grading_kit/labels.jsonl")
ds_labels = glob.glob("/kaggle/input/**/labels.jsonl", recursive=True)
if ds_labels:
    ds_gold = sh.load_gold(Path(ds_labels[0]))
    for pid in sorted(set(repo_gold) | set(ds_gold)):
        a, b = repo_gold.get(pid), ds_gold.get(pid)
        if a is None or b is None:   print(f"  {pid}: only in {'repo' if b is None else 'dataset'}")
        elif a != b:                 print(f"  {pid}: TEXT DIFFERS  repo={len(a.split())}w dataset={len(b.split())}w")

# Score against the REPO labels, restricted to the images present here — that keeps this
# run comparable to reports/ocr_baseline.json, which was scored against exactly these.
gold = {p: t for p, t in repo_gold.items() if (DS / f"{p}.jpg").is_file()}
print("scoring", len(gold), "pages:", sorted(gold))
```

If any page prints `TEXT DIFFERS`, stop and reconcile with your teammate before trusting the score. Gold has to have one home, and that home is the repo.

## Cell 3 — load the 8B

```python
import torch
from transformers import AutoModelForImageTextToText, AutoProcessor

NAME = "Qwen/Qwen3-VL-8B-Instruct"
proc = AutoProcessor.from_pretrained(NAME, min_pixels=256 * 28 * 28, max_pixels=2048 * 28 * 28)
# float16, NOT bfloat16: the T4 is compute 7.5 and has no bf16 support.
# 8B fp16 ≈ 16.5GB, so this needs BOTH T4s — set Accelerator to "GPU T4 x2".
try:
    model = AutoModelForImageTextToText.from_pretrained(NAME, dtype=torch.float16, device_map="auto")
except TypeError:      # transformers < 5 spells it torch_dtype
    model = AutoModelForImageTextToText.from_pretrained(NAME, torch_dtype=torch.float16, device_map="auto")
print(model.hf_device_map)   # every entry should be 0 or 1 — never 'cpu' or 'disk'
```

## Cell 4 — read and score

```python
import time
from doc_agent import config
from doc_agent.llm.prompts import TRANSCRIBE
from doc_agent.vision import ocr

cfg = config.load(ROOT / "configs/config.yaml")
cfg["ocr"] = {**cfg["ocr"], "model": NAME, "sidecar": "", "pages_dir": str(DS),
              "deed_groups": str(ROOT / "data/deed_groups.csv")}

class Qwen3Reader:
    """Same crop and prompt as production; own generate() so the knobs are explicit.

    score_pages only needs .transcribe_region and .generation_stats, so this drops in
    wherever the repo Reader would go.
    """
    def __init__(self, cfg, max_new_tokens=768, repetition_penalty=1.0):
        self._inner = ocr.Reader(cfg)          # reuses the EXIF-correct cropping
        self.pages = self._inner.pages
        self.generation_stats = []
        self.max_new_tokens, self.repetition_penalty = max_new_tokens, repetition_penalty

    def transcribe_region(self, region):
        image = self._inner.crop(region)
        messages = [{"role": "user",
                     "content": [{"type": "image", "image": image},
                                 {"type": "text", "text": TRANSCRIBE}]}]
        prompt = proc.apply_chat_template(messages, tokenize=False, add_generation_prompt=True)
        inputs = proc(text=[prompt], images=[image], return_tensors="pt").to(model.device)
        with torch.inference_mode():
            out = model.generate(**inputs, max_new_tokens=self.max_new_tokens, do_sample=False,
                                 repetition_penalty=self.repetition_penalty)
        trimmed = out[0][inputs["input_ids"].shape[1]:]
        n = int(trimmed.shape[0])
        self.generation_stats.append({"page_id": region.page_id, "generated_tokens": n,
                                      "truncated": n >= self.max_new_tokens})
        text = proc.decode(trimmed, skip_special_tokens=True)
        del inputs, out
        torch.cuda.empty_cache()
        return text

# Smoke-test ONE single-region page first — don't spend an hour to rediscover the same failure.
t0 = time.time()
smoke = sh.score_pages({"dolil_208": gold["dolil_208"]}, cfg, reader=Qwen3Reader(cfg))
print(f"{time.time() - t0:.0f}s  f1={smoke[0]['f1']:.3f}  cut={smoke[0]['truncated']}")
print(repr(smoke[0]["pred"][:400]))
```

Then, only if the smoke output looks like actual transcription, the full run:

```python
reader = Qwen3Reader(cfg)
rows = sh.score_pages(gold, cfg, reader=reader)
summary = sh.summarise(rows)
sh._print_table(rows, summary)

Path("/kaggle/working/ocr_qwen3vl_8b.json").write_text(json.dumps(
    {"model": NAME, "dtype": "float16", "max_pixels": 2048 * 28 * 28,
     "max_new_tokens": 768, "summary": summary, "pages": rows},
    ensure_ascii=False, indent=2) + "\n", encoding="utf-8")
```

## Practical notes

- **`GPU T4 x2` is required**, not optional — 8B fp16 doesn't fit one 15 GB card. If `hf_device_map` shows `cpu`, stop and fix it; it'll be unusably slow.
- **~21 regions** across those 6 pages, at maybe 1.5–3 min each on 2×T4 = **30–60 minutes**. Hence the smoke test.
- I kept `max_new_tokens=768` deliberately so this is directly comparable to `ocr_baseline.json`. `max_pixels` is raised to ~1.6M (from 1.0M) since you have the extra VRAM.
- If Cell 4 errors about image tokens or placeholders, the fix is in the `messages` dict — Qwen3-VL's chat template is stricter than 2.5's. Send me the traceback.

On expectations, once and then I'll drop it: the 3B failure was categorical — invented prose, not near-misses — so I'd predict the same from 8B. But it's a cheap, falsifiable test, and saving it as `ocr_qwen3vl_8b.json` next to the 3B baseline turns it into a second data point for your design table either way. A documented "we escalated and it also failed" is worth more in the report than an untested assumption.

CLAUDE:
`transformers` 5.13.0+ already ships `qwen3_5`, so the pip release is enough — no git install needed (the README's advice is about the serving extra).

Two things in that model card would have silently wrecked the run:

**1. Qwen3.5 runs in thinking mode by default**, emitting `<think>…</think>` before any answer. With `max_new_tokens=768` it would spend the entire budget reasoning and return no transcription — and you'd have read that as another failure. Must pass `enable_thinking=False`.

**2. The vision tokenizer is different.** `patch_size: 16, merge_size: 2` → **32×32 px per visual token**, not 28×28. So all my `N * 28 * 28` arithmetic is wrong for this model, and it takes `size={"longest_edge", "shortest_edge"}` in pixels rather than `min_pixels`/`max_pixels`. Default `longest_edge` is 16.7M pixels (~16k tokens) — that alone would OOM a T4.

Cell 2 is unchanged. Replace 1, 3 and 4:

## Cell 3 — load Qwen3.5-9B

```python
import torch
from transformers import AutoModelForImageTextToText, AutoProcessor

NAME = "Qwen/Qwen3.5-9B"
# 32*32 px per visual token here (patch 16 x merge 2). ~1600 tokens = ~1.64M px.
# The default longest_edge is 16.7M px (~16k tokens) and will OOM a T4 instantly.
proc = AutoProcessor.from_pretrained(
    NAME, size={"shortest_edge": 65536, "longest_edge": 1600 * 32 * 32})
# float16, NOT the config's bfloat16: T4 is compute 7.5 with no bf16 support.
model = AutoModelForImageTextToText.from_pretrained(NAME, dtype=torch.float16, device_map="auto")
print(model.hf_device_map)   # all entries must be 0 or 1 — never 'cpu' or 'disk'
```

~18 GB of weights to download, so give it 10 minutes. 9B in fp16 is ~18.5 GB, which needs **both** T4s (30 GB total).

## Cell 4 — read and score

```python
import re, time
from doc_agent import config
from doc_agent.llm.prompts import TRANSCRIBE
from doc_agent.vision import ocr

cfg = config.load(ROOT / "configs/config.yaml")
cfg["ocr"] = {**cfg["ocr"], "model": NAME, "sidecar": "", "pages_dir": str(DS),
              "deed_groups": str(ROOT / "data/deed_groups.csv")}

class Qwen35Reader:
    """Production crop + prompt; explicit knobs. score_pages only needs the two members."""
    def __init__(self, cfg, max_new_tokens=768, repetition_penalty=1.0):
        self._inner = ocr.Reader(cfg)          # reuses the EXIF-correct cropping
        self.pages = self._inner.pages
        self.generation_stats = []
        self.max_new_tokens, self.repetition_penalty = max_new_tokens, repetition_penalty

    def transcribe_region(self, region):
        image = self._inner.crop(region)
        messages = [{"role": "user",
                     "content": [{"type": "image", "image": image},
                                 {"type": "text", "text": TRANSCRIBE}]}]
        prompt = proc.apply_chat_template(
            messages, tokenize=False, add_generation_prompt=True,
            enable_thinking=False)             # ESSENTIAL — else it reasons instead of reading
        inputs = proc(text=[prompt], images=[image], return_tensors="pt").to(model.device)
        with torch.inference_mode():
            out = model.generate(**inputs, max_new_tokens=self.max_new_tokens, do_sample=False,
                                 repetition_penalty=self.repetition_penalty)
        trimmed = out[0][inputs["input_ids"].shape[1]:]
        n = int(trimmed.shape[0])
        self.generation_stats.append({"page_id": region.page_id, "generated_tokens": n,
                                      "truncated": n >= self.max_new_tokens})
        text = proc.decode(trimmed, skip_special_tokens=True)
        text = re.sub(r"<think>.*?</think>", "", text, flags=re.S).strip()   # belt and braces
        del inputs, out
        torch.cuda.empty_cache()
        return text

t0 = time.time()
smoke = sh.score_pages({"dolil_208": gold["dolil_208"]}, cfg, reader=Qwen35Reader(cfg))
print(f"{time.time() - t0:.0f}s  f1={smoke[0]['f1']:.3f}  cut={smoke[0]['truncated']}")
print(repr(smoke[0]["pred"][:400]))
```

Then the full run if the smoke output is real transcription:

```python
rows = sh.score_pages(gold, cfg, reader=Qwen35Reader(cfg))
summary = sh.summarise(rows)
sh._print_table(rows, summary)

Path("/kaggle/working/ocr_qwen35_9b.json").write_text(json.dumps(
    {"model": NAME, "dtype": "float16", "longest_edge": 1600 * 32 * 32,
     "max_new_tokens": 768, "enable_thinking": False, "summary": summary, "pages": rows},
    ensure_ascii=False, indent=2) + "\n", encoding="utf-8")
```

## Notes

- **`GPU T4 x2` required.** If `hf_device_map` shows `cpu`, stop — it'll be unusably slow.
- **fp16 caveat:** the model was trained in bf16 and the T4 can't do bf16. If output comes back empty or NaN-ish rather than merely wrong, that's fp16 overflow, not a reading failure — it'd need an L4/A100 or a 4-bit load.
- ~21 regions, 9B on 2×T4 → roughly **45–90 minutes**. Hence the smoke test.
- If `from_pretrained` raises on the architecture, fall back to `pip install "transformers @ git+https://github.com/huggingface/transformers.git@main"`.

And I'll revise my prediction, since I was wrong about the model: my "escalation won't help" argument was about 3B→7B *within the 2024-era Qwen2.5-VL family*. Qwen3.5 is a natively multimodal 2026 model claiming 201 languages and carrying a CC-OCR benchmark score. Bangla handwriting has a genuine chance here, and my prior doesn't transfer. Apache-2.0 also makes it far cleaner for your A4 deployment than GraDeT-HTR's request-only academic weights — so if this works, it simplifies more than just Stage 3.

CLAUDE:
Fixed locally. Here's what happened and what it means.

## Root cause

Commit `c637378 "Added 10 pages"` appended records **pretty-printed** (multi-line, indented) to a file whose format is one JSON object per line. Lines 1–8 were valid; everything after line 9 was a fragment like `{` or ` "page_id": "dolil_100",`. That's 285 of 300 lines unparseable — `json.loads("{")` gives exactly the `line 1 column 2` error you saw.

Whoever adds labels should use `scripts/transcribe_heldout.py` — its `save_records` writes correct JSONL. Hand-editing with `json.dump(..., indent=…)` breaks the format silently.

## Repaired

18 records recovered by parsing the concatenated objects, rewritten one-per-line, **round-trip verified** (every record identical as a Python object — no data loss). Backup of the broken file is in my scratchpad. Only `grading_kit/labels.jsonl` is modified.

## The good news buried in this

Your gold set went from **7 pages to 17**: `dolil_38, 49, 60, 71, 82, 93, 98, 99, 100, 101` were added, all `status=done`, 133–180 words each. Only `dolil_11` is still TODO. That's a much stronger evaluation set than the 7 the 0.022 baseline was measured on — future scores will be far less noisy, and the 0.108 fluke I warned about is less likely to happen with 17 pages.

## One thing to fix before it bites you

Those 10 new gold pages have **no image in `grading_kit/heldout_pages/`** — it still holds only the original 8. The images exist in `data/raw/`, but that's gitignored, so a fresh clone (like your Kaggle one) can't see them.

Kaggle is unaffected: Cell 2 already filters gold to images actually present, so it'll score the 6 pages in your teammate's dataset. But `heldout_pages/` is meant to be the set-aside slice, and gold pages living only in gitignored `data/raw/` will break the next person who clones. Copying those 10 JPEGs in is the fix.

Worth asking the team too: are these 10 pages excluded from the index build? Held-out pages that also get chunked and embedded leak the oracle into retrieval.

CLAUDE:
The clone on Kaggle still has the broken file — my repair was local only, and you haven't pushed it. Also worth knowing: re-running Cell 1 wouldn't have helped, because `git clone` into an existing `/kaggle/working/doc-agent-12` fails and leaves the stale copy.

Rather than depend on the push, make the notebook tolerant of both formats. **Replace Cell 2 with this** — it parses JSONL *and* concatenated/pretty-printed objects, so it works against either version of the file:

```python
import glob, json, importlib.util
from pathlib import Path

ROOT = Path("/kaggle/working/doc-agent-12")
spec = importlib.util.spec_from_file_location("sh", ROOT / "scripts/score_heldout.py")
sh = importlib.util.module_from_spec(spec); spec.loader.exec_module(sh)

def load_gold_any(path):
    """Gold labels from JSONL *or* concatenated/pretty-printed JSON objects.

    labels.jsonl currently mixes both: records 1-8 are one-per-line, the 10 added later
    are pretty-printed across many lines. raw_decode walks objects, ignoring newlines.
    """
    raw = Path(path).read_text(encoding="utf-8")
    dec, out, i = json.JSONDecoder(), {}, 0
    while i < len(raw):
        while i < len(raw) and raw[i].isspace():
            i += 1
        if i >= len(raw):
            break
        obj, i = dec.raw_decode(raw, i)
        if obj.get("status") == "done" and (obj.get("text") or "").strip():
            out[obj["page_id"]] = obj["text"].strip()
    return out

DS = Path(glob.glob("/kaggle/input/**/dolil_13.jpg", recursive=True)[0]).parent
print("images:", DS, sorted(p.name for p in DS.glob("*.jpg")))

repo_gold = load_gold_any(ROOT / "grading_kit/labels.jsonl")
print("gold in repo:", len(repo_gold), "pages")

ds_labels = glob.glob("/kaggle/input/**/labels.jsonl", recursive=True)
if ds_labels:
    ds_gold = load_gold_any(ds_labels[0])
    for pid in sorted(set(repo_gold) | set(ds_gold)):
        a, b = repo_gold.get(pid), ds_gold.get(pid)
        if a is None or b is None:   print(f"  {pid}: only in {'repo' if b is None else 'dataset'}")
        elif a != b:                 print(f"  {pid}: TEXT DIFFERS  repo={len(a.split())}w dataset={len(b.split())}w")

gold = {p: t for p, t in repo_gold.items() if (DS / f"{p}.jpg").is_file()}
print("scoring", len(gold), "pages:", sorted(gold))
```

Expect `gold in repo: 17` and `scoring 6 pages`. Cells 3 and 4 are unchanged.

Same failure, one generation and 3× the parameters later. Translated, it's producing: *"It is seen that, I, the value of this deed is 1000 taka and the value of this deed is 1000 taka and…"* — looping from the first sentence.

```python

# dolil_20's gold contains PRINTED ENGLISH: the stamp and registrar block.
for probe in ["rupee", "annas", "INDIA", "Sub-Registrar", "Raipur", "Noakhal", "15.1.19"]:
    print(f"  {probe:<15} {'FOUND' if probe.lower() in rows20[0]['pred'].lower() else '-'}")
```

`dolil_20` is the one page whose gold has printed English (`One rupee eight annas / INDIA / Sub-Registrar Raipur, Dist. Noakhal…`). If the model reads those printed strings but hallucinates the Bangla handwriting around them, you have a sharp, citable capability boundary: *the VLM can read print on these scans, it cannot read the handwriting.* That's a far stronger statement in your report than "it scored 0.02", and it tells you whether a VLM has any role left in the pipeline — for stamps, dates, and registry numbers, say.

If it can't read the printed English either, the answer is that these scans defeat this model family entirely, and Qwen is done as a candidate.

Either way, that's where I'd stop with Qwen and put the quota into GraDeT-HTR — which means the access email is now the thing on your critical path.

Want me to record this 9B run in `ocr.py`'s D2 MEASURED block and the design table as the second data point? Two failures across two generations is a much harder argument to wave away than one, and it's the evidence that justifies the pivot at A2.

CLAUDE:
That's the most informative run you've done. The boundary is now sharp:

**Printed text: read correctly.** `ONE RUPEE EIGHT ANNAS`, `I R. 8 As.`, `Sub-Registrar Raipur, Dist. Noakhali`, plus the registration/Act stamp block. Five of seven probes hit, and the misses (`INDIA`, `15.1.19`) are on the embossed circular stamp — the hardest surface on the page.

**Bangla handwriting: still not read.** `সহ মাদম-বাস্তব লালু বসক পাঠারি` is Bangla-shaped, structured like a deed (names, districts, জাতি/জেলা fields), and wrong. It's imitating the *shape* of the content, not transcribing it. Then it loops on `10/10/2002 তারিখে।` to the cap.

So f1=0.093 — your highest — and now you know exactly what earned it: real printed-English matches, not the numeric-collision noise that produced the 0.108 fluke earlier. This score is signal.

## This changes the finding, and improves it

The claim is no longer "Qwen fails on this corpus." It's:

> **The VLM reads printed text on these scans accurately and cannot read the Bangla handwriting at all.**

That's more precise, more defensible, and more useful — because printed elements on a dolil are exactly the *verifiable legal fields* your precision-first NFR targets: registrar block, section/Act references, stamp denominations, registry numbers, and printed dates. A VLM isn't dead in your pipeline; it's the wrong tool for one specific job.

Which points at a **hybrid reader**: VLM for printed regions, Bangla HTR for handwritten ones. And that re-opens D1 a third time, in a new direction — layout would need to distinguish printed from handwritten regions, where today it emits everything as `kind="text"` with `heading/table/figure` deliberately unused. The `Region.kind` field you left unused suddenly has a job.

I'd hold that as a hypothesis, not a plan, until GraDeT-HTR is in hand — but it's the shape the evidence is pointing to, and it's worth putting in the A2 report as the direction rather than pretending the pivot was obvious.

## One small thing to check

The model wrote **`Noakhali`** where your gold says **`Noakhal`**. Worth looking at the scan — if the page says Noakhali, that's a typo in the oracle, and every future score on `dolil_20` inherits it. Gold errors are the one bug class that makes everything downstream quietly wrong.

## Where I'd stop

Qwen is answered on both questions now — it can't read the handwriting, it can read the print. Further Qwen runs buy nothing. **The access email for GraDeT-HTR is the only thing on your critical path**, and it's gated on a human replying, so every day you wait is a day added to the end.

Want me to write this up now — the D2 MEASURED block gets the 9B result and the printed/handwritten boundary, and design row 3 gets the hybrid direction? It's a materially stronger record than what's in there: two model generations, a precise capability boundary, and a evidence-backed direction rather than just a rejection. I'd also draft the GraDeT-HTR access email at the same time if you want it.

## What I did *not* do, and why it matters

**I built the index from the 17 human-typed gold transcriptions, not from OCR of 50–100 images.** Deliberately:

The reader hallucinates. On `dolil_208` the 9B produced *"the value of this deed is 1000 taka and the value of this deed is 1000 taka…"* — invented money language, on a loop, on every page. Adri's query is **specifically about money**. An index built on OCR output would have matched the model's own hallucinated boilerplate and returned a confident page_id that means nothing. It would look like it worked. That's worse than failing.

Gold text isolates his Stage 4/5 from the broken Stage 3 — so this result actually validates *his* code, which is what he wanted.

Two other reasons the 50–100 image run wasn't possible today: I have no GPU here (`torch.cuda.is_available()` → False), and `data/raw/` is gitignored, so the corpus isn't on Kaggle either — someone would have to upload 2.33 GB as a dataset first.

Also, to answer his size question: the **3B** is ~7 GB (that's the one he's thinking of), **Qwen3.5-9B is ~18 GB**. And `e5-small` is 470 MB.

## One bug to fix

`configs/config.yaml` sets `embed.cache_dir: ".hf_cache"`, and **`.hf_cache` is not in `.gitignore`**. Whoever runs the index pipeline locally drops ~470 MB inside the repo, ready to be committed. I redirected mine to scratchpad so your tree is clean. One line in `.gitignore` fixes it.

## Cell 2 — corpus, config, page selection

```python
import glob, json, re, sys
from collections import Counter
from pathlib import Path

ROOT = Path("/kaggle/working/doc-agent-12")
sys.path.insert(0, str(ROOT / "src"))
from doc_agent import config
from doc_agent.contracts import Chunk, Page

N_PAGES = 50                       # Adri asked for 50-100. Start at 20 to validate the chain.
NAME    = "Qwen/Qwen2.5-VL-3B-Instruct"   # 3B: ~7GB, ~2-3x faster than the 9B for a bulk run
OUT     = Path("/kaggle/working"); (OUT / "hf").mkdir(exist_ok=True)

hits = Counter(Path(p).parent for p in glob.glob("/kaggle/input/**/dolil_*.jpg", recursive=True))
assert hits, "no dolil_*.jpg under /kaggle/input — attach the corpus dataset first"
RAW = hits.most_common(1)[0][0]

def page_num(p):                   # dolil_2 before dolil_10
    m = re.search(r"(\d+)", p.stem); return int(m.group(1)) if m else 0

images = sorted(RAW.glob("dolil_*.jpg"), key=page_num)[:N_PAGES]
print(f"corpus: {RAW}  ({len(list(RAW.glob('dolil_*.jpg')))} images)  -> processing {len(images)}")

cfg = config.load(ROOT / "configs/config.yaml")
cfg["ocr"]   = {**cfg["ocr"], "model": NAME, "sidecar": "", "pages_dir": str(RAW),
                "deed_groups": str(ROOT / "data/deed_groups.csv")}
cfg["embed"] = {**cfg["embed"], "cache_dir": str(OUT / "hf")}
cfg["index"] = {**cfg["index"], "path": str(OUT / "index")}
```

## Cell 3 — load the reader

```python
import torch
from transformers import AutoModelForImageTextToText, AutoProcessor

IS_Q35 = NAME.startswith("Qwen/Qwen3.5")
proc = AutoProcessor.from_pretrained(NAME, **(
    {"size": {"shortest_edge": 65536, "longest_edge": 1600 * 32 * 32}} if IS_Q35
    else {"min_pixels": 256 * 28 * 28, "max_pixels": 1280 * 28 * 28}))
model = AutoModelForImageTextToText.from_pretrained(NAME, dtype=torch.float16, device_map="auto")
print(model.hf_device_map)
```

## Cell 4 — OCR, incremental and resumable

```python
import time
from doc_agent.llm.prompts import TRANSCRIBE
from doc_agent.vision import layout, ocr

PAGES_JSONL = OUT / "pages.jsonl"      # one page-level Chunk per line, written as we go

class QwenReader:
    def __init__(self, cfg, max_new_tokens=768):
        self._inner = ocr.Reader(cfg); self.pages = self._inner.pages
        self.generation_stats = []; self.max_new_tokens = max_new_tokens

    def transcribe_region(self, region):
        image = self._inner.crop(region)
        messages = [{"role": "user", "content": [{"type": "image", "image": image},
                                                 {"type": "text", "text": TRANSCRIBE}]}]
        prompt = proc.apply_chat_template(messages, tokenize=False, add_generation_prompt=True,
                                          **({"enable_thinking": False} if IS_Q35 else {}))
        inputs = proc(text=[prompt], images=[image], return_tensors="pt").to(model.device)
        with torch.inference_mode():
            out = model.generate(**inputs, max_new_tokens=self.max_new_tokens, do_sample=False)
        trimmed = out[0][inputs["input_ids"].shape[1]:]
        n = int(trimmed.shape[0])
        self.generation_stats.append({"page_id": region.page_id, "generated_tokens": n,
                                      "truncated": n >= self.max_new_tokens})
        text = proc.decode(trimmed, skip_special_tokens=True)
        del inputs, out; torch.cuda.empty_cache()
        return re.sub(r"<think>.*?</think>", "", text, flags=re.S).strip()

# Resume: skip pages already written, so a session timeout costs only the current page.
done = {json.loads(l)["page_ids"][0] for l in PAGES_JSONL.read_text().splitlines()
        if l.strip()} if PAGES_JSONL.is_file() else set()
print(f"already done: {len(done)}")

reader = QwenReader(cfg)
t0 = time.time()
with PAGES_JSONL.open("a", encoding="utf-8") as fh:
    for i, img in enumerate(images, 1):
        pid = img.stem
        if pid in done:
            continue
        page = Page(id=pid, image_path=str(img), doc_id=reader.pages[pid]["doc_id"])
        regions = layout.detect([page], cfg)
        chunks = ocr.transcribe(regions, cfg, reader=reader)
        for c in chunks:
            fh.write(json.dumps(c.model_dump(), ensure_ascii=False) + "\n")
        fh.flush()                                   # survive a kernel kill
        cut = sum(s["truncated"] for s in reader.generation_stats if s["page_id"] == pid)
        print(f"[{i}/{len(images)}] {pid}: {len(regions)} regions, "
              f"{len(chunks[0].text.split()) if chunks else 0} words, {cut} truncated, "
              f"{time.time() - t0:.0f}s elapsed", flush=True)
print(f"OCR done in {(time.time() - t0) / 60:.1f} min")
```

## Cell 5 — chunk → embed → store

```python
from doc_agent.index import chunk as chunking, embed, store

pages = [Chunk(**json.loads(l)) for l in PAGES_JSONL.read_text(encoding="utf-8").splitlines() if l.strip()]
print(f"{len(pages)} page-level chunks from OCR")

pieces = chunking.split(pages, cfg);            print(f"chunk.split  -> {len(pieces)} chunks")
vectors = embed.encode(pieces, cfg);            print(f"embed.encode -> {vectors.shape}")
store.build(pieces, vectors, cfg)
loaded = store.load(cfg);                       print(f"store        -> {loaded['index'].ntotal} vectors")
```

## Cell 6 — the query Adri wants

```python
QUERY = "কোন দলিলে জমির মূল্য বা টাকা উল্লেখ আছে?"

qv = embed.encode_query(QUERY, cfg)
scores, ids = loaded["index"].search(qv, 5)
print(f"QUERY: {QUERY}\n")
for rank, (i, s) in enumerate(zip(ids[0], scores[0]), 1):
    rec = loaded["metadata"][int(i)]
    print(f"  {rank}. page_id={rec.get('page_id'):<12} score={float(s):.4f}")
    print(f"     {loaded['chunks'][int(i)].text[:150]}")

top = loaded["metadata"][int(ids[0][0])].get("page_id")
print(f"\nTOP page_id: {top}")
print("NOTE: retrieved over OCR text from a reader measured at ocr_f1 0.022 (hallucinates "
      "'this deed's value is 1000 taka' on most pages). Treat as a plumbing test of "
      "Stage 2-5, NOT as an answer about the deeds.")
```

## Practical notes

- **Time:** ~3 regions/page × 50 pages ≈ 150 VLM calls. At ~25–50 s each on the 3B that's **1–2 hours**; the 9B would be 3–6 h and risks the 9 h session cap. Start with `N_PAGES = 20` to prove the chain, then raise it — Cell 4 resumes, so nothing is redone.
- **Resumability** is built in: `pages.jsonl` is appended and flushed per page. If the kernel dies, re-run Cells 1–4 and it picks up where it stopped.
- **Download `pages.jsonl`** from the output pane when it finishes — that's the artifact worth keeping, and it lets Adri re-run Cells 5–6 on CPU without any GPU.
- Cells 5–6 need no GPU at all, so Adri can run those himself from `pages.jsonl` once you send it to him.

## Kaggle fix — replace Cell 2

Filter to grouped pages, then take N from what's left, and stage them so `load_page_index` only sees those:

```python
import csv, glob, json, re, sys
from collections import Counter
from pathlib import Path

ROOT = Path("/kaggle/working/doc-agent-12")
sys.path.insert(0, str(ROOT / "src"))
from doc_agent import config
from doc_agent.contracts import Chunk, Page

N_PAGES = 50
NAME    = "Qwen/Qwen2.5-VL-3B-Instruct"
OUT     = Path("/kaggle/working"); (OUT / "hf").mkdir(exist_ok=True)

hits = Counter(Path(p).parent for p in glob.glob("/kaggle/input/**/dolil_*.jpg", recursive=True))
assert hits, "no dolil_*.jpg under /kaggle/input — attach the corpus dataset first"
RAW = hits.most_common(1)[0][0]

# deed_groups.csv defines the corpus: 546 DISTINCT pages, after 105 duplicate re-scans and
# 2 non-content pages were removed (data/provenance.md). A page with no row is excluded by
# policy, not an error — and indexing duplicates would double-count them anyway.
groups = {r["page_id"]: r["doc_id"]
          for r in csv.DictReader(open(ROOT / "data/deed_groups.csv", encoding="utf-8"))}

def page_num(p):
    m = re.search(r"(\d+)", p.stem); return int(m.group(1)) if m else 0

all_imgs = sorted(RAW.glob("dolil_*.jpg"), key=page_num)
kept     = [p for p in all_imgs if p.stem in groups]
skipped  = [p.stem for p in all_imgs if p.stem not in groups]
images   = kept[:N_PAGES]
print(f"corpus {RAW}: {len(all_imgs)} images, {len(kept)} in grouping, {len(skipped)} excluded")
print(f"  excluded e.g. {skipped[:6]}\nprocessing {len(images)}: {images[0].stem} .. {images[-1].stem}")

# Stage only the pages we're processing — load_page_index scans the whole dir it's given.
STAGE = OUT / "pages"; STAGE.mkdir(exist_ok=True)
for p in images:
    link = STAGE / p.name
    link.unlink(missing_ok=True)
    link.symlink_to(p)

cfg = config.load(ROOT / "configs/config.yaml")
cfg["ocr"]   = {**cfg["ocr"], "model": NAME, "sidecar": "", "pages_dir": str(STAGE),
                "deed_groups": str(ROOT / "data/deed_groups.csv")}
cfg["embed"] = {**cfg["embed"], "cache_dir": str(OUT / "hf")}
cfg["index"] = {**cfg["index"], "path": str(OUT / "index")}
```

Cells 3–6 are unchanged. This also makes the run *more* correct than what I gave you before: you index 50 distinct pages instead of 50 that may include duplicate re-scans of each other.

## The decision your team needs to make

**Is `dolil_66` a duplicate re-scan?** It's one of your 17 human-typed gold pages, and the dedup dropped it from the corpus. Only two consistent outcomes:

- **It is a duplicate** → it shouldn't be a held-out gold page at all. Retire it from `heldout_pages/` and `labels.jsonl`, or re-point that gold text at the canonical page it duplicates. Note your 3B baseline scored it, so `ocr_baseline.json` covers a page that's no longer in the corpus.
- **It isn't** → it needs its row back in `deed_groups.csv`.

Either way someone has to look. Ask Aishi — it's her dedup, and `deed_groups.csv` has a `duplicate_of` column that should say which canonical page it maps to.

## The code fix I'd make

`load_page_index` should **skip pages absent from the grouping with a warning**, not `KeyError`. Skipping isn't the same as inventing a `doc_id` — the guard that docstring is defending is about never fabricating one, and an excluded page has a correct answer: leave it out. That single change unbreaks `score_heldout.py`, kills the Kaggle crash at the source, and means the next dedup doesn't take down the reader.

Want me to make it (TDD, like the rest) and add the `.hf_cache` gitignore line? Both are small and both are in your lane.

## What changed

**1. `load_page_index` skips ungrouped pages** — [ocr.py:136](src/doc_agent/vision/ocr.py#L136). TDD'd: 2 new tests in `tests/test_ocr.py`, watched fail with the exact `KeyError: 'dolil_2'` you hit. It now logs which pages were excluded and why:

```
WARN: 1 page(s) in grading_kit/heldout_pages are absent from the deed grouping and were
excluded, e.g. ['dolil_66']. If one of these is a page you meant to read, it needs a row
in data/deed_groups.csv.
```

The comment records why skipping doesn't violate the module's no-invented-`doc_id` rule: an excluded page has a correct answer, which is to leave it out.

**2. `.hf_cache/` added to `.gitignore`** — verified it no longer shows in `git status`.

**3. `score_heldout.py` main() now filters unscorable gold pages** with a printed notice. Without this, the harness still died: 11 of your 17 gold pages have no image in `heldout_pages/`. `score_pages` itself stays strict — the filtering is CLI-level, so a library caller still gets a hard error rather than a silent drop. This bit is CLI glue verified by running it, not TDD'd.

**Gates:** 71 passed, 9 skipped; `black` clean; no new `ruff` errors (the 7 `F405` are the template's mandated star-import).

The grading kit is now internally consistent — **18 images, 1:1 with `labels.jsonl`, no orphans either direction**. I followed your `dolil_66` → `dolil_65` rename: copied `dolil_65.jpg` in, dropped the orphaned `dolil_66.jpg`.
